# Dulja Phase 6C — Staff: minimal `employee`, `pos_cashier`, `branch_pos_policy`, `attendance_event`, branch isolation, authority re-verification

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Close the two defects the legacy system never closed — a till credential that is a login (LEGACY-025, 026, 028) and a scope that is a report parameter rather than an access control (LEGACY-045) — by shipping five things. (1) The **minimal `app.employee`** of §4.6.2, byte-identical to the DDL migration `0064_create_employee_minimal.sql` freezes, because cashiers are employees (spec:1030, 2097) and whichever of 6C and 8A executes first commits that one file. (2) **`app.pos_cashier`**: an Argon2id PIN hash with a per-cashier salt, a branch, and a `snapshot` **projection** of the permission codes and limits the till evaluates offline — regenerated by trigger, never edited by hand, and compared nightly against `app.principal_has` by the `pos.cashier.compare` job (spec:1324, D14). (3) **`app.branch_pos_policy`**, the relational limit row per branch (§4.6.4 — *never a blob*), down-synced only to that branch's terminals and re-verified **at sync apply against the row, never against the snapshot**. (4) **`app.attendance_event`** (`clock_in | clock_out | manual_correction`), from the till's clock feed and from back-office corrections under `hr.attendance.record` — readable by HR, never an automatic pay input (spec:2089). (5) **`branch_isolation`** — `app.actor_in_branch`, `app.actor_in_warehouse` and the five RESTRICTIVE policy forms of §4.5.6, all attached by migration `0068` in one file, plus the `branch_isolation_policy` linter rule that makes a missing policy a build failure. The slice's owned acceptance test is **37**: an over-limit discount, a void or a blind return is refused at apply unless a *second* principal's `pos.ticket.override` event authorises it, and the refused ticket lands in `needs_attention`, never in the ledger.

**Architecture:** Four seams, and none of them is a new authorisation mechanism. (1) **The snapshot is a projection with exactly one writer.** `app.refresh_pos_cashier(p_principal_id)` recomputes `pos_cashier.snapshot` from `app.principal_has` and `app.branch_pos_policy` and is called from triggers on `role_assignment`, `role_assignment_branch`, `role_permission` and `branch_pos_policy`; no handler ever writes the column, and `pos.cashier.compare` recomputes every row nightly and reports drift rather than repairing it silently — a silent repair would hide the trigger bug it exists to find. **Which codes go into the snapshot is derived, never listed**: every `app.permission` row whose `module = 'pos'` and whose code is not `pos.sync.*`, mirrored in TypeScript as a filter over `PERMISSIONS`, so 6B's and 6D's codes join without one line of 6C changing. (2) **Sync apply asks the database, not the payload.** `packages/db/src/sync/limits.ts` is a pure-ish checker that takes the ticket, the cashier's principal id and the `branch_pos_policy` row read **inside the apply transaction** and returns a list of refusals; 6A's `applyBatch` calls it before `postDocument`, and every authority question underneath it is `app.principal_has(cashier, code, company, branch)` — the parameterised twin of `app.actor_has` 2B already shipped. **The snapshot is never consulted server-side.** (3) **Branch isolation is row-level and restrictive.** Phases 1–5 have relied on `actor_has(code, company, branch)` in handlers and triggers (F-18); 0068 adds the RLS half, so "a `branch_manager` at A cannot reach B's rows" stops depending on a handler remembering a filter. The stock half (Form C) exists because `warehouse.branch_id` is the only branch key in the inventory model, and the in-transit exception lives on `app.warehouse`'s own policy (Form A″) because `app.actor_in_warehouse` reads that table and an exception inside the function would test a row RLS had already filtered away. (4) **PIN hashing is WebAssembly, not a native module.** The same function must run in `apps/api` on Bun, under Vitest on Node 24, and in Electron's main process at the till (6B) — ADR 0060 chooses `hash-wasm`'s Argon2id for exactly that reason, so no `electron-builder` ABI rebuild stands between a cashier and their PIN. **This slice adds four route modules and therefore exactly four `PROBES` keys.**

**Tech Stack:** Bun 1.4 (install, scripts, the API entrypoint) · Node 24 LTS (runs Vitest) · TypeScript strict · PostgreSQL 18 (Testcontainers `postgres:18`) · `postgres` (postgres.js) tagged templates for every write and every read · Drizzle ORM mirrors for typed reads and as the migration diff source (ADR-0002) · Hono + `@hono/zod-openapi` · Zod · `hash-wasm` (Argon2id, pure WebAssembly — ADR 0060) · `@dulja/domain`'s `Money` for every limit comparison · pg-boss through `JobClient` / `registerHandler` · React 19 + `@dulja/ui` + i18next (the OD-24 screens) · Vitest + Testcontainers · Playwright (the both-directions re-run of test 23) · oxlint + oxfmt · Turborepo. No package version appears in any source file — the lockfile owns versions (spec:104).

**Spec:** `docs/spec/rebuild-spec.md` v1.2 — §0.2 the never/always lists (spec:97–108), §3.0.3 the **Staff identity for branch cashiers** row (spec:1030), §3.0.4 rows 1062 (POS selling — the staff-PIN clause), **1064 (Cashier authority offline — this slice's owned row)** and 1065 (branch scoping), §3.7.5 the whole **Cashier identity offline** paragraph (spec:1320–1337, especially spec:1324), §3.8 the branch-isolation comment under `actor_has` (spec:1566–1569), §3.9 Naming, Enumerations, Deletion, Error codes, Database access (spec:2037–2053), §3.10.1 **Employee master** (spec:2075), **Attendance, minimal** (spec:2089) and **Termination** (spec:2090 — `pos_cashier.revoked_at` must exist here), §3.10.2 **Identity: employee, principal, cashier** (spec:2095–2097), §4.2 the PIN-hashing sentence (spec:2132) and the one-SQL-query sentence (spec:2134), §4.2.1 the `branch_manager` and `cashier` rows and the whole **Branch scoping** paragraph (spec:2160–2161, 2166), §4.2.2 **R8** (spec:2179), §5.2 the repository layout (spec:2352–2389), §5.3 the owner rows for the alias domain and the cashier list (spec:2406), Part V the Phase 6 row and the sequencing note (spec:2292, 2301), Part VI tests **34**, **36**, **37** (spec:2452, 2454, 2455), Part I **LEGACY-025** (spec:435), **LEGACY-026** (spec:448), **LEGACY-028** (spec:471), **LEGACY-033** (spec:510), **LEGACY-044** (spec:593), **LEGACY-045** (spec:598).

Hazard 33 therefore has nothing to bind here, and Task 9 asserts that fact rather than assuming it. **Report codes (§4.16.1) and `DatasetId` members (§4.16.2) — none.** Hazard 4's minimal-set list (spec:2300) gives Phase 6's two reports (`pos.z_report`, `pos.sales_by_cashier`) to **6D**; this slice ships no report and does not widen `ReportCodeSchema`. **`fixtures.ts` helpers (§4.17) — exactly the three rows §4.17 assigns 6C: `seedEmployee` (shared with 8A — first to execute commits it), `seedCashier`, `seedBranchPosPolicy`**, plus **two additive options on two of them** (`seedEmployee.hiredOn?`, `seedCashier.principalId?`), recorded on their own rows by Task 0 amendment **A12** under §4.17's closing rule. No fourth helper. **All three insert under `withTenant(db.migrator, s.tenant, s.system, …)` and not under `db.rw`/`s.human`** — every one of their tables carries a `branch_isolation` policy from this slice's own migration 0068, a `using`-only policy is applied as the `with check` on INSERT, and `seedTenant` grants `s.human` nothing (fact 2 below). That is Task 0 amendment **A15**, the same clause 6D's A17 states for `seedShift`; it changes no signature and no return. **Shared test helpers (§4.17.1) — one new file, registered by amendment A9:** `tests/invariants/support/staff.ts` with `StaffFixture`, `staffFixture`, `TicketDraft`, `applyTicket` and `branchActor` — **a thin layer over 6A's `posFixture`, never a second POS corpus** — plus **two additive options on 6A's `buildTicket`** (`discount?`, `lines?`), recorded by amendment **A13** on 6A's own §4.17.1 row.
- **Nine `Seed`, fixture and register facts every test in this plan depends on, stated once because getting one wrong is a silent failure.** (1) **`Seed` has no `branch` member** — it is `{ tenant; system; human; company; period; closedPeriod; lockedPeriod; cash; sales; docType; policy; branchType; hq }` (§4.1), and `s.hq` is the head-office `dimension_value` of type `BRANCH`. Every second branch in this plan comes from **0D's `seedBranch(db, s, opts?)`**. (2) **`seedTenant` grants `s.human` nothing** (§4.1, pinned) — every test that needs authority calls **2B's `grantCodes`** first, and `grantCodes`' `branchIds` option is what makes a principal branch-restricted. (3) **`app.principal` is a GLOBAL table with no `tenant_id`** (plan0A:988–998): a principal is inserted **without `RETURNING`**, with its `membership` row in the same transaction (plan0A:837), and every FK to it is the **single-column** inline form — `references app.principal(id)`, never a composite `(tenant_id, principal_id)` pair, which PostgreSQL refuses at DDL time. (4) **`app.dimension_value` is the branch table.** `home_branch_id`, `branch_id` on `pos_cashier`, `branch_pos_policy` and `attendance_event` are all `foreign key (tenant_id, <col>) references app.dimension_value(tenant_id, id)`, exactly as `pos_terminal.branch_id` already is (plan0A:2537). (5) **`app.employee` has no `archived_at` at 0064** — 8A adds it in 0080 (§4.6.1). Nothing in this slice archives an employee; deletion is not admitted (spec:2049) and revocation is `pos_cashier.revoked_at`. (6) **`pos_cashier` is in `NO_ID_TABLES` and `AUDIT_EXEMPT`** (§3.5.3) — its primary key is `principal_id`, it has no `id` column, and it carries **no `audit_row` trigger**, deliberately: an audited before/after image of a PIN hash is a copy of a credential in a table with a different read gate. (7) **Plan 0A seeds exactly three fiscal periods and only 2026-01 is open**; a test that posts outside January calls **3B's `ensureOpenPeriods(db, s, 2026)`** first. (8) **`app_rw` holds DELETE on exactly five tables** (§3.5.4) and **2B is the only slice that ever extends that list** — nothing in this slice deletes a row. (9) **A restrictive policy is ANDed, a permissive one is ORed.** Every policy this slice creates is `as restrictive`; `ALLOWED_PERMISSIVE` never grows (§3.5.3) and Task 5's own lint run is what proves it.
- **Never re-decide:** D1–D27 (spec:114–142); every name in the contracts document is frozen — a needed change goes to that document first (that is what Task 0 is).
- **Contracts version:** 2026-09-07.

## Global Constraints

Copied verbatim from the spec and the contracts document; every task's requirements implicitly include this section.

1. **Never**, at any phase, for any reason (spec:97–104): write to a ledger or movement table outside the posting service; represent money as a float, anywhere, including in TypeScript; build SQL by string concatenation (LEGACY-036/037/069); issue DDL from application code (LEGACY-053); disable an invariant, constraint or trigger "temporarily" (LEGACY-027, 041, 053); gate an audit write on any flag (LEGACY-030); hard-code a package version into a source file — the lockfile owns versions.
2. **Always**, for every new table (spec:106): `tenant_id`, an RLS policy named exactly `tenant_isolation`, `created_at`, a migration with a hand-written down, and a test that a second tenant cannot see it. For every new endpoint: Zod schema, OpenAPI entry, idempotency on unsafe methods, and a tenant-isolation test. For every UI screen: verified in both text directions before merge. **This slice creates four tables** and every one carries the §3.3 binding form and a two-tenant test in the same task.
3. **This slice writes no ledger, movement, layer, projection or tax row** (hazard 22). It posts nothing itself: the only path to `postDocument` is **6A's `applyBatch`**, which this slice extends with a *refusal* and never with a write. `packages/db/src/sync/limits.ts` performs `select` statements and nothing else, and Task 10 asserts that the `posting-only-tables` lint has no new allow-list entry.
4. **Money** is `numeric(19,4)` in Postgres and the opaque `Money` type in `@dulja/domain` in TypeScript; **a percentage limit is `numeric(9,6)` in Postgres and a 6-dp decimal string in TypeScript, never a `number`** (`no-number-money` matches `/amount|price|cost|debit|credit|total|balance|rate|tax|net|gross/i`, §3.7.5 — `maxVoidAmount` and `maxBlindReturnAmount` are inside it). Every comparison in `limits.ts` is `Money.compare` or `Decimal` arithmetic through `@dulja/domain`; there is no `Number()`, no `parseFloat` and no `.toFixed()` anywhere in this slice.
5. **Every query, reads included, runs inside `withTenant`** (spec:2053). `db.` is a lint violation; only a `tx` obtained from `withTenant` may tag SQL. **No `sql.unsafe` in any of this slice's source tiers** — `packages/db/src/**`, `packages/contracts/**`, `apps/api/src/**`, `apps/worker/src/**` — and Task 10 Step 5's pinned test asserts exactly that scope. **One documented use in the *test* tier**: Task 10 Step 1's table-driven case interpolates the fourteen literals of its own `TABLES` array into `tx.unsafe`, in a file under `tests/`, which is outside the lint runner's `ROOTS` (§3.7.5). Nothing from any input reaches it, and the alternative — fourteen hand-written cases — is fourteen chances to omit one, which is the leak the test exists to find (§3.3, hazard 21).
6. **One migration per PR** (spec:2057); each file runs whole in one transaction; every statement carries a comment naming the LEGACY-/spec reference it serves; SQL keywords lowercase; section banners `-- ── N. title ───`; a hand-written `NNNN.down.sql` that drops only what the up added, in reverse dependency order (§3.4.5).
7. **No enum label is added** (§3.4.4, hazard 11). The one new closed vocabulary in this slice — `attendance_event.kind` — is `text` + a named `check`, so 8D can add a label in one file if leave ever lands.
8. **Deletion** (spec:2049): financial rows never; master data is `archived_at`. §3.5.4's pinned `app_rw` DELETE list is **unchanged** by this slice and Task 10 re-asserts it as an equality. A cashier is **revoked** (`revoked_at`), never deleted; an employee is terminated (8C) and never deleted.
9. **Error codes** are `ERR_<DOMAIN>_<REASON>` enumerated in `packages/contracts/src/errors.ts`, and a code no code raises fails the build (spec:2051). **This slice adds exactly one row to `ERRORS`: `ERR_POS_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` (422), allocated by §4.2.4 to 6C, added in the PR that raises it (Task 9).** Everything else it raises is already shipped: `ERR_VALIDATION_FAILED`, `ERR_NOT_FOUND`, `ERR_RBAC_FORBIDDEN`, `ERR_RLS_DENIED`, `ERR_CONFLICT_UNIQUE`, `ERR_CONFLICT_REFERENCE`, `ERR_RBAC_KIND`, `ERR_OVERRIDE_NOT_AUTHORISED`, `ERR_TABLE_IMMUTABLE`, `ERR_ACTOR_IMMUTABLE`, `ERR_SYNC_TICKET_REJECTED`, `ERR_INTERNAL`. **This list is closed: a token in any test of this slice that is not on it is either a typo or an unregistered code, and both fail the `error-codes` lint.**
10. **The permission vocabulary and module list are closed** (§3.6.1, hazard 26). This slice seeds exactly the four codes §3.6.2 assigns it and invents none. **`pos.attendance.clock`, `pos.price.check` and `pos.shift.own` are 6B's and are not seeded here**; `pos.shift.close_any` and `pos.report.read` are 6D's.
11. **`app.actor_has` and `app.principal_has` are the only authorisation predicates** (spec:2166). No handler, trigger or policy in this slice asks a different question, and **nothing server-side reads `pos_cashier.snapshot` to make a decision** — the snapshot exists for the till, and the server holds the row it was projected from.
12. **The terminal acts, the cashier is attributed** (hazard 19, spec:1324, LEGACY-044). Nothing in this slice makes the cashier the acting principal: `attendance_event.recorded_by` is stamped by `app.stamp_actor` and is the terminal on the sync path; `employee_id` is the attribution. A column that records attribution is **never** named `*_by` (§3.3) — hence `cashier_principal_id`, `supervisor_principal_id` and `attributed_to`.
13. **No plan invents a literal in a shared namespace** (hazard 32). Document-type codes, account codes, report codes, `DatasetId` members, `fixtures.ts` helper names and `ERR_*` names all come from the registers; **Task 0 amends a register before any task writes a literal into it**.
14. **No real Malaysian statutory rate, threshold, ceiling or formula appears anywhere in this slice** (hazard 1). This slice touches HR tables and seeds no HR data at all: the `employee` rows in its tests carry a number, a branch and two dates, and `branch_pos_policy`'s defaults are all zero.
15. **The UI is not specified** (spec:79, 108). Task 13 begins with OD-24 and targets keyboard parity (spec:2254); no workflow is invented (hazard 29).
16. **Localisation** (spec:2061): every user-facing string goes through i18next with an ICU message; `packages/i18n` catalogues are complete in `en` and `ar` before merge; every screen is verified in both text directions (test 23).
17. **Commits** are conventional with the scope = workspace, subject `<type>(<scope>): <summary> [6C T<n>]`, and every commit message ends with the trailer `Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>` (§3.8). `bun run lint`, `bun run lint:rules`, `bun run format:check`, `bun run typecheck` and `bun run test` at the repository root stay green after every task. **This slice does not rewrite `AGENTS.md`'s "Current phase" section — §3.8 names 6D as Phase 6's closing slice.**

## This slice's share of the Phase 6 definition of done

The Phase 6 row, verbatim (spec:2292) — **Definition of done:** *"A till sells 8 hours offline and reconciles with **zero duplicates and zero lost tickets**; the shift Z-report ties to the ledger to the cent."* §2.7's card says this slice's share is **neither sentence**: 6A holds the server half of the first, 6B the till half, 6D the second. **6C gates the phase exit through tests 36 and 37 instead**, and those two are what this plan's DoD table tracks.

| Clause | Where it is green |
|---|---|
| **Test 37 — "a ticket whose discount exceeds the branch limit … is rejected at sync apply"** | **Task 9 Steps 3–4** — `verifyTicket` returns a `LimitRefusal` for a line discount over `max_line_discount_pct` and for a ticket discount over `max_ticket_discount_pct`, read from `app.branch_pos_policy` **inside the apply transaction**; `applyBatch` answers `rejected` with `ERR_POS_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`. |
| **Test 37 — "a void, or a blind return"** | **Task 9 Steps 3–4** — a `TicketEvent` with `subject: "void"` is checked against `max_void_amount`; a `kind: "return"` ticket with `returnsClientRef === null` **is** a blind return and is checked against `blind_return_allowed` and `max_blind_return_amount`. Both additionally require the cashier to hold `pos.ticket.void` / `pos.ticket.return_blind` through `app.principal_has` at that branch. |
| **Test 37 — "unless it carries an override by a principal other than the cashier who holds the permission at that branch"** | **Task 9 Steps 4–5** — `verifyTicket` lifts a refusal only for a `TicketEvent { kind: "override", subject, supervisorPrincipalId }` whose subject `OVERRIDE_LIFTS` maps to that limit kind; the supervisor's own authority and the second-principal rule are enforced by 2B's `assert_override_authorised` when **6A's `applyBatch` step (h)** inserts the event through `recordOverride` (§4.5.3) — one control, one writer, and this slice adds neither a second check nor a second insert. **Task 9 Step 3's fifth and sixth cases** prove it by re-sending the identical ticket with the cashier as their own supervisor, and with a supervisor who holds the code only at the *other* branch; both are `ERR_OVERRIDE_NOT_AUTHORISED`. |
| **Test 37 — "rejected tickets land in needs-attention, never in the ledger"** | **Task 9 Step 3's second and third cases** — after a refused batch, `app.needs_attention` holds one row carrying `error_code` and the refused ticket's branch, and `select count(*) from app.ledger_entry` is **unchanged from a value captured before the batch**, asserted as an equality. |
| **Test 36 — "a `branch_manager` at branch A cannot read or write documents, shifts, stock, attendance or tickets of branch B through any endpoint, filter or export"** | **Task 10 Steps 2–4** — one table-driven test over `document`, `pos_terminal`, `pos_cashier`, `branch_pos_policy`, `attendance_event`, `warehouse`, `price_list`, `promotion`, `inventory_movement`, `stock_on_hand`, `cost_layer`, `cost_consumption` and `stock_valuation`, run as a branch-A `branch_manager` against branch-B rows, through `withTenant` **and** through the API's list routes, asserting zero rows and a 404 on the by-id read. "Shifts" is 6D's table and Task 10 names the row 6D adds. |
| **Test 36 — "'who can reset a PIN at branch B' is one SQL query"** | **Task 10 Step 3** — `app.who_can_reset_a_pin`, one statement, asserted to return exactly the branch-B manager and not the branch-A one. |

**What is deliberately *not* in this slice's DoD share:** the offline sell (6B), the shift and Z-report (6D), the sync protocol and `POS_TICKET` itself (6A), and the **termination trigger** — 8C's migration 0093 is what sets `revoked_at` from `terminated_on`, and 8C's 0094 is the deferrable "cashiers are employees" constraint. This slice makes both *possible* (the column, the tombstone, the revision bump) and tests the revocation path by setting `revoked_at` directly.

### Task 0: Contracts amendments and the 6A / 3A / 2B repository gate

**No application code.** This task does two things and stops. First, it lists the **sixteen** amendments the architect makes to `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-dulja-plan-contracts.md` in one PR, so no later task writes a literal into a register that does not yet hold it (hazard 32). Second, it runs a gate script that checks the **repository**, not the document: the names this plan imports from 6A, 3A, 3B, 2B and 0B, plus three informational probes whose answers change what Tasks 5 and 7 do. **Task 1 does not start until `bash scripts/check/contracts-6c-amendments.sh` exits 0.**

**6A's plan file now exists and this plan has been reconciled against it.** Every name below that 6A owns — `applyBatch`'s four-parameter shape, `TerminalApplyContext`, `TicketOutcome`, `BatchOutcome`, `SyncError`, `SyncErrorCode`, `posFixture`, `buildTicket`, `app.catalogue_revision('<stream>')`, `app.catalogue_tombstone`'s `unique (stream, row_id)`, `app.terminal_enrolment`'s NOT NULL `name` / `terminal_code` / `bytea code_hash`, and the `app.pos_cashier_sync` / `app.branch_pos_policy_sync` functions 6A's `readCatalogue` calls — is consumed **verbatim from 6A's merged file**. The gate below turns each of them into a hard `need_repo` line, because a rename in 6A between now and execution is the one failure mode this task exists to catch.

The project folder is read-only to this plan's writer except this file, so the architect makes the amendments; the script is what proves they landed.

**Files:**
- Create: `scripts/check/contracts-6c-amendments.sh`
- Modify (architect, separate PR): `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-dulja-plan-contracts.md`

**Interfaces**

- Consumes: nothing at run time. The script uses `grep`, `test` and `git`.
- Produces: the gate script, and the fourteen register rows below.

- [ ] **Step 1: The sixteen contract amendments**

**Every amendment's text in the contracts document opens with the marker `**6C amendment A<n>.**`**, the convention 6D's A15–A18 already use. Step 2's gate greps for that marker rather than for a phrase from the amendment's body, because several of those phrases — `terminal_enrolment` is the clearest — already appear in the document today and would make a probe report `ok` before anything landed.

| # | § | Amendment | Blocks |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A1** | 4.5.6, 3.5.1 step 7 | **0068's Form A table list gains 6A's `terminal_enrolment` and `needs_attention`; Form B's gains 6A's `catalogue_tombstone`.** All three carry a branch key, all three are created by migrations 0060–0061 — *before* 0068 — and §3.5.1 step 7 attaches a form only "if 6C's migration 0068 has landed", so 6A **cannot** attach one and 0068 must. Without this, the `branch_isolation_policy` rule A2 adds reports three violations the day it ships and 6A's terminal-enrolment codes and needs-attention queue leak across branches, which is the same defect on the other side of the till. **No sixth *form* is created** — §4.5.6's "exactly five policy forms" is untouched; three tables join two existing lists, `branch_id NOT NULL` → Form A, nullable → Form B. **⚠️ Reconciled with 6A: this is a verification, not a second edit.** 6A's own Task 0 Step 4 makes exactly this amendment (6a:225 — *"Add to Form A's table list: `terminal_enrolment` and `needs_attention` … Add to Form B's table list: `catalogue_tombstone`"*), and 6A merges before 6C (§6.3). If §4.5.6 already carries it, A1 is discharged and nothing is written twice; if it does not, 6A's amendment was dropped in review and the architect lands it here under 6A's own wording. **The gate probes the one sentence 6A's Step 4 appends to §4.5.6's Form C paragraph** — *"6A's three tables are attached in 0068 like every other pre-0068 table"* — and never the bare word `terminal_enrolment`, which the contracts document already contains five times and which would make the probe report `ok` before anything landed. | **T5** |
| **A2** | 3.5.2, 3.5.3 | **The `branch_isolation_policy` rule's exemption set is declared: `BRANCH_ISOLATION_EXEMPT = new Set(["role_assignment_branch"])`, and it holds exactly that one name.** §3.5.2 defines the rule as firing on any `app` table with `branch_id` or `home_branch_id`, and `app.role_assignment_branch` (plan0A:1348–1357) has `branch_id uuid not null`. It must **never** carry the policy: `app.actor_in_branch` selects **from** `role_assignment_branch`, so a restrictive policy on it whose predicate calls `actor_in_branch` is self-referential and PostgreSQL aborts every read of the table with *"infinite recursion detected in policy for relation role_assignment_branch"*. The table is the branch **grant**, not branch-scoped **data**; it is already tenant-isolated and already unreadable across tenants. **The set is closed** — a slice that wants a second exemption amends this row. | **T5** |
| **A3** | 4.2.3, 3.11 | **`AuthAdmin` gains a second method: `createUser(email: string): Promise<InvitedUser>`** — the Supabase admin *create* call (random undisclosed password, no invitation e-mail), which spec:1324 requires at hire for a cashier who never opens the back office. `inviteUser` is unchanged and still the `/v1/admin/users` path. **The return type is `InvitedUser` and no `CreatedUser` is declared** — the shape is `{ sub, email }` in both cases and two names for one shape is hazard 9. `StubAuthAdmin.createUser` returns `sub = 'stub:' + sha256(email)`, identical to its invite; `SupabaseAuthAdmin` gains a `createPath` constructor option beside `invitePath`. **Which implementation the deployed container receives is 0C's ADR 0011 decision and is not re-opened here** (§3.11: `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` never enters the container). | **T8** |
| **A4** | 4.2.7, 4.2.8 | **Four route modules are registered to 6C**, with their paths, router exports and gate codes: `/v1/companies/:companyId/employees` → `employeeRoutes` (`hr.employee.read`, `hr.employee.manage`); `/v1/companies/:companyId/cashiers` → `createCashierRoutes(authAdmin)` — a **factory**, like 0B's users module and 2C's attachments module, because it needs `AuthAdmin`; gates `admin.user.manage`, `pos.cashier.manage_pin`; `/v1/companies/:companyId/branch-pos-policies` → `branchPosPolicyRoutes` (`admin.company.manage`); `/v1/companies/:companyId/attendance-events` → `attendanceEventRoutes` (`hr.employee.read`, `hr.attendance.record`). Four `PROBES` keys, no sub-path key (hazard 31). Contracts modules `hr/employee.ts`, `hr/attendance.ts`, `pos/cashier.ts`, `pos/branch-pos-policy.ts` join §4.2.8's register with the exports Task 3, 4 and 8 list. | **T3, T4, T8** |
| **A5** | 4.6.3, 4.2.5 | **The snapshot's code set is declared as a derivation, not a list: `CASHIER_SNAPSHOT_CODES`** in `packages/contracts/permissions.ts` = every `PermissionDef` whose `module === "pos"` and whose `code` does not start with `"pos.sync."`, and the SQL twin inside `app.refresh_pos_cashier` is `select code from app.permission where module = 'pos' and code not like 'pos.sync.%'`. A literal list would have to name `pos.shift.close_any` and `pos.report.read` — 6D's codes — which `PERMISSION[…]` cannot reference before 6D seeds them, and which the `permission-catalogue` lint would reject. **The two spellings are asserted equal by a test in T7**, which is what keeps the derivation honest. `pos.sync.*` is excluded because it is the **terminal's** authority (R8), never a cashier's. | **T7** |
| **A6** | 4.2.6 | **A third audit `table_name` sentinel is registered: `cashier_snapshot_drift`**, written by `pos.cashier.compare` when a recomputed snapshot differs from the stored one. §4.2.6 allocates `integrity_incident` and `month_end_ready` (both 1B's) and requires a slice needing a third to register it here first. The job **reports and does not repair**: a silent repair would hide the trigger fault the comparison exists to find (D14, ADR 0061). | **T7** |
| **A7** | 4.2.6 | **`JobPayload["pos.cashier.compare"]` is `{ companyId?: string }`** — omit the field for the whole tenant, exactly the shape 1B gave `accounting.ledger.integrity_check`. The `JOBS` entry itself (`schedule: "30 2 * * *", owner: "6C"`) is already in 0B's closed sixteen-name registry and **is not edited** — this slice adds one key to the type map and nothing else in `packages/db/src/jobs/`. | **T7** |
| **A8** | 4.10.4, 4.10.1 | **The attendance-insert path inside `applyBatch` is 6C's, in the shape §4.10.1 already uses for shifts and till events.** `Ticket.attendance[]` is 6A's message member but `app.attendance_event` is 6C's table (migration 0067), so 6A ships the path behind a capability check and **refuses, never drops**: a ticket arriving with a non-empty `attendance` array before 0067 has run answers `rejected` with `ERR_SYNC_MESSAGE_UNSUPPORTED`, the till keeps the outbox row, and **6C's Task 4 replaces the refusal with the insert and re-runs 6A's batch test** — word for word the contract §4.10.1's last paragraph writes for 6D's `shifts` and `tillEvents`. **The replacement lands in Task 4, not Task 9**: 6A's guard is `to_regclass('app.attendance_event') is null` (6A:3679), so the instant migration 0067 is applied the guard goes false and 6A has no insert path — a ticket carrying a clock-in would be accepted with the HR event silently dropped, which is the failure spec:1305 forbids, and 6A's own `apply.test.ts` case would stay red for five tasks against Global Constraint 17. Migration 0067 and the seam that consumes it ship in the same PR. | **T4** |
| **A9** | 4.17.1 | **`tests/invariants/support/staff.ts` is registered to 6C** with `StaffFixture`, `staffFixture`, `TicketDraft`, `applyTicket` and `branchActor`. Four test files need the same second branch, two cashiers, one supervisor and two policy rows — `tests/invariants/pos-authority.test.ts`, `tests/invariants/branch-scoping.test.ts`, `apps/api/test/routes/branch-scoping.test.ts` and Task 10's `legacy-044` block — and hazard 9 forbids four spellings of it. **It is a thin layer over 6A's `posFixture`, never a second POS corpus.** §4.17.1's 6A row already says *"the POS corpus every Phase 6 slice re-uses: a seeded company with items, a warehouse, stock, the tax pack, the `POS_TICKET` type and an enrolled terminal … 6C, 6D and 7C all consume it"* — so `staffFixture(api, name)` calls `posFixture(api, name)` first and adds **only** branch B, the three `pos_cashier` rows and the two `branch_pos_policy` rows; it seeds no company, no item, no warehouse, no stock, no tax pack and no document type. The row this amendment adds to §4.17.1 says exactly that, so a later reader cannot mistake it for a rival corpus. **It lives in the test tier and not in `fixtures.ts`** because it takes a `StartedApi` (`posFixture`'s own parameter) and `packages/db/test` may not import an app. `apps/api/test/**` reaches it through §4.17.1's existing named allowance and needs no new one. | **T9, T10** |
| **A10** | 3.6.2, 2.7 (6C card) | **Recorded, not changed.** §2.7's 6C card says `attendance_event` arrives "from the till clock-in **under `pos.attendance.clock`**". That code is **6B's** (§3.6.2), and 6B executes *after* 6C: a `PERMISSION["pos.attendance.clock"]` reference in this slice would not typecheck, and a `principal_has` check against a code no role yet holds would refuse every clock event. So at 6C the server's gate on a synced clock event is **the cashier's own unrevoked `pos_cashier` row at the terminal's branch**, the till evaluates the code from the snapshot (where A5's derivation puts it the moment 6B seeds it), and **6B adds the server-side `app.principal_has(cashier, 'pos.attendance.clock', …)` line in the same PR that seeds the code** — named here so it is a one-line addition in a named slice and not a gap nobody owns. Back-office corrections are gated by `hr.attendance.record` from this slice's first day. | **T4, T9** |
| **A11** | 4.5.8 | **Recorded, not changed.** 6C introduces `hr.employee.read` but adds **no** row to `MODULE_READ_CODE`. The map's `hr` row is `hr.payroll.read` and §4.5.8 assigns it to **8B**; its `pos` row is `pos.report.read` and belongs to **6D**. The line is added to §4.5.8's prose so a later reader does not go looking for the row 6C "should" have added, and so nobody wires an HR attachment gate to an employee-directory code. | — |
| **A12** | 4.17 | **Two `fixtures.ts` options are added additively and recorded: `seedEmployee`'s `hiredOn?: string` and `seedCashier`'s `principalId?: string`.** `seedCashier` mints its own principal and returns it; Task 4 Step 6 needs a `pos_cashier` row for a principal **6A's `posFixture` already created**, so the helper takes an optional existing principal id and skips the mint when it is supplied. Everything else about both helpers is unchanged, and neither return type moves. On `seedEmployee`: **it gains a fourth option, `hiredOn?: string`.** §4.17's row is `seedEmployee(db, s, opts?: { employeeNumber?; homeBranchId?; principalId? })` and its closing rule is *"a slice needing … an option a listed one lacks **adds the option additively** … and records it here in the same PR"*. Task 1 ships `hiredOn?` (defaulting to `'2026-01-01'`) because Task 8's directory tests and 8C's termination work both need a hire date they chose; without this row the register and the shipped helper diverge, which is the drift Task 0 exists to prevent, and 8A's "verify the options cover this slice's needs" task would find a fourth option nobody recorded. **No existing parameter changes and neither return is changed.** | **T1, T4** |
| **A13** | 4.17.1 | **6A's `buildTicket(f, opts)` gains two options, additively: `discount?: string` and `lines?: readonly { sku?; quantity?; unitPrice?; discount? }[]`.** §4.17.1's 6A row fixes `buildTicket` as the one place a `Ticket` is composed — it computes `total`, extracts the SYNTH tax from it, and negates all four fields on a `return`. Acceptance test 37 is *about discounts*, and one of its cases needs **three** lines each within the line limit whose sum breaks the ticket limit, so the helper as shipped cannot express the test that consumes it. The two options are additive: with neither, the single-line behaviour is byte-identical. **`discount` reduces `total` BEFORE the tax is extracted** — `total = unitPrice × quantity − discount`, then `taxAmount` from that — because `applyBatch` step (g) recomputes tax from the stored line and refuses a disagreement (6A:3673), so a helper that taxed the gross would make every discounted ticket in this slice fail with `ERR_VALIDATION_FAILED`. 6C adds them in Task 9's PR and re-runs 6A's `packages/db/test/sync/` suites in the same commit. | **T9** |
| **A14** | 4.2.4, 4.10.4 | **`SyncErrorCode` gains `ERR_RBAC_FORBIDDEN` and `ERR_POS_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`.** 6A ships `export class SyncError extends Error { constructor(readonly code: SyncErrorCode, readonly detail: string) }` in `packages/db/src/sync/enrolment.ts` with a seven-member union, and states the reason a second class does not exist: `AppError` lives in `@dulja/contracts` and `dependency-rule` pins `packages/db` to `['@dulja/domain']`. `SyncErrorCode` is *"a literal **subset** of `ErrCode` and never a new code"*, so widening it by two codes that are both in `ERRORS` keeps that property exactly. `applyBatch`'s per-ticket classifier already turns a `SyncError` into `TicketOutcome.error.code`, so this is the whole of what Task 9's seam needs — **no third error type is created in `packages/db/src/sync/`, and `PostingErrorCode` is not touched.** `ERR_POS_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` is added to `ERRORS` in the same PR (Task 9), which is what keeps the `error-codes` lint green. | **T9** |
| **A15** | 4.17 | **§4.17's preamble gains a second exception clause: a helper whose row's table carries a `branch_isolation` policy inserts under `withTenant(db.migrator, s.tenant, s.system, …)`, not under `db.rw`/`s.human`.** §4.17 today says *"Helpers insert on `db.rw` inside `withTenant(sql, s.tenant, s.human, …)` … unless the row is catalog-level"*. From migration 0068 that sentence is **unfollowable** for a branch-keyed table: `app.actor_in_branch` (§4.5.6) is true only for a `kind = 'system'` principal or one holding a live `app.role_assignment` in the company, `seedTenant` grants `s.human` nothing (§4.1, pinned), and a policy declared with `using` and no `with check` is applied **as** the `with check` on INSERT — so an `s.human` insert fails with `new row violates row-level security policy for table "<table>"`. The clause therefore reads: *"or unless the row's table carries a `branch_isolation` policy"*. **The three 6C rows it binds are `seedEmployee` (Form A′), `seedCashier` (Form A on `pos_cashier`) and `seedBranchPosPolicy` (Form A), all of which Tasks 1, 2 and 3 ship in that shape from their first commit** — not retro-fitted at Task 5, because a fixture that was green for four tasks and red at the fifth is the drift this row exists to stop. **6D's amendment A17 states the identical clause for `seedShift`** (6d:321) and cites 6C as the precedent; the two are one rule with one wording, and this row is what makes 6D's citation true. **It changes no signature and no return.** The helpers that must NOT change are named too: 0A's `draftDocument` is frozen (§4.1) and 3A's `seedWarehouse` is 3A's — a suite that calls either against a branch-keyed table calls **2B's `grantCodes(db, s)`** in its `beforeAll` instead, which gives `s.human` a company-wide `role_assignment` and makes `actor_in_branch` true for every branch. **The amendment's text opens with the marker `**6C amendment A15.**`** | **T1, T2, T3, T5** |
| **A16** | 2.7 (6C card), 3.4.1 | **§2.7's 6C card and §3.4.1's parenthetical are updated to `0067_create_attendance_event_and_cashier_snapshot.sql`.** Both documents name the file `0067_create_attendance_event.sql`; this slice ships the longer name from the file's first commit, because `app.refresh_pos_cashier`, its four triggers and `app.who_can_reset_a_pin` are DDL, 0064–0069 are all spoken for, and §3.4.2's round-2 rule renames a file whose name understates its contents. **That rule's own precedent text is explicit that the register is updated with it** — *"Both cards in §2.3 now state the long name"* (2A's `0018_…`, 2B's `0023_…`) — so leaving §2.7 saying the short name would make the register and the repository disagree, which is the drift Task 0 exists to prevent. **No rename ever happens in the repository**: Task 4 creates the file already so named and Task 7 appends to it. **The amendment's text opens with the marker `**6C amendment A16.**`** | **T4, T7** |

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the gate script**

`scripts/check/contracts-6c-amendments.sh`:

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Gate for slice 6C. Exits 0 only when every contracts amendment has landed and every repo name this
# plan imports exists with the spelling this plan uses. Run from the repository root.
set -uo pipefail
C="docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-dulja-plan-contracts.md"
fail=0

need_doc() {  # need_doc <label> <fixed-string>
  if grep -qF -- "$2" "$C"; then printf 'ok   (contracts) %s\n' "$1"
  else printf 'MISSING (contracts) %s -- expected the literal: %s\n' "$1" "$2"; fail=1; fi
}
need_repo() {  # need_repo <label> <file> <fixed-string>
  if [ -f "$2" ] && grep -qF -- "$3" "$2"; then printf 'ok   (repo) %s\n' "$1"
  else printf 'MISSING (repo) %s -- %s does not carry: %s\n' "$1" "$2" "$3"; fail=1; fi
}

# A1 is probed by the SENTENCE 6A's Task 0 Step 4 appends to §4.5.6's Form C paragraph, never by the bare
# word `terminal_enrolment`: that word is already in the contracts document five times today, so a probe on
# it would print `ok` before any amendment landed and the gate would be lying about the one row it cannot
# afford to be wrong on. A1 is a VERIFICATION of 6A's amendment, not a second edit (see its row above).
need_doc "A1 0068 gains 6A's three tables"  "6A's three tables are attached in 0068 like every other pre-0068 table"
need_doc "A2 BRANCH_ISOLATION_EXEMPT"       "BRANCH_ISOLATION_EXEMPT"
need_doc "A3 AuthAdmin.createUser"          "createUser(email: string): Promise<InvitedUser>"
need_doc "A4 four 6C route modules"         "/v1/companies/:companyId/branch-pos-policies"
need_doc "A5 CASHIER_SNAPSHOT_CODES"        "CASHIER_SNAPSHOT_CODES"
need_doc "A6 cashier_snapshot_drift"        "cashier_snapshot_drift"
need_doc "A7 compare payload"               '"pos.cashier.compare": { companyId?: string }'
need_doc "A8 attendance apply path is 6C's" "the attendance-insert path inside"
need_doc "A9 support/staff.ts"              "tests/invariants/support/staff.ts"
need_doc "A10 pos.attendance.clock is 6B's" "6B adds the server-side"
need_doc "A11 no MODULE_READ_CODE row"      "adds **no** row to"
need_doc "A12 fixtures options"             "principalId?; hiredOn?"
need_doc "A13 buildTicket discount/lines"   "lines?: readonly { sku?; quantity?; unitPrice?; discount? }[]"
need_doc "A14 SyncErrorCode widened"        "SyncErrorCode"
# A15 and A16 are probed by their own markers. A15's body sentence ("or unless the row's table carries a
# `branch_isolation` policy") is word for word 6D's A17, so a probe on it would pass on 6D's amendment and
# say nothing about 6C's; A16's long file name is unique to this amendment and is probed directly as well.
need_doc "A15 fixtures insert as system"    "6C amendment A15."
need_doc "A15 the preamble clause itself"   "or unless the row's table carries a"
need_doc "A16 0067 long name in the card"   "0067_create_attendance_event_and_cashier_snapshot.sql"

# ── Repository names this plan imports. The OWNING plan is the authority for what it exports (§4.18):
#    a MISSING line here is a reconciliation with that slice, never a second spelling invented locally.
need_repo "0B AuthAdmin port"        apps/api/src/ports.ts                  "export interface AuthAdmin"
need_repo "0B StubAuthAdmin"         apps/api/src/auth-admin.ts             "export class StubAuthAdmin"
need_repo "0B buildRoutes"           apps/api/src/routes/index.ts           "export function buildRoutes"
need_repo "0B sharedApi"             tests/support/api.ts                   "export function sharedApi"
need_repo "0A lintSchema sets"       packages/db/src/lint-schema.ts         "export const AUDIT_EXEMPT"
need_repo "2A seedParty"             packages/db/test/fixtures.ts           "export async function seedParty"
need_repo "2B grantCodes"            packages/db/test/fixtures.ts           "export async function grantCodes"
need_repo "3A seedWarehouse"         packages/db/test/fixtures.ts           "export async function seedWarehouse"
need_repo "3B ensureOpenPeriods"     tests/invariants/support/post-inventory.ts "export async function ensureOpenPeriods"
need_repo "6A applyBatch"            packages/db/src/sync/apply.ts          "export async function applyBatch"
need_repo "6A TerminalApplyContext"  packages/db/src/sync/apply.ts          "export interface TerminalApplyContext"
need_repo "6A TicketOutcome"         packages/db/src/sync/apply.ts          "export interface TicketOutcome"
need_repo "6A TicketInput"           packages/db/src/sync/apply.ts          "export interface TicketInput"
need_repo "6A SyncError"             packages/db/src/sync/enrolment.ts      "export class SyncError"
need_repo "6A SyncErrorCode"         packages/db/src/sync/enrolment.ts      "export type SyncErrorCode"
need_repo "6A readCatalogue"         packages/db/src/sync/catalogue.ts      "export async function readCatalogue"
need_repo "6A Ticket message"        packages/sync/src/messages.ts          "export interface Ticket"
need_repo "6A TicketEvent"           packages/sync/src/messages.ts          "export interface TicketEvent"
need_repo "6A seedTerminal"          packages/db/test/fixtures.ts           "export async function seedTerminal"
need_repo "6A posFixture"            tests/invariants/support/pos.ts        "export async function posFixture"
need_repo "6A buildTicket"           tests/invariants/support/pos.ts        "export function buildTicket"

# 2B's two artefacts, probed through names 2B owns rather than through file names this plan guessed.
# §4.3.1 fixes the posting directory as errors.ts / load.ts / issue.ts / post.ts / reverse.ts / index.ts --
# there is no lifecycle.ts -- and 6A's own gate probes reverse.ts and 0022_create_document_status_triggers.sql.
# Probing the BARREL and globbing the 0022_* file makes this gate independent of 2B's internal file naming.
need_repo "2B recordOverride"        packages/db/src/posting/index.ts       "recordOverride"
if ls packages/db/migrations/0022_*.sql >/dev/null 2>&1 && \
   grep -qF -- "app.principal_has" packages/db/migrations/0022_*.sql; then
  printf 'ok   (repo) %s\n' "2B principal_has (0022_*.sql)"
else
  printf 'MISSING (repo) %s\n' "2B principal_has -- no packages/db/migrations/0022_*.sql carries app.principal_has"; fail=1
fi

# ── Three INFORMATIONAL probes. They never fail the gate; they change what T5 and T7 do. Read them.
#    (The two probes that asked what applyBatch's parameters and error class are have been PROMOTED to the
#     need_repo lines above: 6A's merged file answers both, and a guess is no longer admissible.)
printf '\n-- informational --\n'
printf -- '-- 1. the branch key of 6A''s three tables (A1: NOT NULL -> Form A, nullable -> Form B) --\n'
grep -nE "branch_id" packages/db/migrations/0060_*.sql packages/db/migrations/0059_*.sql 2>/dev/null || \
  printf '     (no 0059/0060 branch_id lines found -- read the migrations by hand before T5)\n'
printf '     ^ T5 Step 1 reads these three declarations and picks the form. A "not null" is Form A.\n'
printf '       6A ships terminal_enrolment.branch_id NOT NULL, needs_attention.branch_id NOT NULL and\n'
printf '       catalogue_tombstone.branch_id NULLABLE, which is what T5 Step 4 writes; this prints the proof.\n'

printf -- '\n-- 2. the trigger ORDER on app.pos_cashier (T7 depends on it) --\n'
grep -n "tg_argv\|nextval('app.catalogue_revision_seq')" packages/db/migrations/0059_*.sql 2>/dev/null | head -10 || true
printf '     ^ 6A%s app.catalogue_revision() takes the stream as tg_argv[0] and assigns new.revision in a\n' "'s"
printf '       BEFORE trigger. T7 section 3a adds snapshot_revision_stamp, also BEFORE, which PostgreSQL fires\n'
printf '       SECOND because same-event triggers fire in NAME order and "catalogue_revision" sorts first.\n'

printf -- '\n-- 3. where does applyBatch call into per-ticket checks? T4 and T9 insert their seams there --\n'
grep -n "savepoint\|principal_has\|postDocument\|recordOverride\|attendance\|to_regclass" packages/db/src/sync/apply.ts 2>/dev/null | head -24 || true
printf '     ^ read these. The limit check runs AFTER step (c)%s authority check and AFTER step (f)%s lines,\n' "'s" "'s"
printf '       and BEFORE step (h)%s recordOverride loop, inside the same savepoint. 6A step (h) ALREADY\n' "'s"
printf '       records every TicketEvent -- T9 adds no second recordOverride call (see T9 Step 5).\n'

exit "$fail"
```

The script prints **seventeen** `(contracts)` lines for sixteen amendments — A15 is probed twice, once by its marker and once by the preamble clause it adds, because the marker proves *this slice's* row landed and the clause proves the sentence a fixture author actually reads was changed.

Expected before the architect's PR: **sixteen** `MISSING (contracts)` lines and exit 1 — **A1 reports `ok` already**, because 6A's own Task 0 Step 4 makes that amendment and 6A merges before 6C (§6.3). A `MISSING` on A1 means 6A's Step 4 was dropped in review: raise it with 6A's owner and land it under 6A's wording rather than inventing a second one. After the architect's PR: seventeen `ok (contracts)` lines, twenty-three `ok (repo)` lines, the three informational blocks, exit 0.

**A `MISSING (repo)` line is a stop-and-raise, not a workaround.** It means this plan and the owning slice disagree about a name; correct **this** plan's import, never add a second spelling (§4.18, hazard 9).

This task carries both branches, because the plan writer cannot know the execution order — §6.3 lets 8A start "as soon as Phase 4 is green", in parallel with Phases 5–7, and Phase 6 starts on the same gate.

`packages/db/test/staff/employee.test.ts`:

```ts
import { afterAll, beforeAll, describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { startDb, uuidv7, withTenant, type Db } from "../harness";
import { seedTenant, seedEmployee, seedBranch, grantCodes, type Seed } from "../fixtures";
import { lintSchema, listApplied } from "../../src";

let db: Db;
let a: Seed;
let b: Seed;

beforeAll(async () => {
  db = await startDb();
  a = await seedTenant(db, "alpha");
  b = await seedTenant(db, "beta");
  // `s.human` reads `app.employee` below, and from Task 5's migration 0068 that table carries Form A′ of
  // the RESTRICTIVE `branch_isolation` family: a principal with no live `app.role_assignment` in the
  // company sees ZERO rows and every read here would come back empty. One company-wide grant (no
  // `branchIds`) restores the pre-0068 behaviour for this suite's own actor, and is what `app.actor_has`
  // already calls "company-wide authority". It is written here, not added at Task 5, because a suite that
  // was green for four tasks and red at the fifth is the drift amendment A15 exists to stop.
  await grantCodes(db, a);
}, 180_000);
afterAll(async () => { await db.stop(); });

describe("app.employee — the minimal master (§4.6.2, spec:2075)", () => {
  it("migration 64 is applied and the table carries exactly the frozen columns", async () => {
    expect(await listApplied(db.migrator)).toContain(64);
    const cols = await db.admin<{ column_name: string; is_nullable: string }[]>`
      select column_name, is_nullable from information_schema.columns
       where table_schema = 'app' and table_name = 'employee' order by column_name`;
    expect(cols.map((c) => c.column_name)).toEqual([
      "company_id", "created_at", "employee_number", "hired_on", "home_branch_id",
      "id", "party_id", "principal_id", "tenant_id", "terminated_on",
    ]);
    // 8A adds position / employment_type / statutory_profile_note / archived_at in 0080 — NOT here (§4.6.1).
    expect(cols.find((c) => c.column_name === "principal_id")!.is_nullable).toBe("YES");
    expect(cols.find((c) => c.column_name === "terminated_on")!.is_nullable).toBe("YES");
  });

  it("seedEmployee creates one party of kind employee and one employee", async () => {
    const id = await seedEmployee(db, a, { employeeNumber: "E-0001" });
    const [row] = await withTenant(db.rw, a.tenant, a.human, (tx) => tx<
      { employee_number: string; home_branch_id: string; kind: string }[]
    >`select e.employee_number, e.home_branch_id, p.kind
        from app.employee e join app.party p on p.id = e.party_id where e.id = ${id}`);
    expect(row).toEqual({ employee_number: "E-0001", home_branch_id: a.hq, kind: "employee" });
  });

  it("employee_number is unique within a company and free across companies", async () => {
    await seedEmployee(db, a, { employeeNumber: "E-DUP" });
    await expect(seedEmployee(db, a, { employeeNumber: "E-DUP" })).rejects.toThrow(/employee_company_id_employee_number_key|duplicate key/);
    await expect(seedEmployee(db, b, { employeeNumber: "E-DUP" })).resolves.toBeTruthy();
  });

  it("principal_id is unique across the whole table — one till identity, one person (spec:2097)", async () => {
    const pid = await uuidv7(db.admin);
    await withTenant(db.migrator, a.tenant, a.system, async (tx) => {
      await tx`insert into app.principal (id, kind, auth_subject, display_name)
               values (${pid}, 'human', ${"sub-" + pid}, 'Cashier One')`;
      await tx`insert into app.membership (tenant_id, principal_id) values (${a.tenant}, ${pid})`;
    });
    await seedEmployee(db, a, { employeeNumber: "E-P1", principalId: pid });
    await expect(seedEmployee(db, a, { employeeNumber: "E-P2", principalId: pid })).rejects.toThrow(/principal_id|duplicate key/);
  });

    await seedEmployee(db, a, { employeeNumber: "E-ISO" });
    const rows = await withTenant(db.rw, b.tenant, b.human, (tx) =>
      tx`select id from app.employee where employee_number = 'E-ISO'`);
    expect(rows).toHaveLength(0);
  });

  it("home_branch_id must be a dimension_value of this tenant", async () => {
    const other = await seedBranch(db, b, { code: "B2" });
    await expect(seedEmployee(db, a, { employeeNumber: "E-XB", homeBranchId: other }))
      .rejects.toThrow(/employee_tenant_id_home_branch_id_fkey|violates foreign key/);
  });

  it("the schema linter reports zero violations after 0064", async () => {
    expect(await lintSchema(db.admin)).toEqual([]);
  });
});
```

```sql
-- 0064_create_employee_minimal.sql — §3.10.1 "Employee master", the minimal master (spec:2075); cashiers are employees (spec:2097).
create table app.employee (
  id               uuid primary key default uuidv7(),
  tenant_id        uuid not null references app.tenant(id),
  company_id       uuid not null,
  party_id         uuid not null,                                  -- 1:1 with a party of kind 'employee'
  employee_number  text not null,
  home_branch_id   uuid not null,                                  -- BRANCH dimension value (spec:2075)
  principal_id     uuid unique references app.principal(id),       -- nullable 1:1: only staff who log in or sell (spec:2097)
  hired_on         date not null,
  terminated_on    date,                                           -- AFTER UPDATE OF terminated_on trigger arrives with 8C (migration 0093)
  created_at       timestamptz not null default now(),
  unique (company_id, employee_number),
  unique (party_id),
  unique (tenant_id, id),
  foreign key (tenant_id, company_id)     references app.company(tenant_id, id),
  foreign key (tenant_id, party_id)       references app.party(tenant_id, id),
  foreign key (tenant_id, home_branch_id) references app.dimension_value(tenant_id, id)
);
call app_meta.enable_tenant_rls('app.employee');
call app_meta.attach_audit('app.employee');
-- branch_isolation is attached by 0068 (6C) if this file ran before it, or here if 0068 already exists:
do $$ begin
  if exists (select 1 from pg_proc p join pg_namespace n on n.oid = p.pronamespace where n.nspname = 'app' and p.proname = 'actor_in_branch') then
    execute 'create policy branch_isolation on app.employee as restrictive using (app.actor_in_branch(company_id, home_branch_id))';
  end if;
end $$;
```

`packages/db/migrations/0064.down.sql`:

```sql
-- §3.4.5: every object the up created is dropped, in reverse dependency order. The policy and both
-- triggers go with the table; app_meta and its ledger are never dropped.
drop table if exists app.employee;
```

**Why the `do $$` block and not a plain `create policy`.** 0068 is this slice's own later migration, so under 6C-first execution `app.actor_in_branch` does not exist yet and a plain statement would abort 0064. Under 8A-first execution — 8A's range is 0080–0086, entirely above 0068 — the function *does* exist and the policy is attached here, which is why 0068's Form A′ is written `if not exists` in return (T5 Step 4). The two files are the only pair in the system that can run in either order, and each carries the guard for the other.

Append (never edit an existing helper — §4.1, hazard 9):

```ts
/**
 * A minimal employee and the `party` of kind 'employee' it extends (§4.6.2; spec:2075, 2097).
 * Owned by 6C / 8A — whichever executes first commits it; the other extends its options additively.
 * `principalId` is left null unless given: only staff who log in or sell have one.
 *
 * **It inserts as `db.migrator` / `s.system`, and that is amendment A15, not a style choice.**
 * `app.employee` carries Form A′ of the RESTRICTIVE `branch_isolation` family from migration 0068
 * (§4.5.6): `app.actor_in_branch(company_id, home_branch_id)`, which is true only for a `kind = 'system'`
 * principal or one holding a live `app.role_assignment` in the company. `seedTenant` grants `s.human`
 * nothing (§4.1, pinned), and a policy declared with `using` and no `with check` is applied AS the
 * `with check` on INSERT — so an `s.human` insert here fails with
 * `new row violates row-level security policy for table "employee"` the moment Task 5 lands.
 * 6D's `seedShift` is written the same way for the same reason (6D amendment A17).
 */
export async function seedEmployee(
  db: Db,
  s: Seed,
  opts: { employeeNumber?: string; homeBranchId?: string; principalId?: string; hiredOn?: string } = {},
): Promise<string> {
  const employeeNumber = opts.employeeNumber ?? `E-${(await uuidv7(db.admin)).slice(-8)}`;
  const partyId = await seedParty(db, s, { kind: "employee", code: `EMP-${employeeNumber}` });
  const id = await uuidv7(db.admin);
  await withTenant(db.migrator, s.tenant, s.system, (tx) => tx`
    insert into app.employee (id, tenant_id, company_id, party_id, employee_number, home_branch_id, principal_id, hired_on)
    values (${id}, ${s.tenant}, ${s.company}, ${partyId}, ${employeeNumber},
            ${opts.homeBranchId ?? s.hq}, ${opts.principalId ?? null}, ${opts.hiredOn ?? "2026-01-01"})`);
  return id;
}
```

**`hiredOn` defaults to `'2026-01-01'`, the same literal amendment A12 records on §4.17's row.** The value is load-bearing nowhere, but the register and the shipped helper saying two different dates is exactly the drift A12 exists to prevent, so the two are written from one literal.

### Task 2: Migration 0065 — `app.pos_cashier`, the Argon2id PIN module, ADR 0060

**The one credential the application hashes** (spec:2132, LEGACY-025/026/028). Everything else about a human's authentication is Supabase's; the PIN exists because a till must let a cashier sign in with no network, and it is **never a login** — no route accepts a PIN, and the server never compares one. This task creates the row, the hash function and the tombstone path that makes revocation visible to a disconnected till.

**Files:**
- Create: `packages/db/migrations/0065_create_pos_cashier.sql`, `packages/db/migrations/0065.down.sql`, `packages/db/src/staff/pin.ts`, `packages/db/src/schema/pos-cashier.ts`, `packages/db/test/staff/pin.test.ts`, `packages/db/test/staff/pos-cashier.test.ts`, `docs/adr/0060-argon2id-in-webassembly.md`
- Modify: `packages/db/src/lint-schema.ts` (two set memberships), `packages/db/test/fixtures.ts` (add `seedCashier`), `packages/db/package.json` (add `hash-wasm` — no version literal in any source file)

**Interfaces**

- Consumes: `app.principal`, `app.company`, `app.employee` (T1), `app.dimension_value`, `app.catalogue_revision()` and `app.catalogue_tombstone` (6A, §4.10.2), `app_meta.enable_tenant_rls`, `AUDIT_EXEMPT`, `lintSchema`, `grantCodes` (2B), `seedEmployee` (T1).

  ```ts
  export const PIN_MAX_LENGTH = 12;
  ```

- [ ] **Step 1: Write ADR 0060 as a proposal (spec:95 — this touches security)**

`docs/adr/0060-argon2id-in-webassembly.md`:

```markdown
# ADR 0060 — Argon2id in WebAssembly: one PIN function for the server, the tests and the till

- Status: **proposed** (spec:95 — a decision touching security is written as a proposal and asked before implementing)
- Date: 2026-09-05
- Slice: 6C

## Context

spec:2132 makes the cashier PIN the only credential the application itself hashes, and names the
algorithm: Argon2id with a per-cashier salt. The same function must run in three places:

1. `apps/api` on **Bun** — the set-PIN and reset-PIN routes.
2. **Vitest on Node 24** (D5) — every test in this slice and in 6B.
3. **Electron's main process at the till** (6B) — the PIN is *verified* offline against the
   down-synced `catalogue_cashier` mirror, with no network and no server.

A native N-API module (`@node-rs/argon2`, `argon2`) satisfies the first two and makes the third an
electron-builder rebuild problem — the same class of problem ADR 0017 already records for
`better-sqlite3`, and one that produces a till which installs and then cannot log anybody in.
`Bun.password` satisfies only the first: it does not exist under Vitest, so the test tier could not
verify a hash the API produced.

## Decision

`hash-wasm`'s `argon2id` / `argon2Verify`, wrapped in **one** module, `packages/db/src/staff/pin.ts`,
exported from `@dulja/db`. Pure WebAssembly: identical bytes on Bun, Node and Electron, no ABI, no
rebuild step, no per-platform binary in the installer.

Parameters: `parallelism: 1`, `iterations: 2`, `memorySize: 19456` (KiB), `hashLength: 32`, and a
16-byte salt from `crypto.getRandomValues`. `outputType: "encoded"`, so `pos_cashier.pin_hash` is a
self-describing PHC string and the parameters travel with every hash — a later cost increase rehashes
on next set and verifies old hashes unchanged.

A PIN is low-entropy by construction, so the cost parameter is the whole defence and the band
`PIN_MIN_LENGTH = 4` / `PIN_MAX_LENGTH = 12` is enforced in `hashPin` rather than only in Zod: the
function that stores the credential is the one that must refuse a one-character one.

## Consequences

- `packages/db` gains one runtime dependency. It is pure WASM, so `packages/db` stays importable from
  `apps/worker`, `apps/api` and (through 6B's main process) Electron, with no change to §3.1's tiers.
- The PIN never leaves the till except at set/reset time, and the server never compares one:
  `verifyPin` exists in `@dulja/db` for 6B to call locally, and **no route in this slice calls it**.
  A test in Task 10 asserts that, by scanning `apps/api` for the symbol.
- Rotating the cost parameters is a change to this file plus a rehash-on-next-set; no migration.
- If the owner or a reviewer prefers a native module, the change is confined to this one file, and
  6B's ADR 0017 is where the Electron consequence must then be re-argued.
```

`packages/db/test/staff/pin.test.ts` — a pure unit test; no database.

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { hashPin, verifyPin, PinFormatError, PIN_MIN_LENGTH, PIN_MAX_LENGTH } from "../../src/staff/pin";

describe("PIN hashing — the one credential the application hashes (spec:2132; LEGACY-028)", () => {
  it("produces a PHC-encoded argon2id string carrying its own parameters", async () => {
    const phc = await hashPin("1234");
    expect(phc.startsWith("$argon2id$")).toBe(true);
    expect(phc).toContain("m=19456");
    expect(phc).toContain("t=2");
    expect(phc).toContain("p=1");
  });

  it("salts per cashier — the same PIN twice gives two different hashes", async () => {
    expect(await hashPin("1234")).not.toBe(await hashPin("1234"));
  });

  it("verifies the right PIN and refuses the wrong one", async () => {
    const phc = await hashPin("998877");
    expect(await verifyPin("998877", phc)).toBe(true);
    expect(await verifyPin("998878", phc)).toBe(false);
  });

  it("returns false rather than throwing on a malformed stored hash", async () => {
    expect(await verifyPin("1234", "not-a-phc-string")).toBe(false);
    expect(await verifyPin("1234", "")).toBe(false);
  });

  it("refuses a PIN outside the length band, in the function that stores it", async () => {
    await expect(hashPin("1")).rejects.toBeInstanceOf(PinFormatError);
    await expect(hashPin("0".repeat(PIN_MAX_LENGTH + 1))).rejects.toBeInstanceOf(PinFormatError);
    await expect(hashPin("0".repeat(PIN_MIN_LENGTH))).resolves.toContain("$argon2id$");
  });

  it("refuses a non-digit PIN — a till keypad has ten keys", async () => {
    await expect(hashPin("12a4")).rejects.toBeInstanceOf(PinFormatError);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write `packages/db/src/staff/pin.ts`**

```bash
bun add --cwd packages/db hash-wasm
```

(The lockfile owns the version — no literal reaches a source file, spec:104.)

```ts
import { argon2id, argon2Verify } from "hash-wasm";

/** ADR 0060. A till keypad has ten keys; the band is enforced here, in the function that stores the credential. */
export const PIN_MIN_LENGTH = 4;
export const PIN_MAX_LENGTH = 12;
const PIN_PATTERN = /^[0-9]+$/;

/** OWASP's Argon2id baseline. The PHC string carries them, so raising a cost rehashes on next set only. */
const PARALLELISM = 1;
const ITERATIONS = 2;
const MEMORY_SIZE_KIB = 19456;
const HASH_LENGTH = 32;
const SALT_BYTES = 16;

export class PinFormatError extends Error {
  readonly code = "ERR_VALIDATION_FAILED" as const;
  constructor(detail: string) {
    super(detail);
    this.name = "PinFormatError";
  }
}

function assertPin(pin: string): void {
  if (pin.length < PIN_MIN_LENGTH || pin.length > PIN_MAX_LENGTH) {
    throw new PinFormatError(`a PIN is ${PIN_MIN_LENGTH}-${PIN_MAX_LENGTH} digits; this one is ${pin.length}`);
  }
  if (!PIN_PATTERN.test(pin)) throw new PinFormatError("a PIN is digits only");
}

/** Argon2id with a fresh 16-byte salt, PHC-encoded (spec:1324, 2132; ADR 0060). */
export async function hashPin(pin: string): Promise<string> {
  assertPin(pin);
  const salt = crypto.getRandomValues(new Uint8Array(SALT_BYTES));
  return argon2id({
    password: pin,
    salt,
    parallelism: PARALLELISM,
    iterations: ITERATIONS,
    memorySize: MEMORY_SIZE_KIB,
    hashLength: HASH_LENGTH,
    outputType: "encoded",
  });
}

/**
 * Verifies at the till (6B) against the down-synced mirror. Returns false — never throws — on a hash
 * this build cannot parse, so a corrupted mirror row is a refused login and not a crashed till.
 */
export async function verifyPin(pin: string, phc: string): Promise<boolean> {
  if (pin.length < PIN_MIN_LENGTH || pin.length > PIN_MAX_LENGTH || !PIN_PATTERN.test(pin)) return false;
  if (!phc.startsWith("$argon2id$")) return false;
  try {
    return await argon2Verify({ password: pin, hash: phc });
  } catch {
    return false;
  }
}
```

```ts
import { afterAll, beforeAll, describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { startDb, uuidv7, withTenant, type Db } from "../harness";
import { seedTenant, seedBranch, seedEmployee, seedCashier, grantCodes, type Seed } from "../fixtures";
import { lintSchema, listApplied, AUDIT_EXEMPT } from "../../src";
import { verifyPin } from "../../src/staff/pin";

let db: Db;
let a: Seed;
let b: Seed;

beforeAll(async () => {
  db = await startDb();
  a = await seedTenant(db, "alpha");
  b = await seedTenant(db, "beta");
  // Every read and UPDATE below runs as `a.human` against `app.pos_cashier` and `app.catalogue_tombstone`,
  // both of which carry a `branch_isolation` policy from Task 5's migration 0068 — Form A and Form B. One
  // company-wide grant (no `branchIds`) is what keeps this suite's actor able to see its own rows after
  // that file lands; without it every `toHaveLength(1)` here becomes `0` and the failure looks like a
  // trigger fault rather than a policy. Amendment A15 states the rule; this is its consumer side.
  await grantCodes(db, a);
}, 180_000);
afterAll(async () => { await db.stop(); });

describe("app.pos_cashier — the till credential (§4.6.3; spec:1324, 2097)", () => {
  it("migration 65 is applied; the PK is principal_id and there is no id column (§3.5.3)", async () => {
    expect(await listApplied(db.migrator)).toContain(65);
    const cols = await db.admin<{ column_name: string }[]>`
      select column_name from information_schema.columns
       where table_schema = 'app' and table_name = 'pos_cashier' order by column_name`;
    expect(cols.map((c) => c.column_name)).toEqual([
      "branch_id", "company_id", "created_at", "employee_id", "pin_hash", "pin_updated_at",
      "principal_id", "revision", "revoked_at", "snapshot", "snapshot_at", "tenant_id",
    ]);
    const [pk] = await db.admin<{ def: string }[]>`
      select pg_get_constraintdef(oid) as def from pg_constraint
       where conrelid = 'app.pos_cashier'::regclass and contype = 'p'`;
    expect(pk!.def).toBe("PRIMARY KEY (principal_id)");
  });

  it("is audit-exempt — an audited copy of a PIN hash is a second copy of a credential", async () => {
    expect(AUDIT_EXEMPT.has("pos_cashier")).toBe(true);
    const triggers = await db.admin<{ tgname: string }[]>`
      select t.tgname from pg_trigger t join pg_class c on c.oid = t.tgrelid
       where not t.tgisinternal and c.relname = 'pos_cashier'`;
    // Two triggers at 0065. **Task 7 adds a third, `snapshot_revision_stamp`, in 0067 and updates this
    // line in the same commit** (T7 Step 6 re-runs this suite for exactly that reason).
    expect(triggers.map((t) => t.tgname).sort()).toEqual(["catalogue_revision", "pos_cashier_tombstone"]);
  });

  it("seedCashier stores a verifiable Argon2id hash and never the PIN", async () => {
    const e = await seedEmployee(db, a, { employeeNumber: "E-C1" });
    const principalId = await seedCashier(db, a, { employeeId: e, branchId: a.hq, pin: "4321" });
    const [row] = await withTenant(db.rw, a.tenant, a.human, (tx) => tx<
      { pin_hash: string; revision: string; revoked_at: string | null }[]
    >`select pin_hash, revision::text as revision, revoked_at from app.pos_cashier where principal_id = ${principalId}`);
    expect(row!.pin_hash).not.toContain("4321");
    expect(await verifyPin("4321", row!.pin_hash)).toBe(true);
    expect(await verifyPin("1111", row!.pin_hash)).toBe(false);
    expect(BigInt(row!.revision)).toBeGreaterThan(0n);      // the catalogue_revision trigger fired (§4.10.2)
    expect(row!.revoked_at).toBeNull();
  });

  it("one cashier row per principal, and the employee must be in the same tenant", async () => {
    const e = await seedEmployee(db, a, { employeeNumber: "E-C2" });
    const p = await seedCashier(db, a, { employeeId: e, branchId: a.hq });
    await expect(withTenant(db.rw, a.tenant, a.human, (tx) => tx`

      values (${p}, ${a.tenant}, ${a.company}, ${e}, ${a.hq}, 'x')`)).rejects.toThrow(/duplicate key/);

      await tx`insert into app.membership (tenant_id, principal_id) values (${a.tenant}, ${p2})`;
```
