`seedCashier` mints its own principal today, so its `principalId?: string` option is added additively **in this step** and recorded on §4.17's `seedCashier` row in the same PR — amendment **A12** carries both that option and `seedEmployee`'s `hiredOn?`. **The amended body, written out — this is the whole of the change to Task 2 Step 8's helper**, and nothing else in it moves:

```ts
export async function seedCashier(
  db: Db,
  s: Seed,
  opts: { employeeId: string; branchId: string; pin?: string; permissions?: readonly string[];
          principalId?: string },     // ← the one added option (A12); everything below is Task 2's
): Promise<string> {
  const principalId = opts.principalId ?? (await uuidv7(db.admin));
  const phc = await hashPin(opts.pin ?? "1234");
  // The mint is SKIPPED when the caller already has a principal — `posFixture`'s cashier is created by 6A
  // and already has its `app.principal` and `app.membership` rows; minting a second `auth_subject` for the
  // same person would abort on `principal_auth_subject_key` and, if it did not, would give one cashier two
  // identities. The guard is on the mint alone: the `pos_cashier` row is written either way.
  if (!opts.principalId) {
    await withTenant(db.migrator, s.tenant, s.system, async (tx) => {
      await tx`insert into app.principal (id, kind, auth_subject, display_name)
               values (${principalId}, 'human', ${"sub-cashier-" + principalId}, 'Cashier')`;
      await tx`insert into app.membership (tenant_id, principal_id) values (${s.tenant}, ${principalId})`;
    });
  }
  await withTenant(db.migrator, s.tenant, s.system, (tx) => tx`
    insert into app.pos_cashier (principal_id, tenant_id, company_id, employee_id, branch_id, pin_hash)
    values (${principalId}, ${s.tenant}, ${s.company}, ${opts.employeeId}, ${opts.branchId}, ${phc})`);
  if (opts.permissions && opts.permissions.length > 0) {
    await grantCodes(db, s, { codes: opts.permissions, principalId, roleCode: `cashier-${principalId.slice(-8)}`, branchIds: [opts.branchId] });
  }
  await withTenant(db.migrator, s.tenant, s.system, (tx) => tx`select app.refresh_pos_cashier(${principalId})`);
  return principalId;
}
```

Re-run `packages/db/test/staff/pos-cashier.test.ts` in this same commit: every existing call omits `principalId` and takes the mint branch, so the suite is unchanged and green — which is what proves the option is additive.

### Task 5: Migration 0068 — `actor_in_branch`, `actor_in_warehouse`, the five `branch_isolation` forms, and the linter rule

**The whole of §4.5.6, in one file.** Phases 1–5 have relied on `app.actor_has(code, company, branch)` in handlers and triggers (F-18); this task adds the row-level half, so *"a `branch_manager` at A cannot reach B's rows"* stops depending on a handler remembering a filter (spec:2166, LEGACY-042, LEGACY-045). Five policy forms, no sixth, and one exemption whose reason is a Postgres error message.

**Files:**
- Create: `packages/db/migrations/0068_create_branch_isolation_policies.sql`, `packages/db/migrations/0068.down.sql`, `packages/db/test/staff/branch-isolation.test.ts`, `docs/adr/0062-branch-isolation-five-forms.md`
- Modify: `packages/db/src/lint-schema.ts` (the `branch_isolation_policy` rule and `BRANCH_ISOLATION_EXEMPT`)

**Interfaces**

- Consumes: `app.current_actor`, `app.current_tenant`, `app.role_assignment`, `app.role_assignment_branch`, `app.membership`, `app.principal`, `app.warehouse` (3A), `app.price_list`, `app.promotion` (3A), `app.inventory_movement`, `app.stock_on_hand` (3A), `app.cost_layer`, `app.cost_consumption`, `app.stock_valuation` (3B), `app.terminal_enrolment`, `app.needs_attention`, `app.catalogue_tombstone` (6A), `app.item`, `app.lot` (3A), `lintSchema`, `Violation`, `listApplied`, `startDb`, `withTenant` (test), `uuidv7`, `seedTenant`, `seedBranch` (0D), `grantCodes` (2B), `seedWarehouse`, `seedItem` (3A), `seedEmployee` (T1), `seedCashier` (T2), `seedBranchPosPolicy` (T3).
- Produces:
  ```sql

  app.actor_in_warehouse(p_company_id uuid, p_warehouse_id uuid) returns boolean    -- §4.5.6 verbatim

  --                                     attendance_event, terminal_enrolment, needs_attention

  --   Form B  (branch_id NULLABLE, 3):  price_list, promotion, catalogue_tombstone
  --   Form C  (through warehouse, 5):   inventory_movement, stock_on_hand, cost_layer,
  ```

- [ ] **Step 1: Read the three 6A declarations Task 0's first informational probe printed**

```bash
grep -nE "branch_id" packages/db/migrations/0059_*.sql packages/db/migrations/0060_*.sql
```

The rule (amendment A1): **`branch_id ... not null` → Form A; a nullable `branch_id` → Form B.** This plan assumes `terminal_enrolment.branch_id` and `needs_attention.branch_id` are NOT NULL (a terminal and a rejected ticket both belong to exactly one branch) and `catalogue_tombstone.branch_id` is nullable (its `item`, `tax_code` and `customer` streams have no branch at all, so a company-wide tombstone must carry NULL and must stay visible to every branch — Form B's whole reason). **Write down what the grep actually said before editing the file below.** Getting this backwards is not a silent failure: Form A on a nullable column hides every company-wide tombstone from every terminal, and the till stops learning about deleted items — which Step 3's Form B case is written to catch.

- [ ] **Step 2: Write ADR 0062 as a proposal (spec:95 — this changes what every read of thirteen tables returns)**

`docs/adr/0062-branch-isolation-five-forms.md`:

```markdown
# ADR 0062 — Branch isolation is a RESTRICTIVE row policy in five fixed forms, and `role_assignment_branch` is the one exemption

- Status: **proposed** (spec:95 — this touches security and the data model)
- Date: 2026-09-05
- Slice: 6C

## Context

spec:2166 requires a RESTRICTIVE `branch_isolation` policy on "every table with a `branch_id` column",
attached from Phase 6 when branch-scoped roles first exist. Tables reach their branch four different
ways, and one table must not carry the policy at all.

## Decision

Two `stable` SQL functions and **five** policy forms, all named `branch_isolation`, all `AS RESTRICTIVE`,
differing only in the `using` expression (§4.5.6). Form A: a NOT NULL `branch_id`. Form A″: `app.warehouse`
— Form A plus `kind = 'in_transit' or …`. Form A′: `app.employee`, whose key is `home_branch_id`. Form B:
a NULLABLE `branch_id`, where `branch_id is null or …` because NULL means *every branch*. Form C: the five
stock tables, reached through `app.actor_in_warehouse`.

**`app.role_assignment_branch` is exempt, and the exemption is closed at one name.** `app.actor_in_branch`
selects **from** `role_assignment_branch`; a restrictive policy on that table whose predicate calls
`actor_in_branch` is self-referential, and PostgreSQL aborts every read of it with *"infinite recursion
detected in policy for relation role_assignment_branch"* — not only the policy's own read, but the
authorisation predicate of the entire system, including `app.actor_has`. The table is the branch **grant**,
not branch-scoped **data**: it is already tenant-isolated, its rows are already unreadable across tenants,
and a principal reading their own grants is the mechanism by which scope is *narrowed*, never widened.

## Consequences

- `document_line` is deliberately absent and never gains a `branch_id` (§4.3.4): it is isolated through
  its parent's composite FK. Naming it here would abort the migration with `column "branch_id" does not exist`.
- The in-transit exception lives on `app.warehouse`'s own policy and **not** inside `actor_in_warehouse`,
  because the function's `select` is itself subject to that policy: an exception inside the function would
  test `kind` on a row RLS had already filtered away, and every two-step transfer receipt would fail.
- `SECURITY DEFINER` is not used anywhere here. It would bypass `tenant_isolation` on `warehouse` as well,
  turning a branch hole into a tenancy hole.
- The system principal passes every form (jobs must still run), and a principal with no
  `role_assignment_branch` rows is company-wide by construction — exactly `app.actor_has`'s NULL-branch rule.
- A new table with a branch key now fails the build until it carries a form (§3.5.1 step 7, the
  `branch_isolation_policy` rule). That is the point: the next slice cannot forget.
```

```ts
import { afterAll, beforeAll, describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { startDb, uuidv7, withTenant, type Db } from "../harness";
import { seedTenant, seedBranch, seedEmployee, seedCashier, seedBranchPosPolicy, seedWarehouse,
         seedItem, grantCodes, type Seed } from "../fixtures";
import { lintSchema, listApplied } from "../../src";

let db: Db;
let s: Seed;
let branchB: string;
let managerA: string;     // a branch_manager restricted to s.hq
let managerB: string;     // a branch_manager restricted to branchB
let itemId: string;       // 3A's item the Form C case puts stock against — `Seed` carries none
let lotId: string;        // that item's sentinel 'NONE' lot; stock_on_hand's PK contains lot_id

/** Restrict a fresh principal to one branch through 2B's grantCodes, and return its id. */
async function branchPrincipal(branchId: string, label: string): Promise<string> {
  const id = await uuidv7(db.admin);
  await withTenant(db.migrator, s.tenant, s.system, async (tx) => {
    await tx`insert into app.principal (id, kind, auth_subject, display_name) values (${id}, 'human', ${"sub-" + id}, ${label})`;
    await tx`insert into app.membership (tenant_id, principal_id) values (${s.tenant}, ${id})`;
  });
  await grantCodes(db, s, { codes: ["inventory.stock.read", "hr.employee.read"], principalId: id, roleCode: `mgr-${label}`, branchIds: [branchId] });
  return id;
}

beforeAll(async () => {
  db = await startDb();
  s = await seedTenant(db, "iso");
  // `s.human` is this file's writer for the two helpers 6C may NOT change — 3A's `seedWarehouse` and the
  // Form B `price_list` inserts below — and after this task's own migration both tables carry a
  // `branch_isolation` policy. One company-wide grant (no `branchIds`) is amendment A15's stated remedy for
  // exactly that case: it gives `s.human` a live `app.role_assignment` with no `role_assignment_branch`
  // rows, which `app.actor_in_branch` treats as company-wide authority. Without it this file's `beforeAll`
  // dies at the first `seedWarehouse` with `new row violates row-level security policy for table
  // "warehouse"` and every case below reports as an error rather than a failure.
  await grantCodes(db, s);
  branchB = await seedBranch(db, s, { code: "BR-B" });
  managerA = await branchPrincipal(s.hq, "mgr-a");
  managerB = await branchPrincipal(branchB, "mgr-b");
  // The Form C case needs an item and a lot, and `Seed` carries neither — it is
  // `{ tenant; system; human; company; period; closedPeriod; lockedPeriod; cash; sales; docType; policy;
  //    branchType; hq }` and nothing else (§4.1). 3A creates a sentinel lot coded 'NONE' with every item,
  // which is the lot every non-lot-tracked stock row uses; `stock_on_hand`'s primary key contains
  // `lot_id`, so a NULL there fails.
  const item = await seedItem(db, s, { code: "ISO-A", trackLots: false });
  itemId = item.itemId;
  const [lot] = await withTenant(db.rw, s.tenant, s.human, (tx) =>
    tx<{ id: string }[]>`select id from app.lot where item_id = ${itemId} and code = 'NONE'`);
  lotId = lot!.id;
}, 180_000);
afterAll(async () => { await db.stop(); });

describe("branch_isolation (§4.5.6; spec:2166; LEGACY-042, 045)", () => {
  it("migration 68 is applied and every policy is RESTRICTIVE (a permissive gate is OR-ed away)", async () => {
    expect(await listApplied(db.migrator)).toContain(68);
    const rows = await db.admin<{ tablename: string; permissive: string }[]>`
      select tablename, permissive from pg_policies where schemaname = 'app' and policyname = 'branch_isolation' order by 1`;
    expect(rows.every((r) => r.permissive === "RESTRICTIVE")).toBe(true);
    expect(rows.map((r) => r.tablename)).toEqual([
      "attendance_event", "branch_pos_policy", "catalogue_tombstone", "cost_consumption", "cost_layer",
      "document", "employee", "inventory_movement", "needs_attention", "pos_cashier", "pos_terminal",
      "price_list", "promotion", "stock_on_hand", "stock_valuation", "terminal_enrolment", "warehouse",
    ]);
  });

  it("role_assignment_branch carries NO policy — the predicate reads it, and a policy would recurse", async () => {
    const rows = await db.admin<{ policyname: string }[]>`
      select policyname from pg_policies where schemaname = 'app' and tablename = 'role_assignment_branch' and policyname = 'branch_isolation'`;
    expect(rows).toHaveLength(0);
    // and the predicate still answers for a branch-restricted principal — the recursion proof, positively stated
    const [ok] = await withTenant(db.rw, s.tenant, managerA, (tx) =>
      tx<{ ok: boolean }[]>`select app.actor_in_branch(${s.company}, ${s.hq}) as ok`);
    expect(ok!.ok).toBe(true);
  });

  it("Form A — a branch_manager at A sees A's cashiers, policies and attendance and not B's", async () => {
    const eA = await seedEmployee(db, s, { employeeNumber: "E-A", homeBranchId: s.hq });
    const eB = await seedEmployee(db, s, { employeeNumber: "E-B", homeBranchId: branchB });
    const cA = await seedCashier(db, s, { employeeId: eA, branchId: s.hq });
    const cB = await seedCashier(db, s, { employeeId: eB, branchId: branchB });
    await seedBranchPosPolicy(db, s, { branchId: s.hq });
    await seedBranchPosPolicy(db, s, { branchId: branchB });

    const seen = await withTenant(db.rw, s.tenant, managerA, (tx) =>
      tx<{ principal_id: string }[]>`select principal_id from app.pos_cashier order by principal_id`);
    expect(seen.map((r) => r.principal_id)).toEqual([cA, cB].filter((x) => x === cA));

    const policies = await withTenant(db.rw, s.tenant, managerA, (tx) =>
      tx<{ branch_id: string }[]>`select branch_id from app.branch_pos_policy`);
    expect(policies.map((r) => r.branch_id)).toEqual([s.hq]);
  });

  it("Form A′ — employee is keyed on home_branch_id, not branch_id", async () => {
    const rows = await withTenant(db.rw, s.tenant, managerB, (tx) =>
      tx<{ employee_number: string }[]>`select employee_number from app.employee order by employee_number`);
    expect(rows.map((r) => r.employee_number)).toEqual(["E-B"]);
  });

  it("Form A″ — an in-transit warehouse is visible to EVERY branch, a stock warehouse only to its own", async () => {
    const whA = await seedWarehouse(db, s, { code: "WH-A", branchId: s.hq });
    const whB = await seedWarehouse(db, s, { code: "WH-B", branchId: branchB });
    const transit = await seedWarehouse(db, s, { code: "WH-T", kind: "in_transit", branchId: s.hq });
    const seen = await withTenant(db.rw, s.tenant, managerB, (tx) =>
      tx<{ code: string }[]>`select code from app.warehouse order by code`);
    expect(seen.map((r) => r.code)).toEqual(["WH-B", "WH-T"]);
    expect([whA, whB, transit]).toHaveLength(3);
  });

  it("Form B — a NULL branch means EVERY branch, and Form A would have hidden it", async () => {
    await withTenant(db.rw, s.tenant, s.human, async (tx) => {
      await tx`insert into app.price_list (tenant_id, company_id, code, name_en, currency, branch_id)
               values (${s.tenant}, ${s.company}, 'PL-ALL', 'Company wide', 'MYR', null)`;
      await tx`insert into app.price_list (tenant_id, company_id, code, name_en, currency, branch_id)
               values (${s.tenant}, ${s.company}, 'PL-B', 'Branch B only', 'MYR', ${branchB})`;
    });
    const seen = await withTenant(db.rw, s.tenant, managerA, (tx) =>
      tx<{ code: string }[]>`select code from app.price_list order by code`);
    expect(seen.map((r) => r.code)).toEqual(["PL-ALL"]);
  });

  it("Form C — stock is isolated through its warehouse, which is the only branch key it has", async () => {
    const whB = await seedWarehouse(db, s, { code: "WH-C", branchId: branchB });
    // EXPLICIT ids, never `select … from app.item join app.lot … limit 1`. `seedTenant` creates no item
    // and no lot, so that select matched nothing, the insert was a no-op, `asB` came back empty and
    // `expect(asA).toHaveLength(0)` passed VACUOUSLY — which is the branch leak this case exists to find.
    // `beforeAll` seeds the item through 3A's own helper and reads its sentinel lot for that reason.
    await withTenant(db.rw, s.tenant, s.system, (tx) => tx`
      insert into app.stock_on_hand (tenant_id, company_id, item_id, warehouse_id, lot_id, quantity)
      values (${s.tenant}, ${s.company}, ${itemId}, ${whB}, ${lotId}, 5.000000)`);
    const asB = await withTenant(db.rw, s.tenant, managerB, (tx) =>
      tx`select warehouse_id from app.stock_on_hand where warehouse_id = ${whB}`);
    expect(asB).toHaveLength(1);        // the row EXISTS — asserted first, so the next line cannot be vacuous
    const asA = await withTenant(db.rw, s.tenant, managerA, (tx) =>
      tx`select warehouse_id from app.stock_on_hand where warehouse_id = ${whB}`);
    expect(asA).toHaveLength(0);
  });

  it("the system principal passes every form, so nightly jobs still run (R8, spec:2179)", async () => {
    const rows = await withTenant(db.rw, s.tenant, s.system, (tx) =>
      tx<{ n: number }[]>`select count(*)::int as n from app.employee`);
    expect(rows[0]!.n).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(2);
  });

  it("a company-wide principal (no role_assignment_branch rows) sees every branch", async () => {
    const wide = await uuidv7(db.admin);
    await withTenant(db.migrator, s.tenant, s.system, async (tx) => {
      await tx`insert into app.principal (id, kind, auth_subject, display_name) values (${wide}, 'human', ${"sub-" + wide}, 'wide')`;
      await tx`insert into app.membership (tenant_id, principal_id) values (${s.tenant}, ${wide})`;
    });
    await grantCodes(db, s, { codes: ["hr.employee.read"], principalId: wide, roleCode: "wide-role" });   // no branchIds
    const rows = await withTenant(db.rw, s.tenant, wide, (tx) => tx`select id from app.employee`);
    expect(rows.length).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(2);
  });

  it("a write into another branch is refused by the policy's WITH CHECK, not only hidden on read", async () => {
    await expect(withTenant(db.rw, s.tenant, managerA, (tx) => tx`
      insert into app.attendance_event (tenant_id, company_id, employee_id, branch_id, kind, occurred_at)
      select ${s.tenant}, ${s.company}, e.id, ${branchB}, 'clock_in', now()
        from app.employee e where e.employee_number = 'E-A'`)).rejects.toThrow(/row-level security|42501/);
  });

  it("the schema linter reports zero violations, and the new rule fires when a policy is missing", async () => {
    expect(await lintSchema(db.admin)).toEqual([]);
    await db.admin`drop policy branch_isolation on app.attendance_event`;
    const v = await lintSchema(db.admin);
    expect(v).toContainEqual({
      table: "attendance_event", rule: "branch_isolation_policy",
      detail: "has a branch key (branch_id) but no policy named branch_isolation (spec:2166)",
    });
    await db.admin`create policy branch_isolation on app.attendance_event as restrictive using (app.actor_in_branch(company_id, branch_id))`;
    expect(await lintSchema(db.admin)).toEqual([]);
  });

  it("the rule also fires when the policy exists but is PERMISSIVE — an OR-ed gate is no gate", async () => {
    await db.admin`drop policy branch_isolation on app.branch_pos_policy`;
    await db.admin`create policy branch_isolation on app.branch_pos_policy using (app.actor_in_branch(company_id, branch_id))`;
    const v = await lintSchema(db.admin);
    expect(v.some((x) => x.table === "branch_pos_policy" && x.rule === "branch_isolation_policy" && x.detail.includes("PERMISSIVE"))).toBe(true);
    await db.admin`drop policy branch_isolation on app.branch_pos_policy`;
    await db.admin`create policy branch_isolation on app.branch_pos_policy as restrictive using (app.actor_in_branch(company_id, branch_id))`;
    expect(await lintSchema(db.admin)).toEqual([]);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write `0068_create_branch_isolation_policies.sql`**

```sql
-- 0068_create_branch_isolation_policies.sql — §4.5.6 in full; spec:1566–1569, 2166; LEGACY-042, LEGACY-045.
-- Phases 1-5 relied on app.actor_has(code, company, branch) in handlers and triggers. This file adds the
-- ROW-level half, so a missed handler check cannot leak another branch's rows. Five forms, no sixth.

-- ── 1. the two predicates ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
-- The system principal passes (jobs must run, R8 spec:2179); a principal with no role_assignment_branch
-- rows is company-wide by construction, which is app.actor_has's own NULL-branch rule (spec:2166).
create function app.actor_in_branch(p_company_id uuid, p_branch_id uuid) returns boolean language sql stable as $$
  select exists (select 1 from app.principal p join app.membership m on m.principal_id = p.id
                  where p.id = app.current_actor() and p.kind = 'system' and m.tenant_id = app.current_tenant())
      or exists (select 1 from app.role_assignment ra
                  where ra.principal_id = app.current_actor() and ra.company_id = p_company_id and (ra.expires_at is null or ra.expires_at > now())
                    and (not exists (select 1 from app.role_assignment_branch b where b.principal_id = ra.principal_id and b.role_id = ra.role_id and b.company_id = ra.company_id)
                         or exists (select 1 from app.role_assignment_branch b where b.principal_id = ra.principal_id and b.role_id = ra.role_id and b.company_id = ra.company_id and b.branch_id = p_branch_id)))
$$;

-- Stock lives in warehouses, not branches: the stock tables carry warehouse_id and no branch column. This
-- resolves the warehouse's branch. It is a PLAIN lookup — the in-transit exception lives on app.warehouse's
-- OWN policy (Form A" below), because this select is itself subject to that policy and an exception written
-- here would never be reached.
create function app.actor_in_warehouse(p_company_id uuid, p_warehouse_id uuid) returns boolean language sql stable as $$
  select exists (select 1 from app.warehouse w
                  where w.id = p_warehouse_id and app.actor_in_branch(w.company_id, w.branch_id))
$$;

-- app.role_assignment_branch carries NO branch_isolation policy, and this is not an oversight: the
-- predicate above SELECTS from it, so a restrictive policy calling actor_in_branch on that table is
-- self-referential and PostgreSQL aborts every read of it -- and therefore every authorisation decision
-- in the system -- with "infinite recursion detected in policy for relation role_assignment_branch".
-- The table is the branch GRANT, not branch-scoped data; it is already tenant-isolated (ADR 0062).

-- ── 2. FORM A — a NOT NULL branch key ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
-- document, pos_terminal (0001); pos_cashier (0065), branch_pos_policy (0066), attendance_event (0067);
-- terminal_enrolment and needs_attention (6A, 0060 — created BEFORE this file, so §3.5.1 step 7 could not
-- attach their form and this file must). 6D adds shift and till_event in 0070.
create policy branch_isolation on app.document            as restrictive using (app.actor_in_branch(company_id, branch_id));
create policy branch_isolation on app.pos_terminal        as restrictive using (app.actor_in_branch(company_id, branch_id));
create policy branch_isolation on app.pos_cashier         as restrictive using (app.actor_in_branch(company_id, branch_id));
create policy branch_isolation on app.branch_pos_policy   as restrictive using (app.actor_in_branch(company_id, branch_id));
create policy branch_isolation on app.attendance_event    as restrictive using (app.actor_in_branch(company_id, branch_id));
create policy branch_isolation on app.terminal_enrolment  as restrictive using (app.actor_in_branch(company_id, branch_id));
create policy branch_isolation on app.needs_attention     as restrictive using (app.actor_in_branch(company_id, branch_id));

-- ── 3. FORM A" — app.warehouse: Form A PLUS the in-transit exception ────────────────────────────────
-- An in-transit warehouse has a NOT NULL branch_id like any other, so under plain Form A it is visible only
-- to its own branch. But a two-step transfer IS a dispatch from branch A into in-transit and a receipt by
-- branch B (spec:2160 gives branch_manager "receiving transfers"), and actor_in_warehouse reads this table
-- -- so if the in-transit row is hidden from B, B's `exists` returns false and every movement, layer and
-- stock_on_hand row against it is refused. The exception belongs HERE, on the warehouse row.
create policy branch_isolation on app.warehouse as restrictive
  using (kind = 'in_transit' or app.actor_in_branch(company_id, branch_id));

-- ── 4. FORM A′ — app.employee, whose branch key is named home_branch_id ─────────────────────────────
-- 0064 attaches this itself when it runs AFTER 0068 (the 8A-first order, §4.6.2's do-block), so this
-- statement is guarded in return. Exactly one of the two guards fires, whichever order the two files ran in.
do $$ begin
  if not exists (select 1 from pg_policies where schemaname = 'app' and tablename = 'employee' and policyname = 'branch_isolation') then
    execute 'create policy branch_isolation on app.employee as restrictive using (app.actor_in_branch(company_id, home_branch_id))';
  end if;
end $$;

-- ── 5. FORM B — a NULLABLE branch key, where NULL means "every branch" ──────────────────────────────
-- price_list and promotion (3A); catalogue_tombstone (6A — its item, tax_code and customer streams have no
-- branch at all, so a company-wide tombstone carries NULL and must stay visible to every terminal, or the
-- till never learns that an item was deleted). 8D's public_holiday takes this form in 0096.
-- NULL must be VISIBLE, not denied: app.actor_in_branch(company_id, null) is false for every
-- branch-restricted principal, so Form A here would hide the head-office price list and every company-wide
-- promotion from exactly the principals that need them.
create policy branch_isolation on app.price_list          as restrictive using (branch_id is null or app.actor_in_branch(company_id, branch_id));
create policy branch_isolation on app.promotion           as restrictive using (branch_id is null or app.actor_in_branch(company_id, branch_id));
create policy branch_isolation on app.catalogue_tombstone as restrictive using (branch_id is null or app.actor_in_branch(company_id, branch_id));

-- ── 6. FORM C — stock, keyed by warehouse ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
-- None of these carries a branch column; warehouse.branch_id is the only branch key in the inventory model,
-- which is why test 36's stock clause cannot be met any other way. 3A and 3B created them branch-ready
-- (warehouse_id NOT NULL) and attached nothing.
create policy branch_isolation on app.inventory_movement  as restrictive using (app.actor_in_warehouse(company_id, warehouse_id));
create policy branch_isolation on app.stock_on_hand       as restrictive using (app.actor_in_warehouse(company_id, warehouse_id));
create policy branch_isolation on app.cost_layer          as restrictive using (app.actor_in_warehouse(company_id, warehouse_id));
create policy branch_isolation on app.stock_valuation     as restrictive using (app.actor_in_warehouse(company_id, warehouse_id));
-- cost_consumption has no warehouse_id; it reaches one through its movement.
create policy branch_isolation on app.cost_consumption as restrictive
  using (exists (select 1 from app.inventory_movement m where m.id = movement_id and app.actor_in_warehouse(m.company_id, m.warehouse_id)));

-- app.document_line is DELIBERATELY absent. It has no branch_id and never gains one (§4.3.4); it is isolated
-- transitively through its composite FK to a document the reader can already see. A warehouse_id alone does
-- not attract the policy -- document_line has one. Naming it here would abort this file with
-- 'column "branch_id" does not exist'.
```

`packages/db/migrations/0068.down.sql`:

```sql
-- Reverse dependency order: the policies first (they call the functions), then the functions.
drop policy if exists branch_isolation on app.cost_consumption;
drop policy if exists branch_isolation on app.stock_valuation;
drop policy if exists branch_isolation on app.cost_layer;
drop policy if exists branch_isolation on app.stock_on_hand;
drop policy if exists branch_isolation on app.inventory_movement;
drop policy if exists branch_isolation on app.catalogue_tombstone;
drop policy if exists branch_isolation on app.promotion;
drop policy if exists branch_isolation on app.price_list;
drop policy if exists branch_isolation on app.employee;
drop policy if exists branch_isolation on app.warehouse;
drop policy if exists branch_isolation on app.needs_attention;
drop policy if exists branch_isolation on app.terminal_enrolment;
drop policy if exists branch_isolation on app.attendance_event;
drop policy if exists branch_isolation on app.branch_pos_policy;
drop policy if exists branch_isolation on app.pos_cashier;
drop policy if exists branch_isolation on app.pos_terminal;
drop policy if exists branch_isolation on app.document;
drop function if exists app.actor_in_warehouse(uuid, uuid);
drop function if exists app.actor_in_branch(uuid, uuid);
```

**`drop policy if exists branch_isolation on app.employee` is in the down even though the up may not have created it.** Under the 8A-first order 0064 created it; under 6C-first this file did. `if exists` makes both orders reversible, and §3.4.6's up → down → up fingerprint test is what proves it — the test that would otherwise fail is the one 0B generalised.

- [ ] **Step 5: Extend `packages/db/src/lint-schema.ts` with the `branch_isolation_policy` rule**

Add the exemption set beside the others:

```ts
/**
 * The ONE table that carries a branch key and must NOT carry the policy: `app.actor_in_branch` selects
 * from `role_assignment_branch`, so a restrictive policy on it calling that function is self-referential
 * and PostgreSQL aborts every read with "infinite recursion detected in policy". It is the branch GRANT,
 * not branch-scoped data (ADR 0062). The set is closed — a second exemption amends contracts §3.5.2 first.
 */
export const BRANCH_ISOLATION_EXEMPT = new Set(["role_assignment_branch"]);

/**
 * Stock tables have no branch column at all; `warehouse.branch_id` is the only branch key in the inventory
 * model, so they are listed explicitly rather than keyed on `warehouse_id` — `document_line` also has a
 * `warehouse_id` and must NOT carry the policy (§4.5.6).
 */
const BRANCH_ISOLATED_BY_WAREHOUSE = new Set([
  "inventory_movement", "stock_on_hand", "cost_layer", "cost_consumption", "stock_valuation",
]);
```

and the rule itself, inside the `for (const t of tables)` loop, after the `extra_permissive_policy` block:

```ts
    const branchKey = cols.has("branch_id") ? "branch_id" : cols.has("home_branch_id") ? "home_branch_id" : null;
    const needsBranchIsolation = !BRANCH_ISOLATION_EXEMPT.has(name)
      && (branchKey !== null || BRANCH_ISOLATED_BY_WAREHOUSE.has(name));
    if (needsBranchIsolation) {
      const bi = policies.find((p) => p.tablename === name && p.policyname === "branch_isolation");
      const via = branchKey ?? "warehouse_id → warehouse.branch_id";
      if (!bi) {
        v.push({ table: name, rule: "branch_isolation_policy",
                 detail: `has a branch key (${via}) but no policy named branch_isolation (spec:2166)` });
      } else if (bi.permissive === "PERMISSIVE") {
        v.push({ table: name, rule: "branch_isolation_policy",
                 detail: `branch_isolation is PERMISSIVE — permissive policies OR together and the gate is voided by tenant_isolation (LEGACY-033)` });
      }
    }
```

**The rule is written to fire before 0068 has run, and that is intentional but must not break CI.** Every table it names either receives the policy in this same migration or is created after it. A slice that adds a branch-keyed table between 0068 and its own policy statement sees a red build inside one PR, which is exactly §3.5.1 step 7 becoming mechanical.

```bash

bun run --cwd packages/db db:lint
cd packages/db && bun run test -- test/staff/branch-isolation.test.ts
```

Expected: `applied 68`, `0 violations`, 12 passing.

- [ ] **Step 7: Run the WHOLE repository suite and fix the fallout — this file changes what every existing test can see**

```bash
bun run test          # repository root: packages/db, packages/contracts, apps/api, apps/worker, tests
```

**This is not a formality and it is the step most likely to find work.** Until this migration, `s.human` — a `Seed`'s human principal, which `seedTenant` grants **nothing** (§4.1, pinned) — could read and write every table in the database that its tenant owned. From this file onward, seventeen of them answer only to a principal that passes `app.actor_in_branch`, and a policy declared with `using` and no `with check` is applied **as** the `with check` on INSERT, so both halves move at once. Two failure shapes appear and they look nothing alike:

- **`new row violates row-level security policy for table "<table>"`** in a `beforeAll` or a fixture call — a write.
- **an assertion that expected rows and got none**, with no error at all — a read. This one is the dangerous one, because a suite that asserted `toHaveLength(0)` for an unrelated reason now passes for the wrong reason.

**The remedy is one of exactly two, and never a third.**

1. **A `fixtures.ts` helper this slice owns** — `seedEmployee`, `seedCashier`, `seedBranchPosPolicy` — inserts under `withTenant(db.migrator, s.tenant, s.system, …)`. All three already do, from Tasks 1, 2 and 3, under amendment **A15**; if one does not, that is the fix.
2. **Any other suite** calls **`await grantCodes(db, s)`** in its `beforeAll`, with no `codes` and no `branchIds`. That inserts one `app.role_assignment` and no `app.role_assignment_branch` rows, which `app.actor_in_branch` reads as company-wide authority — the same rule `app.actor_has` already applies to a NULL branch. It grants no permission code, so it cannot make an RBAC assertion pass by accident.

**Never widen a policy, never move a table out of 0068, and never change a helper's signature or return.** A `branch_isolation` policy that a test can talk its way past is LEGACY-045 shipped under a new name.

**The helpers this slice may NOT change, and what to do instead.** Plan 0A's **`draftDocument`** writes `app.document` as `db.rw`/`s.human` (plan0A:1605–1610) and 0A is frozen (§4.1); 3A's **`seedWarehouse`** and **`seedPriceList`** write `app.warehouse` and `app.price_list` the same way and are 3A's rows. Every suite that calls one of those against a branch-keyed table takes remedy 2. Two facts limit how much of that there is: 6A's **`seedTerminal`** already inserts as `s.system` (6a:2611) and needs nothing, and 2B's `grantCodes` is already called "once per seed object" across 1A's, 1B's and 2A's posting suites, with a memoised grant inside `postJournal` covering the whole `tests/invariants` tier (§4.17's `grantCodes` row). **List the files this step touched in the PR body**, one line each with which remedy it took — that list is what 6D, 7C and 8A read before writing their own fixtures, and it is the evidence the ripple was assessed rather than assumed.

**If a suite outside this slice needs a change that is not remedy 2** — a fixture body, a policy, a helper signature — **stop and raise it with that slice's owner** (§4.18: the owning plan is the authority). Do not add a second spelling of a helper here.

```bash

```

The generalised `up → down → up` test walks every applied version. A failure here almost always means a `drop policy` is missing from `0068.down.sql` or is in the wrong order — `schemaFingerprint`'s policy query is what catches it.

The spec's role table (spec:2157–2163) is the source; §3.6.4 requires each introducing slice to record its grants in the same PR:

| Code | Roles | The sentence it comes from |
|---|---|---|
| `hr.employee.read` | `user`, `branch_manager`, `hr_payroll` | `user` — *"read accounts, items, stock and **the staff directory** (no sensitive fields)"* (spec:2159); `branch_manager` needs to see the employee behind a cashier before resetting a PIN; `hr_payroll` owns the master. |
| `hr.employee.manage` | `hr_payroll` | *"Employee master including sensitive data"* (spec:2162). |

| `pos.cashier.manage_pin` | `branch_manager` | *"**resetting a PIN for a cashier in the same branch**"* (spec:2160). "In the same branch" is not a second condition to code: `branch_isolation` on `pos_cashier` makes another branch's cashier invisible, which is a 404 (Task 10 Step 2's by-id case). |

**`auditor` receives none of the four.** Its row (spec:2163) lists the ledger, documents, reports, stock, costs, the payroll register, statutory schedules, the audit log and document events — not the staff directory. R7 would admit `hr.employee.read` (it is non-mutating), so this is a deliberate reading of the spec's list and not an oversight; a tenant that wants it clones the role under `admin.role.manage`.

```ts
//   hr_payroll:      + "hr.employee.read", "hr.employee.manage", "hr.attendance.record"
// `admin` is COMPUTED from PERMISSIONS and is never edited (§3.6.4).
```

```bash

bun run lint:rules            # role-seed + permission-catalogue (§3.6.3)
cd packages/db && bun run test -- test/permission-catalogue.test.ts
```

`role-seed` checks three things over the four new grants: no seeded role other than `admin` holds both codes of an `INCOMPATIBLE_PAIRS` entry (none of the four appears in a pair — `hr.employee.read_sensitive` is 8A's and is a different code); `auditor` holds no mutating code (it holds none of the four at all); and no role holds a code absent from the catalogue.

**`permission-catalogue` will fail until Tasks 3, 4 and 8 have landed their route modules**, because a catalogue code with no `PERMISSION[…]` reference in `apps/**` is a build failure by design (spec:2135). The Execution handoff therefore sequences **T6 before T8 but inside the same review batch as T3, T4 and T8**; if the four route modules are not merged together with this migration, the lint is red in the interval. That is the F-21 rule applied to a slice with four codes and four modules, and the mitigation is batching, never a suppression.

### Task 7: The cashier snapshot — `app.refresh_pos_cashier`, its triggers, and the `pos.cashier.compare` job

**A projection with exactly one writer and a nightly comparison** (spec:1324, D14, ADR 0061). The till evaluates authority offline from `pos_cashier.snapshot`; the server evaluates it from `app.principal_has` and `app.branch_pos_policy`. If those two ever disagree, a cashier is either doing something the server will refuse — an annoyance — or the till is authorising something the server would refuse and only the *sync* catches it, which is the LEGACY-027 shape. The comparison exists to find that, and it **reports rather than repairs**, because a silent repair hides the trigger fault.

**Files:**
- Modify: `packages/db/migrations/0067_create_attendance_event_and_cashier_snapshot.sql`, gaining sections **3, 3a, 4 and 5** (**no rename happens — Task 4 committed the file under this name from the start, which is Step 1's point**); `packages/db/migrations/0067.down.sql` (the matching drops); `packages/db/test/staff/pos-cashier.test.ts` (Task 2's trigger-list assertion gains `snapshot_revision_stamp`); `packages/db/test/fixtures.ts` (un-comment `seedCashier`'s refresh call); `packages/contracts/permissions.ts` (`CASHIER_SNAPSHOT_CODES`); `packages/db/src/jobs/payloads.ts` (one key); `packages/db/src/index.ts`; `apps/worker/src/register.ts`; `tests/invariants/probes.ts`
- Create: `packages/db/src/staff/cashier.ts`, `apps/worker/src/jobs/cashier-compare.ts`, `packages/db/test/staff/cashier-snapshot.test.ts`, `apps/worker/test/jobs/cashier-compare.test.ts`, `docs/adr/0061-cashier-snapshot-is-a-projection.md`

**Interfaces**

- Consumes: `app.principal_has` (2B), `app.pos_cashier` (T2), `app.branch_pos_policy` (T3), `app.permission`, `app.role_assignment`, `app.role_assignment_branch`, `app.role_permission`, `app.audit_log`, `PERMISSIONS`, `PermissionCode`, `JobHandler`, `registerHandler`, `JobEnvelope`, `systemContext`, `startWorker`, `grantCodes` (2B).
- Produces:
  ```sql
  app.refresh_pos_cashier(p_principal_id uuid) returns void   -- the ONE writer of snapshot's codes and limits
  app.stamp_snapshot_revision()                               -- trigger fn: the ONE writer of snapshot->'revision'
  app.refresh_pos_cashier_for_assignment()                    -- trigger fn: role_assignment, role_assignment_branch
  app.refresh_pos_cashier_for_role()                          -- trigger fn: role_permission
  app.refresh_pos_cashier_for_branch_policy()                 -- trigger fn: branch_pos_policy
  app.who_can_reset_a_pin                                     -- the one-query view of spec:2134 / test 36
  ```

  ```ts
    maxLineDiscountPct: string; maxTicketDiscountPct: string; maxVoidAmount: string;
    maxPriceOverridePct: string; blindReturnAllowed: boolean; maxBlindReturnAmount: string;
    roundingIncrement: string; requireCustomerOver: string | null; maxCashInDrawer: string | null;

  export async function computeSnapshot(tx: Sql, principalId: string): Promise<CashierSnapshot | null>;
  export async function findSnapshotDrift(tx: Sql, opts?: { companyId?: string }): Promise<SnapshotDrift[]>;
  export async function readSnapshot(tx: Sql, principalId: string): Promise<CashierSnapshot>;
  ```

- [ ] **Step 1: Understand why this task edits 0067 rather than adding a seventh migration**

6C's range is **0064–0069 and every number is already used** (§3.4.1: a slice writes only inside its range and never borrows). `app.refresh_pos_cashier`, its four triggers and the `who_can_reset_a_pin` view are DDL and must live in a migration file. They go into **0067**, whose name lengthens to `0067_create_attendance_event_and_cashier_snapshot.sql` under §3.4.2's round-2 rule — *a file whose name understates what it does is renamed, not left*; 2A's `0018_…` and 2B's `0023_…` are the recorded precedents.

**0067 has not shipped**, so this is a development-time edit of an unapplied migration and never an expand/contract case (§3.4.7 binds tables that hold production rows). If a development database has already applied it: `bun run --cwd packages/db db:rollback 66`, edit, re-apply. Tasks 4 and 7 are therefore **two commits on one migration file in one PR** — "one migration per PR" (spec:2057) binds the PR, not the commit count.

- [ ] **Step 2: Write ADR 0061 as a proposal**

`docs/adr/0061-cashier-snapshot-is-a-projection.md`:

```markdown
# ADR 0061 — The cashier snapshot is a projection with one writer and a nightly comparison

- Status: **proposed** (spec:95 — this touches security and the data model)
- Date: 2026-09-05
- Slice: 6C

## Context

spec:1324 requires the till to evaluate a cashier's authority with no network, from the permission codes
and limits that travel with the catalogue. It also says, in the same sentence, that this is "a
**projection**, regenerated by the catalogue-revision trigger whenever a `role_assignment`,
`role_assignment_branch` or `role_permission` row affecting that principal changes (and on termination),
compared nightly against `actor_has()` like every other projection (D14), never edited by hand."

Two things follow that a plan must state or a later reader will get wrong.

## Decision

1. **One writer.** `app.refresh_pos_cashier(p_principal_id uuid)` is the only statement in the system that
   writes `pos_cashier.snapshot`. It is called by AFTER triggers on `role_assignment`,
   `role_assignment_branch`, `role_permission` and `branch_pos_policy`, and by 8C's termination trigger. No
   route, job or handler writes the column; Task 10 asserts that by scanning `apps/**` for the column name.
2. **Which codes it holds is DERIVED, never listed**: `select code from app.permission where module = 'pos'
   and code not like 'pos.sync.%'`, mirrored in TypeScript as `CASHIER_SNAPSHOT_CODES` over `PERMISSIONS`.
   A literal list would have to name 6D's codes before 6D seeds them. `pos.sync.*` is excluded because it
   is the *terminal's* authority (R8), never a cashier's.
3. **The server never reads it.** Sync apply re-verifies against `app.principal_has` and the
   `branch_pos_policy` row (spec:1324). The snapshot exists for the till and for nothing else.
4. **The nightly job reports and does not repair.** `pos.cashier.compare` recomputes every cashier's
   snapshot and writes one `app.audit_log` row with `table_name = 'cashier_snapshot_drift'` per differing
   cashier (§4.2.6's sentinel mechanism, third registered sentinel). Repairing would make a broken trigger
   invisible for exactly as long as the job kept running.

## Consequences

- A permission change is visible at the till only after the next catalogue pull. That is the design: the
  revision bumps in the same transaction as the grant, so the pull that follows carries it, and the *server*
  refuses anything the stale snapshot wrongly allowed.
- `refresh_pos_cashier` runs inside the writer's transaction and under the writer's RLS. A `branch_manager`
  cannot change a role assignment (they hold no `admin.role.assign`), so in practice the callers are the
  admin, `hr_payroll` and the system principal — all of which see every branch.
- Adding a POS permission code in a later slice changes no line here; the derivation picks it up, and T7's
  equality test between the SQL and TypeScript spellings is what keeps the two honest.
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the failing snapshot test**

`packages/db/test/staff/cashier-snapshot.test.ts`:

```ts
import { afterAll, beforeAll, describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { startDb, withTenant, type Db } from "../harness";
import { seedTenant, seedBranch, seedEmployee, seedCashier, seedBranchPosPolicy, grantCodes, type Seed } from "../fixtures";
import { CASHIER_SNAPSHOT_CODES } from "@dulja/contracts/permissions";
import { readSnapshot, computeSnapshot } from "../../src/staff/cashier";

let db: Db;
let s: Seed;
let other: Seed;          // a second tenant, for the security_invoker case only
let branch: string;
let employee: string;

beforeAll(async () => {
  db = await startDb();
  s = await seedTenant(db, "snap");
  other = await seedTenant(db, "snap-other");
  // `snapshotOf` reads `app.pos_cashier` as `s.human`, and that table carries Form A of
  // `branch_isolation` from Task 5's migration 0068. One company-wide grant (no `branchIds`) keeps the
  // read non-empty — amendment A15's consumer side. Without it every snapshot assertion in this file
  // fails on `row!` being undefined and the failure points at `readSnapshot` rather than at the policy.
  await grantCodes(db, s);
  branch = await seedBranch(db, s, { code: "BR-S" });
  employee = await seedEmployee(db, s, { employeeNumber: "E-S", homeBranchId: branch });
}, 180_000);
afterAll(async () => { await db.stop(); });

/** `readSnapshot` takes a `tx` like every repository function (spec:2053); this wraps it once. */
const snapshotOf = (principalId: string) =>
  withTenant(db.rw, s.tenant, s.human, (tx) => readSnapshot(tx, principalId));

describe("pos_cashier.snapshot — a projection with one writer (§4.6.3, ADR 0061)", () => {
  it("the SQL and TypeScript spellings of the code set agree (amendment A5)", async () => {
    const rows = await db.admin<{ code: string }[]>`
      select code from app.permission where module = 'pos' and code not like 'pos.sync.%' order by code`;
    expect(rows.map((r) => r.code)).toEqual([...CASHIER_SNAPSHOT_CODES].sort());
    expect(CASHIER_SNAPSHOT_CODES).not.toContain("pos.sync.pull");
    expect(CASHIER_SNAPSHOT_CODES).not.toContain("pos.sync.push");
  });

  it("a new cashier's snapshot holds only the codes their assignment grants, at their branch", async () => {
    await seedBranchPosPolicy(db, s, { branchId: branch, limits: { maxLineDiscountPct: "0.100000", maxVoidAmount: "40.0000" } });
    const p = await seedCashier(db, s, { employeeId: employee, branchId: branch, permissions: ["pos.ticket.sell", "pos.ticket.discount"] });
    const snap = await snapshotOf(p);
    expect(snap.codes.sort()).toEqual(["pos.ticket.discount", "pos.ticket.sell"]);
    expect(snap.limits.maxLineDiscountPct).toBe("0.100000");
    expect(snap.limits.maxVoidAmount).toBe("40.0000");
    expect(BigInt(snap.revision)).toBeGreaterThan(0n);
  });

  it("granting a code regenerates the snapshot and bumps the revision — by TRIGGER, not by a handler", async () => {
    const e2 = await seedEmployee(db, s, { employeeNumber: "E-S2", homeBranchId: branch });
    const p = await seedCashier(db, s, { employeeId: e2, branchId: branch, permissions: ["pos.ticket.sell"] });
    const before = await snapshotOf(p);
    await grantCodes(db, s, { codes: ["pos.ticket.void"], principalId: p, roleCode: "void-role", branchIds: [branch] });
    const after = await snapshotOf(p);
    expect(after.codes.sort()).toEqual(["pos.ticket.sell", "pos.ticket.void"]);
    expect(BigInt(after.revision)).toBeGreaterThan(BigInt(before.revision));
  });

  it("editing the branch's limit row regenerates every cashier snapshot at that branch", async () => {
    const e3 = await seedEmployee(db, s, { employeeNumber: "E-S3", homeBranchId: branch });
    const p = await seedCashier(db, s, { employeeId: e3, branchId: branch, permissions: ["pos.ticket.sell"] });
    await withTenant(db.rw, s.tenant, s.human, (tx) =>
      tx`update app.branch_pos_policy set max_void_amount = 99.0000 where branch_id = ${branch}`);
    expect((await snapshotOf(p)).limits.maxVoidAmount).toBe("99.0000");
  });

  it("a cashier at a branch with NO policy row gets the closed defaults, never a null limit set", async () => {
    const bare = await seedBranch(db, s, { code: "BR-BARE" });
    const e4 = await seedEmployee(db, s, { employeeNumber: "E-S4", homeBranchId: bare });
    const p = await seedCashier(db, s, { employeeId: e4, branchId: bare, permissions: ["pos.ticket.sell"] });
    const snap = await snapshotOf(p);
    expect(snap.limits).toEqual({
      maxLineDiscountPct: "0.000000", maxTicketDiscountPct: "0.000000", maxVoidAmount: "0.0000",
      maxPriceOverridePct: "0.000000", blindReturnAllowed: false, maxBlindReturnAmount: "0.0000",
      roundingIncrement: "0.0500", requireCustomerOver: null, maxCashInDrawer: null,
    });
  });

  it("revoking empties the snapshot's codes — a revoked cashier authorises nothing offline (test 34)", async () => {
    const e5 = await seedEmployee(db, s, { employeeNumber: "E-S5", homeBranchId: branch });
    const p = await seedCashier(db, s, { employeeId: e5, branchId: branch, permissions: ["pos.ticket.sell"] });
    await withTenant(db.rw, s.tenant, s.human, (tx) =>
      tx`update app.pos_cashier set revoked_at = now() where principal_id = ${p}`);
    await withTenant(db.rw, s.tenant, s.human, (tx) => tx`select app.refresh_pos_cashier(${p})`);
    expect((await snapshotOf(p)).codes).toEqual([]);
  });

  it("computeSnapshot (the job's recomputation) agrees with the stored row for every cashier", async () => {
    const stored = await withTenant(db.rw, s.tenant, s.system, (tx) =>
      tx<{ principal_id: string; snapshot: unknown }[]>`select principal_id, snapshot from app.pos_cashier order by principal_id`);
    for (const row of stored) {
      const fresh = await withTenant(db.rw, s.tenant, s.system, (tx) => computeSnapshot(tx, row.principal_id));
      expect(fresh).toEqual(row.snapshot);
    }
  });

  it("snapshot->>'revision' is the row's OWN revision, as a string, after every write", async () => {
    // The trap this case exists for: `refresh_pos_cashier`'s UPDATE fires 0065's `catalogue_revision`
    // BEFORE trigger, so any revision the function itself computed would be one behind the row it stored,
    // and the case above would fail for every cashier in the table. `snapshot_revision_stamp` runs second
    // (name order: catalogue_revision < snapshot_revision_stamp) and stamps the value actually stored.
    const e6 = await seedEmployee(db, s, { employeeNumber: "E-S6", homeBranchId: branch });
    const p = await seedCashier(db, s, { employeeId: e6, branchId: branch, permissions: ["pos.ticket.sell"] });
    const check = async () => {
      const [row] = await withTenant(db.rw, s.tenant, s.system, (tx) => tx<
        { revision: string; snap_revision: string }[]
      >`select revision::text as revision, snapshot->>'revision' as snap_revision
          from app.pos_cashier where principal_id = ${p}`);
      expect(typeof row!.snap_revision).toBe("string");         // never a JSON number: a bigint has no safe one
      expect(row!.snap_revision).toBe(row!.revision);
    };
    await check();
    await withTenant(db.rw, s.tenant, s.system, (tx) =>
      tx`update app.pos_cashier set pin_updated_at = now() where principal_id = ${p}`);
    await check();                                              // a write that is NOT a refresh keeps them equal
  });

  it("who_can_reset_a_pin is evaluated as the READER — a second tenant's manager sees zero rows", async () => {
    // security_invoker = true. Without it the view runs with app_migrator's privileges, and the one object
    // in this slice whose RLS is not the caller's is exactly the object spec:2134 hands to an auditor.
    await grantCodes(db, s, { codes: ["pos.cashier.manage_pin"], principalId: s.human, roleCode: "pin-resetter" });
    const mine = await withTenant(db.rw, s.tenant, s.human, (tx) => tx<{ principal_id: string }[]>`
      select principal_id from app.who_can_reset_a_pin where company_id = ${s.company}`);
    expect(mine.map((r) => r.principal_id)).toContain(s.human);
    const theirs = await withTenant(db.rw, other.tenant, other.human, (tx) => tx<{ principal_id: string }[]>`
      select principal_id from app.who_can_reset_a_pin where company_id = ${s.company}`);
    expect(theirs).toHaveLength(0);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Append sections 3–5 to `0067_…_and_cashier_snapshot.sql`**

```sql
-- ── 3. the snapshot projection (§4.6.3; spec:1324; ADR 0061) ────────────────────────────────────────
-- The ONE writer of pos_cashier.snapshot. Which codes it holds is DERIVED from the catalogue, never
-- listed: a literal list would have to name 6D's pos.shift.close_any and pos.report.read before 6D seeds
-- them. pos.sync.* is excluded because it is the TERMINAL's authority (R8, spec:2179), never a cashier's.
-- A revoked cashier's code array is empty: the till must authorise nothing for them even before the next
-- catalogue pull removes the row (spec:2090, test 34).
-- **Two things this function deliberately does NOT write, and why.**
-- (1) `revision`. The UPDATE below fires 0065's `catalogue_revision` BEFORE trigger, which assigns
--     `new.revision := nextval(...)`. Any revision this function computed would therefore be one behind the
--     row the statement actually stores, `computeSnapshot` (which reads the row's CURRENT revision) would
--     disagree with the stored snapshot for EVERY cashier, and `findSnapshotDrift` would report the whole
--     table as drifted forever. Section 3a stamps the revision instead, after the trigger has assigned it.
-- (2) An untyped `0` or `0.05` fallback. `coalesce(b.max_void_amount, 0)::text` yields `'0'` for a branch
--     with no policy row — scale 0, because an untyped integer literal has scale 0 — while a real row yields
--     `'0.0000'`. Every fallback is therefore cast to its own column's type first, so a missing row and a
--     zeroed row produce byte-identical JSON and `limits.ts`'s TypeScript fallback can match them exactly.
create function app.refresh_pos_cashier(p_principal_id uuid) returns void language plpgsql as $$
declare c app.pos_cashier%rowtype;
begin
  select * into c from app.pos_cashier where principal_id = p_principal_id;
  if c.principal_id is null then return; end if;      -- not a cashier, or not visible to this actor

  update app.pos_cashier set
    snapshot = jsonb_build_object(
      'codes',
        case when c.revoked_at is not null then '[]'::jsonb
        else coalesce((
          select jsonb_agg(pm.code order by pm.code)
            from app.permission pm
           where pm.module = 'pos' and pm.code not like 'pos.sync.%'
             and app.principal_has(c.principal_id, pm.code, c.company_id, c.branch_id)
        ), '[]'::jsonb) end,
      'limits', (
        select jsonb_build_object(
                 'maxLineDiscountPct',   coalesce(b.max_line_discount_pct,   0::numeric(9,6))::text,
                 'maxTicketDiscountPct', coalesce(b.max_ticket_discount_pct, 0::numeric(9,6))::text,
                 'maxVoidAmount',        coalesce(b.max_void_amount,         0::numeric(19,4))::text,
                 'maxPriceOverridePct',  coalesce(b.max_price_override_pct,  0::numeric(9,6))::text,
                 'blindReturnAllowed',   coalesce(b.blind_return_allowed,    false),
                 'maxBlindReturnAmount', coalesce(b.max_blind_return_amount, 0::numeric(19,4))::text,
                 'roundingIncrement',    coalesce(b.rounding_increment,      0.05::numeric(19,4))::text,
                 'requireCustomerOver',  b.require_customer_over::text,
                 'maxCashInDrawer',      b.max_cash_in_drawer::text)
          from (select 1) one
          left join app.branch_pos_policy b on b.company_id = c.company_id and b.branch_id = c.branch_id)),
    snapshot_at = now()
  where principal_id = p_principal_id;
end $$;

-- ── 3a. the revision stamp — the ONE writer of snapshot->'revision' ──────────────────────────────────
-- A bigint has no safe JS number form, so the value is a decimal STRING in the JSON and every reader takes
-- `snapshot->>'revision'` (§4.10.2). It is written by a BEFORE trigger rather than by refresh_pos_cashier
-- because `new.revision` is not known until 0065's `catalogue_revision` trigger has run.
--
-- ⚠️ **Trigger ORDER is load-bearing and is a name, not a declaration.** PostgreSQL fires triggers of the
-- same event in *name* order, and `catalogue_revision` < `snapshot_revision_stamp`, so the sequence value is
-- assigned first and stamped second. **Renaming either trigger silently inverts them** and puts a stale
-- revision back in the snapshot; Step 3's *"snapshot->>'revision' is the row's OWN revision"* case is what
-- would catch it, and the `computeSnapshot agrees with the stored row` case would go red for every cashier.
-- Stamping on EVERY insert or update — not only on a refresh — is deliberate: a PIN reset or a revocation
-- bumps the revision too, and a snapshot whose revision lagged its own row would tell a till it was current.
create function app.stamp_snapshot_revision() returns trigger language plpgsql as $$
begin
  new.snapshot := jsonb_set(coalesce(new.snapshot, '{}'::jsonb), '{revision}',
                            to_jsonb(new.revision::text), true);
  return new;
end $$;

create trigger snapshot_revision_stamp before insert or update on app.pos_cashier
  for each row execute function app.stamp_snapshot_revision();

-- ── 4. the four triggers that call it (spec:1324 names the first three; the fourth is the limit row) ──
create function app.refresh_pos_cashier_for_assignment() returns trigger language plpgsql as $$
declare target uuid := coalesce(new.principal_id, old.principal_id);
begin
  perform app.refresh_pos_cashier(target);
  return null;
end $$;

create function app.refresh_pos_cashier_for_role() returns trigger language plpgsql as $$
declare r record;
begin
  for r in select ra.principal_id from app.role_assignment ra
            where ra.role_id = coalesce(new.role_id, old.role_id) loop
    perform app.refresh_pos_cashier(r.principal_id);
  end loop;
  return null;
end $$;

create function app.refresh_pos_cashier_for_branch_policy() returns trigger language plpgsql as $$
declare r record;
begin
  for r in select principal_id from app.pos_cashier
            where company_id = new.company_id and branch_id = new.branch_id loop
    perform app.refresh_pos_cashier(r.principal_id);
  end loop;
  return null;
end $$;

create trigger refresh_pos_cashier after insert or update or delete on app.role_assignment
  for each row execute function app.refresh_pos_cashier_for_assignment();
create trigger refresh_pos_cashier after insert or update or delete on app.role_assignment_branch
  for each row execute function app.refresh_pos_cashier_for_assignment();
create trigger refresh_pos_cashier after insert or update or delete on app.role_permission
  for each row execute function app.refresh_pos_cashier_for_role();
create trigger refresh_pos_cashier after insert or update on app.branch_pos_policy
  for each row execute function app.refresh_pos_cashier_for_branch_policy();

-- ── 5. one query answers "who can reset a PIN at branch B" (spec:2134, test 36) ─────────────────────
-- A view, not a function, so the answer is a relation an auditor can filter and join like any other.
-- `security_invoker = true` because from PostgreSQL 15 a view otherwise executes with its OWNER's
-- privileges, and the owner here is app_migrator, the schema owner. The three tables it reads carry
-- `tenant_isolation` with FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY so nothing would leak either way — but this view is the
-- one object in the slice whose RLS would not be evaluated as the reader, and "the auditor's own query"
-- is the whole claim spec:2134 makes. Step 3's *"evaluated as the READER"* case asserts a second tenant
-- reads zero rows through it.
create view app.who_can_reset_a_pin with (security_invoker = true) as
  select distinct ra.company_id, b.branch_id, ra.principal_id
    from app.role_assignment ra
    join app.role_permission rp on rp.role_id = ra.role_id and rp.permission_code = 'pos.cashier.manage_pin'
    left join app.role_assignment_branch b
           on b.principal_id = ra.principal_id and b.role_id = ra.role_id and b.company_id = ra.company_id
   where ra.expires_at is null or ra.expires_at > now();
comment on view app.who_can_reset_a_pin is
  'spec:2134 / test 36: a branch_id of NULL means company-wide (no role_assignment_branch rows), exactly as app.actor_has reads it.';
grant select on app.who_can_reset_a_pin to app_rw;
```

The down file gains, **before** the table drop:

```sql
drop view if exists app.who_can_reset_a_pin;
drop trigger if exists refresh_pos_cashier on app.branch_pos_policy;
drop trigger if exists refresh_pos_cashier on app.role_permission;
drop trigger if exists refresh_pos_cashier on app.role_assignment_branch;
drop trigger if exists refresh_pos_cashier on app.role_assignment;
drop trigger if exists snapshot_revision_stamp on app.pos_cashier;
drop function if exists app.stamp_snapshot_revision() cascade;
drop function if exists app.refresh_pos_cashier_for_branch_policy() cascade;
drop function if exists app.refresh_pos_cashier_for_role() cascade;
drop function if exists app.refresh_pos_cashier_for_assignment() cascade;
drop function if exists app.refresh_pos_cashier(uuid) cascade;
```

**Why AFTER triggers with `return null`.** They are statement-effect-free row triggers: `perform` runs the update, the return value of an AFTER row trigger is ignored, and `null` is the idiomatic way to say so. **Why `role_permission` fans out through `role_assignment`.** A permission added to a role changes the authority of every principal holding it, and the spec names `role_permission` as one of the three triggers. The loop is bounded by the number of holders of one role, which in a distributor with nine seeded roles is small; if a tenant ever makes it large, the fix is a statement-level trigger with a transition table, and that is a performance change, not a semantic one.

- [ ] **Step 5: Write `packages/db/src/staff/cashier.ts`'s snapshot half**

```ts
import type { Sql } from "postgres";

export interface CashierLimits {
  maxLineDiscountPct: string; maxTicketDiscountPct: string; maxVoidAmount: string;
  maxPriceOverridePct: string; blindReturnAllowed: boolean; maxBlindReturnAmount: string;
  roundingIncrement: string; requireCustomerOver: string | null; maxCashInDrawer: string | null;
}

/** `revision` is a decimal STRING: a bigint has no safe JS number form (§4.10.2). */
export interface CashierSnapshot { codes: string[]; limits: CashierLimits; revision: string }

/**
 * Recomputes a cashier's snapshot WITHOUT writing it — the nightly comparison's half of ADR 0061.
 * It runs the identical SQL `app.refresh_pos_cashier` runs, so a difference is a trigger fault and never
 * a difference between two implementations of the same rule.
 */
export async function computeSnapshot(tx: Sql, principalId: string): Promise<CashierSnapshot | null> {
  const [row] = await tx<{ snapshot: CashierSnapshot }[]>`
    select jsonb_build_object(
      'codes', case when c.revoked_at is not null then '[]'::jsonb else coalesce((
                 select jsonb_agg(pm.code order by pm.code) from app.permission pm
                  where pm.module = 'pos' and pm.code not like 'pos.sync.%'
                    and app.principal_has(c.principal_id, pm.code, c.company_id, c.branch_id)), '[]'::jsonb) end,
      'limits', (select jsonb_build_object(
                   'maxLineDiscountPct',   coalesce(b.max_line_discount_pct,   0::numeric(9,6))::text,
                   'maxTicketDiscountPct', coalesce(b.max_ticket_discount_pct, 0::numeric(9,6))::text,
                   'maxVoidAmount',        coalesce(b.max_void_amount,         0::numeric(19,4))::text,
                   'maxPriceOverridePct',  coalesce(b.max_price_override_pct,  0::numeric(9,6))::text,
                   'blindReturnAllowed',   coalesce(b.blind_return_allowed,    false),
                   'maxBlindReturnAmount', coalesce(b.max_blind_return_amount, 0::numeric(19,4))::text,
                   'roundingIncrement',    coalesce(b.rounding_increment,      0.05::numeric(19,4))::text,
                   'requireCustomerOver',  b.require_customer_over::text,
                   'maxCashInDrawer',      b.max_cash_in_drawer::text)
                   from (select 1) one
                   left join app.branch_pos_policy b on b.company_id = c.company_id and b.branch_id = c.branch_id),
      -- `::text`, matching the stamp trigger byte for byte. The whole point of this function is that
      -- `JSON.stringify(fresh) === JSON.stringify(stored)` holds for a healthy row, and a JSON number here
      -- against a JSON string there would report every cashier in the tenant as drifted.
      'revision', c.revision::text) as snapshot
      from app.pos_cashier c where c.principal_id = ${principalId}`;
  return row?.snapshot ?? null;
}

export interface SnapshotDrift { principalId: string; companyId: string; branchId: string; stored: CashierSnapshot; fresh: CashierSnapshot }

/** Every cashier whose stored snapshot differs from a fresh computation. Reads only. */
export async function findSnapshotDrift(tx: Sql, opts: { companyId?: string } = {}): Promise<SnapshotDrift[]> {
  const rows = await tx<{ principalId: string; companyId: string; branchId: string; stored: CashierSnapshot }[]>`
    select principal_id as "principalId", company_id as "companyId", branch_id as "branchId", snapshot as stored
      from app.pos_cashier
     where (${opts.companyId ?? null}::uuid is null or company_id = ${opts.companyId ?? null}::uuid)
     order by principal_id`;
  const drift: SnapshotDrift[] = [];
  for (const row of rows) {
    const fresh = await computeSnapshot(tx, row.principalId);
    if (fresh && JSON.stringify(fresh) !== JSON.stringify(row.stored)) drift.push({ ...row, fresh });
  }
  return drift;
}

/** Test-tier convenience; also what 6B's catalogue mirror reads through the sync endpoint. */
export async function readSnapshot(tx: Sql, principalId: string): Promise<CashierSnapshot> {
  const [row] = await tx<{ snapshot: CashierSnapshot }[]>`select snapshot from app.pos_cashier where principal_id = ${principalId}`;
  return row!.snapshot;
}
```

Add `CASHIER_SNAPSHOT_CODES` to `packages/contracts/permissions.ts`:

```ts
/**
 * The codes a cashier's offline snapshot carries (§4.6.3, ADR 0061). DERIVED, never listed: 6B's and 6D's
 * codes join the moment they are seeded, and `PERMISSION["pos.shift.close_any"]` cannot be referenced
 * before 6D exists. `pos.sync.*` is the TERMINAL's authority (R8) and is excluded.
 * The SQL twin lives in `app.refresh_pos_cashier`; a test in 6C asserts the two are equal.
 */
export const CASHIER_SNAPSHOT_CODES: readonly PermissionCode[] =
  PERMISSIONS.filter((p) => p.module === "pos" && !p.code.startsWith("pos.sync.")).map((p) => p.code);
```

Run the two earlier suites again — `pos-cashier.test.ts` and `branch-isolation.test.ts` — and confirm both are still green; a fixture that silently changed shape under an earlier test is exactly what this step exists to catch.

```ts
      ["catalogue_revision", "pos_cashier_tombstone", "snapshot_revision_stamp"]);
```

- [ ] **Step 7: Write the job handler and its test**

`apps/worker/src/jobs/cashier-compare.ts`:

```ts
// One static import for both names. There is no cycle here — `apps/worker` depends on `@dulja/db` and
// `@dulja/db` depends on nothing in this direction — so the dynamic `await import("@dulja/db")` an earlier
// draft used inside the handler bought nothing and hid the dependency from the module graph the
// `dependency-rule` lint walks. Every other module in this slice imports statically; so does this one.
import { findSnapshotDrift, withTenant } from "@dulja/db";
import type { JobHandler } from "../handler";
import { log } from "../log";

/**
 * D14's nightly comparison for `pos_cashier.snapshot` (spec:1324, ADR 0061). It REPORTS and does not
 * repair: a silent repair would hide the trigger fault the comparison exists to find. One audit row per
 * differing cashier, under the sentinel `table_name = 'cashier_snapshot_drift'` (§4.2.6, amendment A6).
 */
export const cashierCompare: JobHandler<"pos.cashier.compare"> = async (sql, job, ctx) => {
  const drift = await withTenant(sql, ctx, (tx) => findSnapshotDrift(tx, { companyId: job.payload.companyId }));
  if (drift.length === 0) {
    log().info({ tenant_id: ctx.tenantId, checked: "pos_cashier" }, "cashier snapshots agree");
    return;
  }
  await withTenant(sql, ctx, async (tx) => {
    for (const d of drift) {
      // The columns are `before` and `after`. `app.audit_log` (plan0A:2260–2274) declares
      // `before jsonb` / `after jsonb`, and `app.audit_chain()` hashes `new.before` / `new.after` by
      // those names — a `before_image` here is `column "before_image" of relation "audit_log" does not
      // exist` on the first drift row, which is the only row this job ever writes. 1B's
      // `integrity_incident` sentinel writes use the same two names.
      await tx`
        insert into app.audit_log (tenant_id, table_name, row_id, action, actor_id, "before", "after")
        values (app.current_tenant(), 'cashier_snapshot_drift', ${d.principalId}, 'update', app.current_actor(),
                ${JSON.stringify(d.stored)}::jsonb, ${JSON.stringify(d.fresh)}::jsonb)`;
    }
  });
  log().warn(
    { tenant_id: ctx.tenantId, drifted: drift.length, principals: drift.map((d) => d.principalId) },
    "cashier snapshot drift — the refresh trigger did not fire for these cashiers",
  );
};
```

Register it (`registerHandler("pos.cashier.compare", cashierCompare)` in `apps/worker/src/register.ts`), add the payload key (`"pos.cashier.compare": { companyId?: string }` in `packages/db/src/jobs/payloads.ts` — **one key, no other line touched**) and add the `JOB_PROBES` entry: seed a cashier in each of two tenants, run the job for the attacker's tenant, assert the victim's snapshot and audit rows are untouched.

`apps/worker/test/jobs/cashier-compare.test.ts` — four cases: (1) a fresh tenant produces **zero** drift rows and one `info` line; (2) a snapshot hand-corrupted through `db.migrator` (the one place a test may write the column directly, and the comment says why) produces exactly one `cashier_snapshot_drift` audit row whose **`before`** carries the corrupted `codes` and `limits` and whose **`after`** carries the recomputation — the two column names `app.audit_log` actually has (plan0A:2270–2271); (3) the job **does not repair** — the stored snapshot is still the corrupted one after the run, which is ADR 0061's decision asserted rather than described; (4) `companyId` narrows the scan to one company.

⚠️ **Case (2) asserts on `codes` and `limits`, never on `revision`.** The corrupting UPDATE is an UPDATE like any other, so `catalogue_revision` and then `snapshot_revision_stamp` both fire on it and rewrite `snapshot->'revision'` to the row's new value — which is *correct* and is what keeps the two in step. The drift the job reports is therefore in the other two members, and a `before` compared whole would be asserting the stamp trigger's own behaviour by accident.

```ts
  it("reports and does not repair — the fault stays visible (ADR 0061)", async () => {
    // The literal has no `revision` member: snapshot_revision_stamp writes one on this very UPDATE (T7
    // section 3a), and supplying a second spelling here would assert the trigger away rather than the drift.
    await withTenant(w.db.migrator, s.tenant, s.system, (tx) =>
      tx`update app.pos_cashier set snapshot = '{"codes":[],"limits":{}}'::jsonb where principal_id = ${cashier}`);
    await w.run("pos.cashier.compare", { tenantId: s.tenant, requestId: crypto.randomUUID(), payload: {} });
    const [row] = await withTenant(w.db.rw, s.tenant, s.system, (tx) =>
      tx<{ snapshot: { codes: string[] } }[]>`select snapshot from app.pos_cashier where principal_id = ${cashier}`);
    expect(row!.snapshot.codes).toEqual([]);                       // still corrupt: the job did not "fix" it
    const audits = await withTenant(w.db.rw, s.tenant, s.system, (tx) =>
      tx`select row_id from app.audit_log where table_name = 'cashier_snapshot_drift'`);
    expect(audits).toHaveLength(1);
  });
```

### Task 8: Employee and cashier API — onboarding through the `AuthAdmin` port and the PIN endpoints

**Every cashier has a real Supabase auth user** (spec:1324, 2097): created through the admin API at hire with a random undisclosed password, even when the person never opens the back office, so `auth_subject` is always a Supabase `sub` and the §4.2 rule that a principal is created only from a Supabase-obtained subject holds for cashiers too. **The application never sees that password**, and the PIN is a separate credential that is never a login.

**Files:**
- Create: `packages/db/src/staff/employee.ts`, `packages/db/src/staff/index.ts`, `packages/contracts/src/hr/employee.ts`, `packages/contracts/src/pos/cashier.ts`, `apps/api/src/routes/hr/employees.ts`, `apps/api/src/routes/pos/cashiers.ts`, `apps/api/test/routes/hr/employees.test.ts`, `apps/api/test/routes/pos/cashiers.test.ts`, `docs/runbooks/cashier-onboarding.md`
- Modify: `apps/api/src/ports.ts` (`AuthAdmin.createUser`), `apps/api/src/auth-admin.ts` (both implementations), `apps/api/src/routes/index.ts`, `packages/db/src/staff/cashier.ts` (the repository half), `packages/db/src/index.ts`, `tests/invariants/probes.ts`

**Interfaces**

- Consumes: `AuthAdmin`, `InvitedUser`, `StubAuthAdmin`, `SupabaseAuthAdmin`, `AppDeps`, `buildRoutes`, `requirePermission`, `PERMISSION` (the four T6 codes plus `admin.user.manage`), `hashPin` (T2), `app.refresh_pos_cashier` (T7), `seedParty` (2A), `startApi`, `grantAdmin`, `problemOf`.
- Produces:
