- [ ] **Step 1: Fix the gate table before writing a route — which code guards what, and why**

| Route | Gate | Why that code and not another |
|---|---|---|
| `GET …/employees`, `GET …/employees/{id}` | `hr.employee.read` | spec:2159 gives the clerk *"the staff directory (no sensitive fields)"*. |
| `POST …/employees` | `hr.employee.manage` | spec:2162 — the master is `hr_payroll`'s. |
| `GET …/cashiers` | `pos.cashier.manage_pin` | The list carries `branchId`, `revokedAt` and the snapshot's code array; a branch manager who may reset a PIN may see whose PIN there is to reset. **The row-level control is `branch_isolation`**, so the list is already this manager's branch only. |
| `POST …/cashiers` | **`admin.user.manage`** | It mints an `app.principal` from a Supabase `sub`. That is exactly what 0B's `/v1/admin/users` does and exactly the code 0B gates it with; a branch manager creating principals would be a quiet path around `admin.user.manage`, and spec:2160 gives them PIN **reset**, not user creation. |
| `POST …/cashiers/{principalId}/pin` | `pos.cashier.manage_pin` | spec:2160 — *"resetting a PIN for a cashier in the same branch"*. |
| `POST …/cashiers/{principalId}/revoke` | `admin.user.manage` | Closing a credential is the authority that opened it. **The ordinary path is not this route at all** — 8C's termination trigger sets `revoked_at` from `employee.terminated_on`; this is the break-glass door for a lost badge before a termination is recorded. |

**"In the same branch" is not a second condition to code.** `branch_isolation` (T5) makes another branch's `pos_cashier` row invisible, so the PIN route's own `select` returns nothing and the handler answers 404. Task 10 Step 2's by-id case is the test; Step 3 is the one-query clause, one of test 36's two.

**The role assignment is not this module's job.** A new cashier's `cashier` role and its `role_assignment_branch` row go through 0B's existing `/v1/companies/{companyId}/role-assignments` under `admin.role.assign`, and the snapshot fills itself when that row lands (T7's trigger). Duplicating the assignment here would put a second writer on `role_assignment` and would bypass `forbid_self_grant` and `assert_assignment_kind`.

- [ ] **Step 2: Extend the `AuthAdmin` port (amendment A3)**

```ts
// apps/api/src/ports.ts
export interface InvitedUser { readonly sub: string; readonly email: string }
export interface AuthAdmin {
  inviteUser(email: string): Promise<InvitedUser>;
  /**
   * Creates a Supabase user with a random undisclosed password and sends NO invitation (spec:1324):
   * a cashier who never opens the back office still needs a real `sub`, so the identity model has exactly
   * one human scheme. The password is generated by Supabase and never reaches this process.
   */
  createUser(email: string): Promise<InvitedUser>;
}
```

```ts
// apps/api/src/auth-admin.ts
export class StubAuthAdmin implements AuthAdmin {
  constructor(private readonly seed = "") {}
  async inviteUser(email: string): Promise<InvitedUser> { return { sub: `stub:${await sha256Hex(this.seed + email)}`, email }; }
  /** The same deterministic subject: a test that creates and then re-creates must collide, exactly as production would. */
  async createUser(email: string): Promise<InvitedUser> { return this.inviteUser(email); }
}

export class SupabaseAuthAdmin implements AuthAdmin {
  constructor(private readonly opts: { url: string; serviceRoleKey: string; invitePath: string; createPath: string }) {}
  async inviteUser(email: string): Promise<InvitedUser> { /* unchanged — 0B's body */ }
  async createUser(email: string): Promise<InvitedUser> {
    const res = await fetch(new URL(this.opts.createPath, this.opts.url), {
      method: "POST",
      headers: { authorization: `Bearer ${this.opts.serviceRoleKey}`, apikey: this.opts.serviceRoleKey, "content-type": "application/json" },
      // No password field: Supabase generates one we never see. email_confirm skips the mailbox round-trip
      // for an alias address that has no mailbox behind it (OD-11).
      body: JSON.stringify({ email, email_confirm: true }),
    });
    if (!res.ok) throw new AppError("ERR_INTERNAL", `the auth admin refused to create ${email}`);
    const body = await res.json() as { id: string; email: string };
    return { sub: body.id, email: body.email };
  }
}
```

**Which implementation the deployed container receives is 0C's ADR 0011 decision and is not re-opened here** — §3.11 keeps `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` out of the container deliberately, and this slice adds no environment variable.

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the failing route tests**

`apps/api/test/routes/pos/cashiers.test.ts` — the eight cases that matter:

```ts
import { afterAll, beforeAll, describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { startApi, type StartedApi } from "../../support/app";
import { seedTenant, seedTenantV2, seedBranch, seedEmployee, grantCodes, uuidv7, withTenant, type Seed } from "@dulja/db/test";
import { grantAdmin, problemOf } from "../../../../../tests/support/api";

let api: StartedApi;
let s: Seed;
let branchA: string;
let branchB: string;
let employeeA: string;

beforeAll(async () => {
  api = await startApi();
  s = await seedTenant(api.db, "cash");
  branchA = s.hq;
  branchB = await seedBranch(api.db, s, { code: "BR-B" });
  employeeA = await seedEmployee(api.db, s, { employeeNumber: "E-1", homeBranchId: branchA });
  await grantAdmin(api, s);
  await api.loadPrincipals();
}, 180_000);
afterAll(async () => { await api.stop(); });

const createCashier = (employeeId: string, branchId: string, as = s.human) =>
  api.request({ method: "POST", path: `/v1/companies/${s.company}/cashiers`, as,
                body: { employeeId, branchId, displayName: "Cashier", pin: "4321",
                        // OD-11's default: RFC 6761's reserved TLD, which can never resolve and can never
                        // be mistaken for a real address. No domain literal ships outside a test (T11).
                        email: `cashier-${employeeId}@example.invalid` } });

/**
 * A principal holding `pos.cashier.manage_pin` restricted to one branch — the branch_manager of the two
 * tests below. T9's `branchActor` does not exist yet, so this file carries its own three-line version.
 */
async function seedTenantManager(started: StartedApi, seed: Seed, branchId: string): Promise<string> {
  const id = await uuidv7(started.db.admin);
  await withTenant(started.db.migrator, seed.tenant, seed.system, async (tx) => {
    await tx`insert into app.principal (id, kind, auth_subject, display_name)
             values (${id}, 'human', ${`sub-mgr-${id}`}, 'Branch manager')`;
    await tx`insert into app.membership (tenant_id, principal_id) values (${seed.tenant}, ${id})`;
  });
  await grantCodes(started.db, seed, {
    codes: ["pos.cashier.manage_pin"], principalId: id, roleCode: `mgr-${id.slice(-8)}`, branchIds: [branchId],
  });
  await started.loadPrincipals();
  return id;
}

describe("POST|GET /v1/companies/{companyId}/cashiers", () => {
  it("creates the auth user, the principal, the membership and the row in one transaction", async () => {
    const res = await createCashier(employeeA, branchA);
    expect(res.status).toBe(201);
    const body = await res.json() as { principalId: string; branchId: string; revokedAt: string | null; snapshot: { codes: string[] } };
    expect(body.branchId).toBe(branchA);
    expect(body.revokedAt).toBeNull();
    expect(body.snapshot.codes).toEqual([]);          // no role assignment yet — that is admin.role.assign's route
    const [p] = await api.db.admin<{ auth_subject: string }[]>`select auth_subject from app.principal where id = ${body.principalId}`;
    expect(p!.auth_subject.startsWith("stub:")).toBe(true);   // a Supabase-obtained sub, never a client-supplied one
  });

  it("never returns the PIN, and never stores it in clear (LEGACY-028)", async () => {
    const e = await seedEmployee(api.db, s, { employeeNumber: "E-2", homeBranchId: branchA });
    const res = await createCashier(e, branchA);
    expect(await res.text()).not.toContain("4321");
    const [row] = await api.db.admin<{ pin_hash: string }[]>`
      select pin_hash from app.pos_cashier where employee_id = ${e}`;
    expect(row!.pin_hash).not.toContain("4321");
    expect(row!.pin_hash.startsWith("$argon2id$")).toBe(true);
  });

  it("a PIN outside the band is 400 before any user is created", async () => {
    const e = await seedEmployee(api.db, s, { employeeNumber: "E-3", homeBranchId: branchA });
    const res = await api.request({ method: "POST", path: `/v1/companies/${s.company}/cashiers`, as: s.human,
      body: { employeeId: e, branchId: branchA, email: "x@example.invalid", pin: "1" } });
    expect(res.status).toBe(400);
    expect((await problemOf(res)).code).toBe("ERR_VALIDATION_FAILED");
    const [{ n }] = await api.db.admin<{ n: number }[]>`select count(*)::int as n from app.pos_cashier where employee_id = ${e}`;
    expect(n).toBe(0);
  });

  it("a branch manager may reset a PIN in their own branch", async () => {
    const e = await seedEmployee(api.db, s, { employeeNumber: "E-4", homeBranchId: branchA });
    const created = await createCashier(e, branchA);
    const { principalId } = await created.json() as { principalId: string };

    const mgr = await seedTenantManager(api, s, branchA);      // grantCodes(["pos.cashier.manage_pin"], branchIds:[branchA])
    const res = await api.request({ method: "POST", path: `/v1/companies/${s.company}/cashiers/${principalId}/pin`, as: mgr,
      body: { pin: "778899" } });
    expect(res.status).toBe(200);
    const [row] = await api.db.admin<{ pin_hash: string }[]>`select pin_hash from app.pos_cashier where principal_id = ${principalId}`;
    expect(row!.pin_hash.startsWith("$argon2id$")).toBe(true);
  });

  it("a branch manager at B cannot reset a PIN at A — 404, not 403 (test 36; branch_isolation is the control)", async () => {
    const e = await seedEmployee(api.db, s, { employeeNumber: "E-5", homeBranchId: branchA });
    const { principalId } = await (await createCashier(e, branchA)).json() as { principalId: string };
    const mgrB = await seedTenantManager(api, s, branchB);
    const res = await api.request({ method: "POST", path: `/v1/companies/${s.company}/cashiers/${principalId}/pin`, as: mgrB,
      body: { pin: "112233" } });
    expect(res.status).toBe(404);
    expect((await problemOf(res)).code).toBe("ERR_NOT_FOUND");
  });

  it("a branch manager may not CREATE a cashier — that mints a principal (admin.user.manage)", async () => {
    const mgr = await seedTenantManager(api, s, branchA);
    const e = await seedEmployee(api.db, s, { employeeNumber: "E-6", homeBranchId: branchA });
    const res = await createCashier(e, branchA, mgr);
    expect(res.status).toBe(403);
    expect((await problemOf(res)).code).toBe("ERR_RBAC_FORBIDDEN");
  });

  it("revoking is idempotent at the row level and writes exactly one tombstone", async () => {
    const e = await seedEmployee(api.db, s, { employeeNumber: "E-7", homeBranchId: branchA });
    const { principalId } = await (await createCashier(e, branchA)).json() as { principalId: string };
    const first = await api.request({ method: "POST", path: `/v1/companies/${s.company}/cashiers/${principalId}/revoke`, as: s.human, body: {} });
    expect(first.status).toBe(200);
    const second = await api.request({ method: "POST", path: `/v1/companies/${s.company}/cashiers/${principalId}/revoke`, as: s.human, body: {} });
    expect(second.status).toBe(200);
    const [{ n }] = await api.db.admin<{ n: number }[]>`select count(*)::int as n from app.catalogue_tombstone where row_id = ${principalId}`;
    expect(n).toBe(1);
    const body = await second.json() as { snapshot: { codes: string[] } };
    expect(body.snapshot.codes).toEqual([]);
  });

  it("an employee of another tenant is 404, never 403", async () => {
    const other = await seedTenant(api.db, "cash-other");
    const foreign = await seedEmployee(api.db, other, { employeeNumber: "E-X" });
    const res = await createCashier(foreign, branchA);
    expect(res.status).toBe(404);
  });
});
```

```ts
describe("POST|GET /v1/companies/{companyId}/employees", () => {
  it("creates the party and the employee in ONE transaction (§4.6.2's closing sentence)", async () => {
    const res = await api.request({ method: "POST", path: `/v1/companies/${s.company}/employees`, as: s.human,
      body: { employeeNumber: "E-100", nameEn: "Aina", homeBranchId: branchA, hiredOn: "2026-01-05" } });
    expect(res.status).toBe(201);
    const body = await res.json() as { id: string; partyId: string; principalId: string | null };
    expect(body.principalId).toBeNull();          // only staff who log in or sell get one (spec:2097)
    const [party] = await api.db.admin<{ kind: string }[]>`select kind from app.party where id = ${body.partyId}`;
    expect(party!.kind).toBe("employee");
  });

  it("a failure leaves NO orphan party behind", async () => {
    const [{ n: before }] = await api.db.admin<{ n: number }[]>`select count(*)::int as n from app.party where kind = 'employee'`;
    const res = await api.request({ method: "POST", path: `/v1/companies/${s.company}/employees`, as: s.human,
      body: { employeeNumber: "E-101", nameEn: "Orphan", homeBranchId: crypto.randomUUID(), hiredOn: "2026-01-05" } });
    expect(res.status).toBe(404);
    const [{ n: after }] = await api.db.admin<{ n: number }[]>`select count(*)::int as n from app.party where kind = 'employee'`;
    expect(after).toBe(before);
  });

  it("a duplicate employee number in the same company is 409", async () => {
    const body = { employeeNumber: "E-DUP", nameEn: "First", homeBranchId: branchA, hiredOn: "2026-01-05" };
    expect((await api.request({ method: "POST", path: `/v1/companies/${s.company}/employees`, as: s.human, body })).status).toBe(201);
    const second = await api.request({ method: "POST", path: `/v1/companies/${s.company}/employees`, as: s.human, body });
    expect(second.status).toBe(409);
    expect((await problemOf(second)).code).toBe("ERR_CONFLICT_UNIQUE");
  });

  it("a reader with hr.employee.read may list and may not create", async () => {
    // T9's `branchActor` does not exist yet; a second seed holding one code says the same thing.
    const reader = await seedTenantV2(api.db, "emp-reader");
    await grantCodes(api.db, reader, { codes: ["hr.employee.read"] });
    await api.loadPrincipals();
    expect((await api.request({ method: "GET", path: `/v1/companies/${reader.company}/employees`, as: reader.human })).status).toBe(200);
    const write = await api.request({ method: "POST", path: `/v1/companies/${reader.company}/employees`, as: reader.human,
      body: { employeeNumber: "E-NO", nameEn: "No", homeBranchId: reader.hq, hiredOn: "2026-01-05" } });
    expect(write.status).toBe(403);
  });

  it("the list filters on the two declared keys and nothing else", async () => {
    const byBranch = await api.request({ method: "GET", as: s.human,
      path: `/v1/companies/${s.company}/employees?filter[homeBranchId][eq]=${branchA}` });
    expect(byBranch.status).toBe(200);
    for (const row of (await byBranch.json() as { items: { homeBranchId: string }[] }).items) {
      expect(row.homeBranchId).toBe(branchA);
    }
    const undeclared = await api.request({ method: "GET", as: s.human,
      path: `/v1/companies/${s.company}/employees?filter[hiredOn][gte]=2026-01-01` });
    expect(undeclared.status).toBe(400);          // an undeclared field is ERR_VALIDATION_FAILED (§4.2.8)
    // This 400 comes from `EmployeeQuery`'s `.strict()` (Step 4) and from nothing else — a plain
    // `ListQuery.extend({…})` STRIPS the key and answers 200. `sort` and `fields` are separately bounded
    // there because `.strict()` admits them: they are declared `ListQuery` members.
    for (const q of ["sort=employeeNumber", "fields=employeeNumber"]) {
      const bounded = await api.request({ method: "GET", as: s.human, path: `/v1/companies/${s.company}/employees?${q}` });
      expect(bounded.status).toBe(400);
      expect((await problemOf(bounded)).code).toBe("ERR_VALIDATION_FAILED");
    }
  });
});
```

`packages/contracts/src/hr/employee.ts`:

```ts
import { z } from "@hono/zod-openapi";
import { DateSchema, ListEnvelope, ListQuery, TimestampSchema, UuidSchema } from "../common";

export const EmployeeSchema = z.object({
  id: UuidSchema,
  partyId: UuidSchema,
  employeeNumber: z.string().min(1).max(40),
  homeBranchId: UuidSchema,
  principalId: UuidSchema.nullable(),
  hiredOn: DateSchema,
  terminatedOn: DateSchema.nullable(),
  createdAt: TimestampSchema,
}).openapi("Employee");
// No `position`, `employmentType` or sensitive field: 8A adds the columns in 0080 and the wire fields with
// them (§4.6.1). A wire field for a column that does not exist is how a value gets silently dropped.

export const CreateEmployeeBody = z.object({
  employeeNumber: z.string().min(1).max(40),
  nameEn: z.string().min(1).max(200),
  nameAr: z.string().max(200).optional(),
  homeBranchId: UuidSchema,
  hiredOn: DateSchema,
}).openapi("CreateEmployeeBody");

/** The ONLY filters: the home branch and the active flag. The employee directory is the one list in this
 *  slice with an undeclared-key assertion against it — Step 3's *"the list filters on the two declared keys
 *  and nothing else"* case sends `?filter[hiredOn][gte]=2026-01-01` and expects **400** — and 8A's Task 4
 *  adds four more against the same route (`filter[nationalId][eq]`, `filter[bankAccount][like]`,
 *  `sort=nationalId`, `sort=-bankAccount`, `fields=nationalId`), because 8A's 0080 columns are the sensitive
 *  ones and test 32 forbids a side channel through a query parameter. `CashierQuery`, `AttendanceQuery` and
 *  `BranchPosPolicyQuery` carry no such assertion and are deliberately left as plain extends.
 *
 *  **A bare `z.object` does not enforce that.** §4.2.8's *"an undeclared field is ERR_VALIDATION_FAILED"* is
 *  the required behaviour, not what Zod does by default: a plain object **strips** an unknown key and answers
 *  200. Two mechanisms are needed because they close different halves:
 *
 *  1. `.strict()` — turns an UNDECLARED key (`filter[hiredOn][gte]`, `filter[nationalId][eq]`) into
 *     ERR_VALIDATION_FAILED. 1B, 5A, 5B and 8A do the same on their literal-key query schemas.
 *  2. The two `z.string().max(0, …)` overrides — `sort` and `fields` are **declared** members of `ListQuery`
 *     (0b:1331), so `.strict()` admits them however sensitive the name they carry, and `listEmployees` reads
 *     neither (it is keyset on the id). Bounding them to the empty string on the same key is 0B's
 *     `AuditLogQuery` remedy (0b:4321): a `.refine` would wrap the object in `ZodEffects` and
 *     `@hono/zod-openapi` would stop generating query parameters from it.
 *
 *  **0B's own `AuditLogQuery` is correctly NOT `.strict()`** (0b:7384) — the audit log is §4.2.8 Layer 1's
 *  open `parseFilters` surface and `legacy-036` sends undeclared keys expecting them stripped. This is a Layer 2
 *  literal-key schema, where the enumeration IS the contract. Not an inconsistency to "harmonise". */
export const EmployeeQuery = ListQuery.extend({
  "filter[homeBranchId][eq]": UuidSchema.optional(),
  "filter[active][eq]": z.enum(["true", "false"]).optional(),     // terminated_on is null
  sort: z.string().max(0, "sort is not supported on this resource").optional(),
  fields: z.string().max(0, "sparse fieldsets are not supported on this resource").optional(),
}).strict();
export const EmployeeList = ListEnvelope(EmployeeSchema);
export type EmployeeDto = z.infer<typeof EmployeeSchema>;
```

`packages/contracts/src/pos/cashier.ts`:

```ts
import { z } from "@hono/zod-openapi";
import { ListEnvelope, ListQuery, TimestampSchema, UuidSchema } from "../common";

/** The projection the till mirrors. `revision` is a decimal STRING — a bigint has no safe JSON number. */
export const CashierSnapshotSchema = z.object({
  codes: z.array(z.string()),
  limits: z.record(z.union([z.string(), z.boolean(), z.null()])),
  revision: z.string(),
}).openapi("CashierSnapshot");

export const CashierSchema = z.object({
  principalId: UuidSchema,
  employeeId: UuidSchema,
  branchId: UuidSchema,
  pinUpdatedAt: TimestampSchema,
  revokedAt: TimestampSchema.nullable(),
  revision: z.string(),
  snapshot: CashierSnapshotSchema,
}).openapi("Cashier");
// `pinHash` is NOT a member and never becomes one: it is a credential, and a response schema is the one
// place a credential leaks without anybody writing the word "leak" (LEGACY-028).

const Pin = z.string().regex(/^[0-9]{4,12}$/, "a PIN is 4-12 digits");   // the same band `hashPin` enforces

export const CreateCashierBody = z.object({
  employeeId: UuidSchema,
  branchId: UuidSchema,
  email: z.string().email(),              // OD-11 makes this optional; until then it is required (T11)
  displayName: z.string().min(1).max(200),
  pin: Pin,
}).openapi("CreateCashierBody");

export const SetPinBody = z.object({ pin: Pin }).openapi("SetPinBody");

export const CashierQuery = ListQuery.extend({ "filter[branchId][eq]": UuidSchema.optional() });
export const CashierList = ListEnvelope(CashierSchema);
export type CashierDto = z.infer<typeof CashierSchema>;
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Write the two route modules**

`apps/api/src/routes/pos/cashiers.ts` — the create handler is the whole onboarding story and is written out in full:

```ts
export function createCashierRoutes(authAdmin: AuthAdmin): OpenAPIHono<AppEnv> {
  const routes = new OpenAPIHono<AppEnv>();
  const Params = z.object({ companyId: z.string().uuid() });
  const PrincipalParams = Params.extend({ principalId: z.string().uuid() });

  routes.openapi(
    createRoute({
      method: "post", path: "/", tags: ["pos"],
      request: { params: Params, headers: IdempotencyHeader, body: { content: { "application/json": { schema: CreateCashierBody } } } },
      middleware: [requirePermission(PERMISSION["admin.user.manage"], { company: "path:companyId", branch: "body:branchId" })],
      responses: {
        201: { description: "the cashier", content: { "application/json": { schema: CashierSchema } } },
        400: { description: "invalid PIN or body", content: { "application/problem+json": { schema: ProblemSchema } } },
        403: { description: "forbidden", content: { "application/problem+json": { schema: ProblemSchema } } },
        404: { description: "no such employee or branch", content: { "application/problem+json": { schema: ProblemSchema } } },
        409: { description: "already a cashier", content: { "application/problem+json": { schema: ProblemSchema } } },
      },
    }),
    async (c) => {
      const { companyId } = c.req.valid("param");
      const body = c.req.valid("json");

      // 1. Hash first. A PIN outside the band must cost nothing: no auth user, no principal, no row
      //    (the create test asserts exactly that). `hashPin` throws PinFormatError, which problem.ts maps
      //    to ERR_VALIDATION_FAILED through the same branch it uses for TenantContextError.
      const pinHash = await hashPin(body.pin);

      // 2. The Supabase user. Outside the transaction on purpose: it is a network call, and holding a
      //    Postgres transaction across one is how a slow identity provider becomes a database outage.
      //    A user created here whose transaction then rolls back is an orphan in Supabase, not in Dulja —
      //    and the retry collides on `auth_subject`'s unique index, which is the 409 below.
      const user = await authAdmin.createUser(body.email);

      const row = await c.var.withTenant(async (tx) => {
        const [employee] = await tx<{ id: string }[]>`
          select id from app.employee where id = ${body.employeeId} and company_id = ${companyId} and terminated_on is null`;
        if (!employee) throw new AppError("ERR_NOT_FOUND", `employee ${body.employeeId} is not an active employee of company ${companyId}`);
        const [branch] = await tx`select 1 from app.dimension_value where id = ${body.branchId}`;
        if (!branch) throw new AppError("ERR_NOT_FOUND", `branch ${body.branchId} is not visible`);

        // 3. principal + membership in one transaction, insert WITHOUT returning (plan0A:837).
        const [{ id: principalId }] = await tx<{ id: string }[]>`select uuidv7() as id`;
        await tx`insert into app.principal (id, kind, auth_subject, email, display_name)
                 values (${principalId}, 'human', ${user.sub}, ${user.email}, ${body.displayName})`;
        await tx`insert into app.membership (tenant_id, principal_id) values (app.current_tenant(), ${principalId})`;
        await tx`update app.employee set principal_id = ${principalId} where id = ${body.employeeId}`;

        const created = await createCashier(tx, companyId, { principalId, employeeId: body.employeeId, branchId: body.branchId, pinHash });
        // 4. The snapshot is a projection and this is its one writer. The row has no role assignment yet,
        //    so `codes` is empty until admin.role.assign lands one — which fires the trigger itself.
        await tx`select app.refresh_pos_cashier(${principalId})`;
        return (await getCashier(tx, principalId))!;
      });
      return c.json(toCashierDto(row), 201);
    },
  );

  routes.openapi(
    createRoute({
      method: "post", path: "/{principalId}/pin", tags: ["pos"],
      request: { params: PrincipalParams, headers: IdempotencyHeader, body: { content: { "application/json": { schema: SetPinBody } } } },
      middleware: [requirePermission(PERMISSION["pos.cashier.manage_pin"], { company: "path:companyId" })],
      responses: {
        200: { description: "the cashier", content: { "application/json": { schema: CashierSchema } } },
        400: { description: "invalid PIN", content: { "application/problem+json": { schema: ProblemSchema } } },
        404: { description: "no such cashier in a branch you can see", content: { "application/problem+json": { schema: ProblemSchema } } },
      },
    }),
    async (c) => {
      const { principalId } = c.req.valid("param");
      const { pin } = c.req.valid("json");
      const pinHash = await hashPin(pin);
      const row = await c.var.withTenant((tx) => setCashierPin(tx, principalId, pinHash));
      // `branch_isolation` is the control: a cashier at another branch is INVISIBLE, so the update touches
      // zero rows and the answer is 404 — never a 403 that would confirm the cashier exists (test 36).
      if (!row) throw new AppError("ERR_NOT_FOUND", `cashier ${principalId} is not visible`);
      return c.json(toCashierDto(row), 200);
    },
  );

  routes.openapi(
    createRoute({
      method: "get", path: "/", tags: ["pos"],
      request: { params: Params, query: CashierQuery },
      middleware: [requirePermission(PERMISSION["pos.cashier.manage_pin"], { company: "path:companyId" })],
      responses: {
        200: { description: "cashiers", content: { "application/json": { schema: CashierList } } },
        403: { description: "forbidden", content: { "application/problem+json": { schema: ProblemSchema } } },
      },
    }),
    async (c) => {
      const { companyId } = c.req.valid("param");
      const q = c.req.valid("query");
      // No branch filter is applied here on purpose: `branch_isolation` has already reduced the table to
      // the branches this actor is assigned to, and a handler-side filter would be a second control that
      // could disagree with the first (LEGACY-045 is exactly that disagreement).
      const { rows, nextCursor } = await c.var.withTenant((tx) => listCashiers(tx, companyId, {
        limit: q.limit, cursor: q.cursor ? decodeCursor(q.cursor) : undefined,
        branchId: q["filter[branchId][eq]"],
      }));
      return c.json({ items: rows.map(toCashierDto), next_cursor: nextCursor ? encodeCursor({ k: [], id: nextCursor }) : null }, 200);
    },
  );

  routes.openapi(
    createRoute({
      method: "get", path: "/{principalId}", tags: ["pos"],
      request: { params: PrincipalParams },
      middleware: [requirePermission(PERMISSION["pos.cashier.manage_pin"], { company: "path:companyId" })],
      responses: {
        200: { description: "the cashier", content: { "application/json": { schema: CashierSchema } } },
        404: { description: "not visible", content: { "application/problem+json": { schema: ProblemSchema } } },
      },
    }),
    async (c) => {
      const { principalId } = c.req.valid("param");
      const row = await c.var.withTenant((tx) => getCashier(tx, principalId));
      if (!row) throw new AppError("ERR_NOT_FOUND", `cashier ${principalId} is not visible`);
      return c.json(toCashierDto(row), 200);
    },
  );

  routes.openapi(
    createRoute({
      method: "post", path: "/{principalId}/revoke", tags: ["pos"],
      request: { params: PrincipalParams, headers: IdempotencyHeader,
                 body: { content: { "application/json": { schema: z.object({}).openapi("RevokeCashierBody") } } } },
      middleware: [requirePermission(PERMISSION["admin.user.manage"], { company: "path:companyId" })],
      responses: {
        200: { description: "the revoked cashier", content: { "application/json": { schema: CashierSchema } } },
        404: { description: "not visible", content: { "application/problem+json": { schema: ProblemSchema } } },
      },
    }),
    async (c) => {
      const { principalId } = c.req.valid("param");
      // Idempotent by construction (see `revokeCashier`), so a retry is a 200 and never a second tombstone.
      // The ordinary revocation path is 8C's termination trigger; this is the break-glass door.
      const row = await c.var.withTenant((tx) => revokeCashier(tx, principalId));
      if (!row) throw new AppError("ERR_NOT_FOUND", `cashier ${principalId} is not visible`);
      return c.json(toCashierDto(row), 200);
    },
  );

  return routes;
}

/** The wire shape: the snapshot travels, the PIN hash never does. */
function toCashierDto(row: CashierRow) {
  return {
    principalId: row.principalId, employeeId: row.employeeId, branchId: row.branchId,
    pinUpdatedAt: row.pinUpdatedAt, revokedAt: row.revokedAt, revision: row.revision, snapshot: row.snapshot,
  };
}

export const cashierRouteModule = (authAdmin: AuthAdmin): RouteModule => ({
  path: "/v1/companies/:companyId/cashiers",
  router: createCashierRoutes(authAdmin),
  permissions: [PERMISSION["admin.user.manage"], PERMISSION["pos.cashier.manage_pin"]],
  auth: "human",
});
```

`packages/db/src/staff/cashier.ts`'s repository half — `revokeCashier` is the one worth writing out, because its idempotence is what the tombstone trigger's `when` clause depends on:

```ts
/** Idempotent: a second revoke leaves `revoked_at` untouched, so `pos_cashier_tombstone`'s
 *  `when (old.revoked_at is null and new.revoked_at is not null)` fires exactly once. */
export async function revokeCashier(tx: Sql, companyId: string, principalId: string): Promise<CashierRow | null> {
  const rows = await tx<CashierRow[]>`
    update app.pos_cashier set revoked_at = coalesce(revoked_at, now())
     where principal_id = ${principalId}
    returning principal_id as "principalId", employee_id as "employeeId", branch_id as "branchId",
              pin_updated_at as "pinUpdatedAt", revoked_at as "revokedAt", revision::text as revision, snapshot`;
  if (rows.length === 0) return null;
  await tx`select app.refresh_pos_cashier(${principalId})`;     // a revoked cashier's codes go empty at once
  return (await getCashier(tx, principalId))!;
}
```

`apps/api/src/routes/hr/employees.ts` — `POST` creates the `party` (kind `employee`) and the `employee` in **one** `withTenant`, exactly as §4.6.2's closing sentence requires (*"the party row is created first, then the employee row — one transaction"*), and answers `ERR_CONFLICT_UNIQUE` on a duplicate `employeeNumber` through `fromDatabaseError`.

- [ ] **Step 6: Write the onboarding runbook**

`docs/runbooks/cashier-onboarding.md` — six steps an operator follows, each naming the route and the code it needs: create the employee (`hr.employee.manage`); create the cashier with an initial PIN (`admin.user.manage`); assign the `cashier` role with the branch row (`admin.role.assign`, 0B's route); confirm the snapshot filled (`GET …/cashiers/{principalId}`, `snapshot.codes` non-empty); have the till pull the catalogue; on a lost PIN, reset it at the branch (`pos.cashier.manage_pin`). The document also states the two things an operator must **not** do: never share a PIN over any channel — reset it instead; and never delete a cashier — revoke, or record the termination and let 8C's trigger do it.

### Task 9: Sync-apply authority re-verification and the branch limits — acceptance test 37

**The slice's owned acceptance test.** Test 37, verbatim (spec:2455): *"A ticket whose discount exceeds the branch limit, a void, or a blind return is rejected at sync apply unless it carries an override by a principal other than the cashier who holds the permission at that branch; rejected tickets land in needs-attention, never in the ledger."* 6A ships the cashier-authority half against `app.principal_has`; this task adds the **limit** half against `app.branch_pos_policy` and the **second-principal** half through 2B's override door, and completes the test.

**Files:**
- Create: `packages/db/src/sync/limits.ts`, `tests/invariants/support/staff.ts`, `tests/invariants/pos-authority.test.ts`
- Modify: `packages/db/src/sync/apply.ts` (the 6A seam), `packages/db/src/sync/enrolment.ts` (two members on `SyncErrorCode` — amendment A14), `packages/contracts/src/errors.ts` (one `ERRORS` row), `packages/db/src/index.ts`, `tests/invariants/support/pos.ts` (6A's `buildTicket` gains `discount?` and `lines?` — amendment A13)

**Interfaces**

- Consumes: from **`packages/db/src/sync/`** (6A's db-local tier — `packages/db` may not import `@dulja/sync`): `applyBatch`, `TerminalApplyContext`, `TicketInput`, `TicketLineInput`, `TicketEventInput`, `TicketBatchInput`, `TicketOutcome`, `BatchOutcome`, `SyncError`, `SyncErrorCode`; from the test tier: `posFixture`, `PosFixture`, `buildTicket` (6A, §4.17.1), `sharedApi` (0B, §4.17.1), `Ticket`, `TicketEvent` (`@dulja/sync`, in `tests/**` only); `app.needs_attention` (6A), `app.assert_override_authorised` (2B, §4.5.3 — reached through 6A's own `recordOverride` call, never through a second one here), `app.principal_has` (2B), `app.branch_pos_policy` (T3), `app.pos_cashier` (T2), `Money`, `currency`, `RoundingMode`, `postDocument` (1A, reached only through `applyBatch`), `seedBranch` (0D), `seedCashier`, `seedEmployee`, `seedBranchPosPolicy`, `grantCodes`.
- Produces:
  ```ts
  // packages/db/src/sync/limits.ts
  // NOTE the import tier: `TicketInput` and `TicketEventInput` come from `./apply` — 6A's db-local
  // structural twins of §4.10.1 — because 0B's `dependency-rule` pins `packages/db` to ['@dulja/domain']
  // and `@dulja/sync` is not on that list. `@dulja/sync`'s own `Ticket` satisfies `TicketInput`
  // structurally, so the tests may keep passing the wire type.
  export type LimitKind = "line_discount" | "ticket_discount" | "void" | "blind_return";
  export interface LimitRefusal { kind: LimitKind; detail: string; lineNo: number | null }
  export interface TicketVerification {
    /** Permission codes the cashier does NOT hold at this branch. Non-empty ⇒ reject, never override-able. */
    missingCodes: string[];
    /** Limits the ticket exceeds and no override lifts. Non-empty ⇒ reject with ERR_POS_LIMIT_EXCEEDED. */
    refusals: LimitRefusal[];
  }
  export interface TicketContext { companyId: string; branchId: string; currency: string }
  /** Reads `app.branch_pos_policy` and `app.principal_has` inside the caller's transaction. Never the snapshot. */
  export async function verifyTicket(tx: Sql, ctx: TicketContext, ticket: TicketInput): Promise<TicketVerification>;
  /** The codes a ticket's shape requires of its cashier, independent of any policy row. Pure. */
  export function requiredCodesFor(ticket: TicketInput): string[];
  /** Which limit kinds an override `subject` lifts. Pure, and the one place the mapping exists.
   *  Keyed by `string` because `TicketEventInput.subject` is `string` in the db-local twin; an unknown
   *  subject lifts nothing, which is the safe direction. */
  export const OVERRIDE_LIFTS: Readonly<Record<string, readonly LimitKind[]>>;
  ```

  **`TicketVerification` has no `overrides` member, and that is the fix this review pass made.** An earlier draft returned the collected override events so this task's seam could record them — but **6A's `applyBatch` step (h) already records every `TicketEvent`** through `setEventContext` + `recordOverride` before `postDocument` (6A:3674). A second recording here would insert two `document_event` rows for one override, which Step 3's fourth case (`expect(events).toHaveLength(1)`) would catch. `verifyTicket` therefore only *lifts* refusals by subject and returns nothing for the caller to write.

- [ ] **Step 1: Fix the four decisions this task cannot leave to a route file**

**(a) What "a void" is at apply time.** `Ticket.kind` is `sale | return` (§4.10.1) — there is no `void` kind. A void reaches the server as a **`TicketEvent` whose `subject` is `"void"`**, and the amount compared against `max_void_amount` is the ticket's absolute total (`Σ |line.total|`). 6D's `till_event.kind = 'void'` is the *event-stream* record of the same act, arriving on a separate `TillEventMsg`, and **6D checks it against the same column through the same function** — that is why `verifyTicket` is exported rather than inlined.

**(b) What "a blind return" is.** `kind === "return"` with `returnsClientRef === null`: a return with no original ticket. It requires `pos.ticket.return_blind`, `blind_return_allowed = true` **and** a total within `max_blind_return_amount`. A return *with* a `returnsClientRef` needs only `pos.ticket.return`.

**(c) `max_price_override_pct` is enforced at the till, not here — and the reason is recorded rather than hidden.** Checking it server-side means re-deriving each line's catalogue price from `price_list_line` at apply time, which is a **second pricing implementation** beside 6B's (hazard 9), and §4.10.1's `TicketLine` carries no catalogue price to compare against. The till enforces the limit from its mirror and sends a `subject: "price_override"` event; the server verifies **that event's supervisor** exactly as it does the other three. Test 37 names discount, void and blind return and not price override, so nothing this slice owns is weakened. **6B owns the till-side check**; if a later slice wants the server-side one, it needs a catalogue price on the wire and that is a §4.10.1 amendment.

**(d) An authority refusal is never override-able.** A cashier who does not hold `pos.ticket.sell` is not a cashier making an over-limit sale; they are a principal with no selling authority, and a supervisor's override lifts a *limit*, not a missing grant. `missingCodes` and `refusals` are therefore two fields and not one list.

- [ ] **Step 2: Write `tests/invariants/support/staff.ts` (§4.17.1, amendment A9)**

**This file adds a branch, three cashiers and two limit rows to 6A's corpus. It builds no second corpus.** §4.17.1's 6A row makes `posFixture` *"the POS corpus every Phase 6 slice re-uses: a seeded company with items, **a warehouse, stock**, the tax pack, **the `POS_TICKET` type** and an enrolled terminal … **6C, 6D and 7C all consume it**"*. Every one of those is load-bearing here and none of them is 6C's to re-seed: `applyBatch` step (e) resolves the `POS_TICKET` document type and sets `warehouse_id`, the policy's `requires` list names `"warehouse"` (6A:3404), step (g) recomputes tax against the SYNTH pack, and `postDocument` consumes real cost layers. A `staffFixture` that seeded its own company would fail every `accepted` assertion in Step 3 *and* be the duplicate corpus hazard 9 forbids.

```ts
import { applyBatch } from "@dulja/db";
import { seedBranch, seedBranchPosPolicy, seedCashier, seedEmployee, grantCodes,
         uuidv7, type Db, type Seed } from "@dulja/db/test";
import { withTenant } from "@dulja/db";                      // the PRODUCTION 3-argument form (§4.2.2)
import type { Ticket, TicketEvent } from "@dulja/sync";
import type { TicketOutcome } from "@dulja/db";              // 6A's db-local outcome, NOT @dulja/sync's TicketResult
import type { StartedApi } from "../../../apps/api/test/support/app";   // TYPE only, exactly as pos.ts does
import { buildTicket, posFixture, type PosFixture } from "./pos";

export interface StaffFixture {
  /** 6A's corpus, whole. Nothing below re-seeds any part of it. */
  pos: PosFixture;
  seed: Seed;            // === pos.s, carried here because every call site reads it
  branchA: string;       // === pos.s.hq — the branch pos.terminal is enrolled at
  branchB: string;
  terminalA: PosFixture["terminal"];   // === pos.terminal
  cashierA: string;      // a pos_cashier at A: sell, discount, return (+ pos.shift.own, added by 6B's T4)
  cashierB: string;      // a pos_cashier at B: sell
  supervisorA: string;   // a pos_cashier at A holding pos.ticket.override and nothing else
  itemId: string; unitId: string;      // === pos.items["SKU-A"], which HAS stock
}

/**
 * 6A's POS world plus a second branch, three cashiers and one limit row per branch.
 * The single shared staff world for every test in this slice (hazard 9).
 * Branch A's limits: line 10 %, ticket 5 %, void 50.0000, blind returns NOT allowed.
 * Branch B's limits: all zero — the closed defaults, so B refuses whatever A permits.
 *
 * It takes a `StartedApi` because `posFixture` does (it enrols a terminal through the real routes). Tests in
 * the `tests` workspace get one from 0B's `sharedApi()`; `apps/api/test/**` passes its own.
 */
export async function staffFixture(api: StartedApi, name: string): Promise<StaffFixture> {
  const pos = await posFixture(api, name);          // company, items, warehouse, STOCK, tax pack,
  const db = api.db;                                // POS_TICKET, POS_EQUIV_INVOICE, enrolled terminal
  const seed = pos.s;
  const branchA = seed.hq;                          // where pos.terminal is enrolled
  const branchB = await seedBranch(db, seed, { code: `${name}-B` });

  const empA = await seedEmployee(db, seed, { employeeNumber: `${name}-EA`, homeBranchId: branchA });
  const empB = await seedEmployee(db, seed, { employeeNumber: `${name}-EB`, homeBranchId: branchB });
  const empS = await seedEmployee(db, seed, { employeeNumber: `${name}-ES`, homeBranchId: branchA });

  await seedBranchPosPolicy(db, seed, {
    branchId: branchA,
    limits: { maxLineDiscountPct: "0.100000", maxTicketDiscountPct: "0.050000", maxVoidAmount: "50.0000" },
  });
  await seedBranchPosPolicy(db, seed, { branchId: branchB });     // every default at the closed position

  // These are 6C's OWN cashier principals, not `pos.cashierId` / `pos.supervisorId`. 6A's two principals
  // carry 6A's grant lists, which include pos.ticket.void and pos.ticket.return_blind on the supervisor;
  // test 37 needs a supervisor who could NOT have sold the ticket themselves, so it mints its own.
  //
  // ⚠️ **`"pos.shift.own"` joins this array in 6B's Task 4 PR, and is NOT to be removed as foreign.**
  // 6B's T4 Step 5 writes the edit out in full (6b:3067) and lists this file in its own Files block and its
  // `git add`. It cannot land here: `seedCashier` turns `permissions` into a per-cashier role through
  // `grantCodes`, which inserts into `app.role_permission`, whose `permission_code` FK points at
  // `app.permission` (plan0A:1322–1326) — and `pos.shift.own` does not exist until **6B's migration 0063**
  // is applied. Without it 6B's T6 `openShift`, which opens with `requireCode(opts.cashier,
  // PERMISSION["pos.shift.own"])`, throws `ERR_RBAC_FORBIDDEN` inside `openTill` and fails every case of
  // acceptance test 19. It is harmless to this slice: every exact-`codes` snapshot assertion (T7's four
  // `snap.codes` / `snapshotOf` cases, T8's two `body.snapshot.codes` cases) mints its **own** cashier with
  // its own `permissions` list, and none of them reads `cashierA`; `CASHIER_SNAPSHOT_CODES` is a derivation
  // (amendment A5), so 0063's three codes widen both its SQL and its TypeScript spelling by themselves.
  const cashierA = await seedCashier(db, seed, { employeeId: empA, branchId: branchA, pin: "4321",
    permissions: ["pos.ticket.sell", "pos.ticket.discount", "pos.ticket.return"] });
  const cashierB = await seedCashier(db, seed, { employeeId: empB, branchId: branchB, pin: "4321",
    permissions: ["pos.ticket.sell"] });
  const supervisorA = await seedCashier(db, seed, { employeeId: empS, branchId: branchA, pin: "9999",
    permissions: ["pos.ticket.override"] });
  await api.loadPrincipals();                       // the API half of Task 10 signs tokens for these

  const item = pos.items["SKU-A"]!;
  return { pos, seed, branchA, branchB, terminalA: pos.terminal,
           cashierA, cashierB, supervisorA, itemId: item.itemId, unitId: item.baseUnitId };
}

export interface TicketDraft {
  cashierPrincipalId: string;
  kind?: "sale" | "return";
  returnsClientRef?: string | null;
  /** One entry per item line. `sku` defaults to "SKU-A", the stocked item every case uses. */
  lines: { unitPrice: string; quantity: string; discount?: string; sku?: string }[];
  events?: TicketEvent[];
  attendance?: Ticket["attendance"];
}

/**
 * Composes a `Ticket` through **6A's `buildTicket`** and applies it through **6A's `applyBatch`**, returning
 * 6A's own `TicketOutcome`. It computes no tax, negates nothing and asserts nothing: 6A owns the ticket's
 * shape and its accounting, this slice owns its authority, and a helper that re-implemented either would be
 * the second spelling hazard 9 forbids.
 *
 * **Why `buildTicket` and not a hand-built literal.** Two of its rules are invisible until they bite.
 * (1) A `return` carries NEGATIVE quantity, total, tax and tender — `applyBatch` step (b) refuses a return
 *     whose line quantity is `> 0` with `ERR_VALIDATION_FAILED` before any 6C check runs (6A:3670), so both
 *     blind-return cases below would fail for the wrong reason.
 * (2) `taxAmount` is the SYNTH rate extracted from the DISCOUNTED total; step (g) recomputes it and refuses
 *     a disagreement, so a helper that sent zero tax would be refused on every ticket.
 * Amendment A13 is what gives `buildTicket` its `discount` and `lines` options.
 */
export async function applyTicket(f: StaffFixture, draft: TicketDraft): Promise<TicketOutcome> {
  const t = buildTicket(f.pos, {
    kind: draft.kind ?? "sale",
    returnsClientRef: draft.returnsClientRef ?? null,
    cashierPrincipalId: draft.cashierPrincipalId,
    events: draft.events ?? [],
    lines: draft.lines.map((l) => ({ sku: l.sku ?? "SKU-A", quantity: l.quantity,
                                     unitPrice: l.unitPrice, discount: l.discount })),
  });
  // `attendance` is not a `buildTicket` option — it rides on the ticket and belongs to no line — so it is
  // set here rather than widening 6A's helper for one field.
  t.attendance = draft.attendance ?? [];

  const db = f.pos.api.db;
  const ctx = { tenantId: f.seed.tenant, actorId: f.terminalA.principalId, requestId: crypto.randomUUID() };
  // applyBatch is FOUR parameters (6A): (tx, ctx: TenantContext, terminal: TerminalApplyContext, batch).
  // `ctx` carries the tenant and the ACTING principal; `terminal` carries the company, the branch, the
  // terminal id and the terminal's principal. Neither is derivable from the other, which is why there are two.
  const terminal = { terminalId: f.terminalA.terminalId, companyId: f.seed.company,
                     branchId: f.branchA, principalId: f.terminalA.principalId };
  const out = await withTenant(db.rw, ctx, (tx) =>
    applyBatch(tx, ctx, terminal,
               { terminalId: terminal.terminalId, batchId: crypto.randomUUID(),
                 tickets: [t], shifts: [], tillEvents: [] }));
  return out.results[0]!;
}

/**
 * A principal holding `codes` in the company, restricted to `branchId` — or **company-wide when
 * `branchId` is null**, which is `grantCodes` with no `branchIds` and is the case Task 10 needs to prove
 * that scope narrows and never widens. Returns the principal id.
 */
export async function branchActor(
  db: Db, s: Seed, branchId: string | null, codes: readonly string[], label: string,
): Promise<string> {
  const id = await uuidv7(db.admin);
  await withTenant(db.migrator, { tenantId: s.tenant, actorId: s.system, requestId: crypto.randomUUID() },
    async (tx) => {
      await tx`insert into app.principal (id, kind, auth_subject, display_name)
               values (${id}, 'human', ${`sub-${label}-${id}`}, ${label})`;
      await tx`insert into app.membership (tenant_id, principal_id) values (${s.tenant}, ${id})`;
    });
  await grantCodes(db, s, {
    codes, principalId: id, roleCode: `role-${label}-${id.slice(-8)}`,
    ...(branchId === null ? {} : { branchIds: [branchId] }),
  });
  return id;
}
```

**`applyTicket` makes the terminal the acting principal**, which is the whole point: `ctx.actorId` is `f.terminalA.principalId`, so `document.created_by` and every stamped column name the terminal and `attributed_to` names the cashier (hazard 19). Passing the cashier there would make every legacy-044 assertion in Task 10 pass for the wrong reason. **`ctx` is passed twice on purpose** — `withTenant` sets the session settings, `applyBatch` hands the same context to `recordOverride` and `postDocument`; 6A's own `apply.test.ts` does exactly this.

**The `tests` workspace uses the production `withTenant(sql, ctx, fn)`** (assumption fact 15). The 4-argument test form belongs to `packages/db/test` and appears nowhere in this file.

**Amendment A13's two `buildTicket` options, written out**, so the executor edits 6A's helper once and correctly:

```ts
// tests/invariants/support/pos.ts — inside buildTicket, replacing the single-line construction.
// Both options are additive: with neither supplied the previous single-line behaviour is unchanged.
  const specs = opts.lines ?? [{ sku: opts.sku, quantity: opts.quantity, unitPrice: opts.unitPrice,
                                 discount: opts.discount }];
  const sign = (opts.kind ?? "sale") === "return" ? -1 : 1;
  const lines = specs.map((spec, i) => {
    const item = f.items[spec.sku ?? "SKU-A"]!;
    const qty = spec.quantity ?? "1.000000";
    const price = Money.of(spec.unitPrice ?? "50.0000", cur);
    const discount = Money.of(spec.discount ?? "0.0000", cur);
    // The discount comes OFF before the tax is extracted: applyBatch step (g) recomputes the tax from the
    // stored line and refuses a disagreement, so taxing the gross would refuse every discounted ticket.
    const net = price.multiply(qty, MODE).subtract(discount);
    const tax = synthTaxOf(net);                       // the existing extraction, unchanged
    return { lineNo: i + 1, itemId: item.itemId, lotId: null, unitId: item.baseUnitId,
             quantity: signed(qty, sign), unitPrice: price.toJSON(), discount: signedMoney(discount, sign),
             taxCodeId: f.taxCodes.SYNTH_STD!, taxAmount: signedMoney(tax, sign),
             total: signedMoney(net, sign) };
  });
```

```ts
import { beforeAll, describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { withTenant, type Db } from "@dulja/db";                 // the production 3-argument form
import { seedCashier, seedEmployee } from "@dulja/db/test";
import { sharedApi } from "../support/api";                       // 0B's memoised StartedApi (§4.17.1)
import { staffFixture, applyTicket, branchActor, type StaffFixture } from "./support/staff";

let db: Db;
let f: StaffFixture;
let ledgerAfterAccepted: number;

/** Every ledger row in this fixture's OWN tenant. `posFixture` seeds a fresh tenant per call, so the count
 *  is this test's alone even though the API instance is shared. */
const ctx = () => ({ tenantId: f.seed.tenant, actorId: f.seed.system, requestId: crypto.randomUUID() });
const countLedger = () => withTenant(db.rw, ctx(), (tx) =>
  tx<{ n: number }[]>`select count(*)::int as n from app.ledger_entry`).then((r) => r[0]!.n);
const countLedgerFor = (documentId: string) => withTenant(db.rw, ctx(), (tx) =>
  tx<{ n: number }[]>`select count(*)::int as n from app.ledger_entry where document_id = ${documentId}`)
  .then((r) => r[0]!.n);

beforeAll(async () => {
  // `sharedApi()` is 0B's registered way for the `tests` workspace to get a running API (§4.17.1); it is
  // memoised and is NOT stopped here — every regression file in this workspace shares it, and this suite
  // works in its own freshly seeded tenant.
  const { api } = await sharedApi();
  db = api.db;
  f = await staffFixture(api, "auth37");
}, 300_000);

describe("Acceptance test 37 — POS authority re-verified server-side (spec:2455; LEGACY-027, 048)", () => {
  it("a within-limit sale is accepted and posts", async () => {
    const r = await applyTicket(f, {
      cashierPrincipalId: f.cashierA,
      lines: [{ unitPrice: "100.0000", quantity: "1.000000", discount: "5.0000" }],   // 5% ≤ the 10% line limit
    });
    expect(r.status).toBe("accepted");
    expect(r.documentId).not.toBeNull();
    // A posted POS_TICKET writes SEVERAL ledger_entry rows, never one: app.ledger_entry is one row per
    // posting line with separate debit/credit columns and a CHECK that only one side is non-zero
    // (plan0A:1917-1921), so a balanced ticket is at minimum a cash debit and a revenue credit, and in
    // practice four once COGS and inventory post. Every count below is therefore an equality against a
    // CAPTURED value or a per-document count — never an arithmetic guess.
    expect(await countLedgerFor(r.documentId!)).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(2);
    ledgerAfterAccepted = await countLedger();
    expect(ledgerAfterAccepted).toBeGreaterThan(0);
  });

  it("an over-limit LINE discount is rejected with ERR_POS_LIMIT_EXCEEDED and nothing reaches the ledger", async () => {
    const r = await applyTicket(f, {
      cashierPrincipalId: f.cashierA,
      lines: [{ unitPrice: "100.0000", quantity: "1.000000", discount: "25.0000" }],  // 25% > 10%
    });
    expect(r.status).toBe("rejected");
    expect(r.error?.code).toBe("ERR_POS_LIMIT_EXCEEDED");
    expect(r.error?.detail).toContain("line 1");
    expect(r.error?.detail).toContain("0.100000");
    expect(r.documentId).toBeNull();
    expect(await countLedger()).toBe(ledgerAfterAccepted);   // "never in the ledger", as an equality
  });

  it("the rejected ticket is in needs_attention, with its payload and its code", async () => {
    const rows = await withTenant(db.rw, ctx(), (tx) => tx<
      { error_code: string; branch_id: string; resolved_at: string | null }[]
    >`select error_code, branch_id::text as branch_id, resolved_at
        from app.needs_attention order by created_at desc limit 1`);
    expect(rows[0]).toMatchObject({ error_code: "ERR_POS_LIMIT_EXCEEDED", branch_id: f.branchA, resolved_at: null });
  });

  it("the SAME ticket with a supervisor override is accepted, and records ONE override event", async () => {
    const r = await applyTicket(f, {
      cashierPrincipalId: f.cashierA,
      lines: [{ unitPrice: "100.0000", quantity: "1.000000", discount: "25.0000" }],
      events: [{ kind: "override", code: "pos.ticket.override", subject: "discount",
                 supervisorPrincipalId: f.supervisorA, reason: "manager approved" }],
    });
    expect(r.status).toBe("accepted");
    const events = await withTenant(db.rw, ctx(), (tx) => tx<
      { override_code: string; actor_id: string; attributed_to: string; reason: string }[]
    >`select override_code, actor_id, attributed_to, reason from app.document_event
        where document_id = ${r.documentId!} and event_kind = 'override'`);
    // ONE row, not two. 6A's applyBatch step (h) is the only writer of this event; Step 5's seam adds no
    // recordOverride call of its own, and this length assertion is what would catch it if somebody did.
    expect(events).toHaveLength(1);
    // hazard 19 / LEGACY-044: the TERMINAL acted, the SUPERVISOR is attributed.
    expect(events[0]).toMatchObject({
      override_code: "pos.ticket.override",
      actor_id: f.terminalA.principalId,
      attributed_to: f.supervisorA,
      reason: "discount: manager approved",
    });
    ledgerAfterAccepted = await countLedger();      // re-capture: this ticket posted too
  });

  it("the cashier cannot be their own supervisor — ERR_OVERRIDE_NOT_AUTHORISED (the second-principal clause)", async () => {
    const r = await applyTicket(f, {
      cashierPrincipalId: f.cashierA,
      lines: [{ unitPrice: "100.0000", quantity: "1.000000", discount: "25.0000" }],
      events: [{ kind: "override", code: "pos.ticket.override", subject: "discount",
                 supervisorPrincipalId: f.cashierA, reason: "self" }],
    });
    expect(r.status).toBe("rejected");
    expect(r.error?.code).toBe("ERR_OVERRIDE_NOT_AUTHORISED");
    expect(await countLedger()).toBe(ledgerAfterAccepted);   // unchanged since the last accepted ticket
  });

  it("a supervisor who does not hold pos.ticket.override AT THIS BRANCH cannot lift the limit", async () => {
    const outsider = await branchActor(db, f.seed, f.branchB, ["pos.ticket.override"], "sup-b");
    const r = await applyTicket(f, {
      cashierPrincipalId: f.cashierA,
      lines: [{ unitPrice: "100.0000", quantity: "1.000000", discount: "25.0000" }],
      events: [{ kind: "override", code: "pos.ticket.override", subject: "discount",
                 supervisorPrincipalId: outsider, reason: "wrong branch" }],
    });
    expect(r.status).toBe("rejected");
    expect(r.error?.code).toBe("ERR_OVERRIDE_NOT_AUTHORISED");
  });

  it("an over-limit TICKET discount is caught even when every line is within the line limit", async () => {
    const r = await applyTicket(f, {
      cashierPrincipalId: f.cashierA,
      // three lines at 8% each: every line ≤ 10%, but the ticket is 8% > the 5% ticket limit
      lines: Array.from({ length: 3 }, () => ({ unitPrice: "100.0000", quantity: "1.000000", discount: "8.0000" })),
    });
    expect(r.status).toBe("rejected");
    expect(r.error?.detail).toContain("ticket discount");
  });

  it("a VOID over max_void_amount needs an override, and the cashier needs pos.ticket.void", async () => {
    const noVoid = await applyTicket(f, {
      cashierPrincipalId: f.cashierA,     // holds sell, discount, return — NOT void
      lines: [{ unitPrice: "80.0000", quantity: "1.000000" }],
      events: [{ kind: "override", code: "pos.ticket.override", subject: "void",
                 supervisorPrincipalId: f.supervisorA, reason: "wrong item" }],
    });
    expect(noVoid.status).toBe("rejected");
    expect(noVoid.error?.code).toBe("ERR_RBAC_FORBIDDEN");
    expect(noVoid.error?.detail).toContain("pos.ticket.void");   // an authority gap is NOT override-able
  });

  it("a BLIND return is refused when the branch does not allow one, and accepted with an override", async () => {
    const blind = { cashierPrincipalId: f.cashierA, kind: "return" as const, returnsClientRef: null,
                    lines: [{ unitPrice: "30.0000", quantity: "1.000000" }] };
    const refused = await applyTicket(f, blind);
    expect(refused.status).toBe("rejected");
    expect(refused.error?.code).toBe("ERR_RBAC_FORBIDDEN");      // cashierA holds no pos.ticket.return_blind

    // `branchActor` alone is NOT enough here. `verifyTicket`'s FIRST check is "is this principal a live
    // pos_cashier at this branch"; a principal with a role assignment and no `pos_cashier` row is refused
    // with ERR_RBAC_FORBIDDEN before any limit is read, and the two assertions below would pass for the
    // wrong reason and then fail on the override case. `seedCashier` is what makes it a cashier.
    const empBlind = await seedEmployee(db, f.seed, { employeeNumber: "auth37-EBL", homeBranchId: f.branchA });
    const cashierBlind = await seedCashier(db, f.seed, {
      employeeId: empBlind, branchId: f.branchA, pin: "5555",
      permissions: ["pos.ticket.return", "pos.ticket.return_blind"],
    });
    const stillRefused = await applyTicket(f, { ...blind, cashierPrincipalId: cashierBlind });
    expect(stillRefused.status).toBe("rejected");
    expect(stillRefused.error?.code).toBe("ERR_POS_LIMIT_EXCEEDED");
    expect(stillRefused.error?.detail).toContain("blind return");
    const allowed = await applyTicket(f, { ...blind, cashierPrincipalId: cashierBlind,
      events: [{ kind: "override", code: "pos.ticket.override", subject: "blind_return",
                 supervisorPrincipalId: f.supervisorA, reason: "customer known" }] });
    expect(allowed.status).toBe("accepted");
  });

  it("a return WITH an original ticket needs no blind-return authority", async () => {
    const original = await applyTicket(f, { cashierPrincipalId: f.cashierA, lines: [{ unitPrice: "20.0000", quantity: "1.000000" }] });
    expect(original.status).toBe("accepted");
    const r = await applyTicket(f, { cashierPrincipalId: f.cashierA, kind: "return",
      returnsClientRef: original.clientRef, lines: [{ unitPrice: "20.0000", quantity: "1.000000" }] });
    expect(r.status).toBe("accepted");
  });

  it("a limit is read from branch_pos_policy and NOT from the snapshot — a stale snapshot cannot widen it", async () => {
    // Widen only the snapshot, exactly as a compromised or lagging till would see it.
    await withTenant(db.migrator, ctx(), (tx) => tx`
      update app.pos_cashier
         set snapshot = jsonb_set(snapshot, '{limits,maxLineDiscountPct}', '"0.900000"')
       where principal_id = ${f.cashierA}`);
    const r = await applyTicket(f, {
      cashierPrincipalId: f.cashierA,
      lines: [{ unitPrice: "100.0000", quantity: "1.000000", discount: "25.0000" }],
    });
    expect(r.status).toBe("rejected");
    expect(r.error?.code).toBe("ERR_POS_LIMIT_EXCEEDED");
  });

  it("a revoked cashier sells nothing, whatever the till's mirror still says", async () => {
    // The cashier must be revoked AT BRANCH A: `applyTicket` always applies through `terminalA`, and
    // `verifyTicket` looks the row up by (principal, company, BRANCH). Revoking `f.cashierB` — a branch-B
    // cashier — would match no row at branch A and the detail would read "no pos_cashier row …", not
    // "revoked". A fresh branch-A cashier is revoked instead, so `f.cashierA` stays usable for the
    // cases after this one.
    const empRev = await seedEmployee(db, f.seed, { employeeNumber: "auth37-ERV", homeBranchId: f.branchA });
    const doomed = await seedCashier(db, f.seed, { employeeId: empRev, branchId: f.branchA, pin: "6666",
      permissions: ["pos.ticket.sell"] });
    await withTenant(db.rw, { tenantId: f.seed.tenant, actorId: f.seed.human, requestId: crypto.randomUUID() },
      (tx) => tx`update app.pos_cashier set revoked_at = now() where principal_id = ${doomed}`);
    const r = await applyTicket(f, { cashierPrincipalId: doomed, lines: [{ unitPrice: "10.0000", quantity: "1.000000" }] });
    expect(r.status).toBe("rejected");
    expect(r.error?.code).toBe("ERR_RBAC_FORBIDDEN");
    expect(r.error?.detail).toContain("revoked");
  });

  it("a cashier of ANOTHER branch is refused at this terminal, and the reason says so", async () => {
    const r = await applyTicket(f, { cashierPrincipalId: f.cashierB, lines: [{ unitPrice: "10.0000", quantity: "1.000000" }] });
    expect(r.status).toBe("rejected");
    expect(r.error?.code).toBe("ERR_RBAC_FORBIDDEN");
    expect(r.error?.detail).toContain("no pos_cashier row");
  });
});
```

```ts
import type { Sql } from "postgres";
import { Money, currency, type CurrencyCode } from "@dulja/domain";
// 6A's DB-LOCAL twins, never `@dulja/sync`: 0B's `dependency-rule` pins `packages/db` to
// ['@dulja/domain'] (6A:1772 states the same constraint for its own `SyncError`). `@dulja/sync`'s
// `Ticket` satisfies `TicketInput` structurally, so the test tier may keep passing the wire type.
import type { TicketInput, TicketLineInput } from "./apply";

export type LimitKind = "line_discount" | "ticket_discount" | "void" | "blind_return";

export interface LimitRefusal { kind: LimitKind; detail: string; lineNo: number | null }

export interface TicketVerification {
  missingCodes: string[];
  refusals: LimitRefusal[];
}

export interface TicketContext { companyId: string; branchId: string; currency: string }

/**
 * Which limit kinds an override subject lifts. The ONE place this mapping exists: 6D's till events read it
 * for the same `void` subject, and two copies would let a void be lifted by a discount approval.
 * `price_override` lifts nothing here — that limit is enforced at the till (see the task's decision (c)),
 * and 6A's applyBatch step (h) records the event either way, so the supervisor is on the document.
 * Keyed by `string` because `TicketEventInput.subject` is `string` in the db-local twin; an unrecognised
 * subject lifts NOTHING, which is the safe direction — a till cannot invent a word that widens a limit.
 */
export const OVERRIDE_LIFTS: Readonly<Record<string, readonly LimitKind[]>> = {
  discount: ["line_discount", "ticket_discount"],
  void: ["void"],
  blind_return: ["blind_return"],
  price_override: [],
};

const MODE = "HALF_AWAY_FROM_ZERO" as const;   // hazard 23's mode; 6B computes the same allowance the same way

const isBlindReturn = (t: TicketInput) => t.kind === "return" && t.returnsClientRef === null;
const hasVoidEvent = (t: TicketInput) => t.events.some((e) => e.subject === "void");
// Through `Money`, never a string compare: a till that formats zero as "0.00" or "0" would otherwise be
// told it needs `pos.ticket.discount` for a ticket carrying no discount at all, and would be refused.
const hasDiscount = (t: TicketInput, c: CurrencyCode) =>
  t.lines.some((l) => !Money.of(l.discount.amount, c).abs().isZero());

/** The codes a ticket's SHAPE requires of its cashier, before any policy row is read. Pure. */
export function requiredCodesFor(ticket: TicketInput): string[] {
  const cur = currency(ticket.currency);
  const codes = new Set<string>([ticket.kind === "return" ? "pos.ticket.return" : "pos.ticket.sell"]);
  if (hasDiscount(ticket, cur)) codes.add("pos.ticket.discount");
  if (isBlindReturn(ticket)) codes.add("pos.ticket.return_blind");
  if (hasVoidEvent(ticket)) codes.add("pos.ticket.void");
  return [...codes].sort();
}

// `.abs()` on both factors: a RETURN carries a negative quantity and a negative total (6A step (b) enforces
// it), and a limit is a magnitude. Nothing in this file reads a sign to decide a direction (§4.4.2).
const gross = (l: TicketLineInput, c: CurrencyCode) =>
  Money.of(l.unitPrice.amount, c).abs().multiply(String(l.quantity).replace("-", ""), MODE);

/**
 * Re-verifies a ticket against the SERVER's authority and the branch's limit ROW (spec:1324, test 37).
 * It never reads `pos_cashier.snapshot`: the snapshot is the till's copy and may lag or be tampered with.
 * Every query runs on the caller's `tx`, inside `applyBatch`'s per-ticket savepoint.
 */
export async function verifyTicket(tx: Sql, ctx: TicketContext, ticket: TicketInput): Promise<TicketVerification> {
  const cur = currency(ctx.currency);
  const missingCodes: string[] = [];
  const refusals: LimitRefusal[] = [];

  // 1. Is this principal a live cashier at THIS branch? A revoked one authorises nothing, whatever the
  //    till's mirror still says (spec:2090, test 34).
  const [cashier] = await tx<{ revoked: boolean }[]>`
    select (revoked_at is not null) as revoked from app.pos_cashier
     where principal_id = ${ticket.cashierPrincipalId} and company_id = ${ctx.companyId} and branch_id = ${ctx.branchId}`;
  if (!cashier) {
    missingCodes.push(`no pos_cashier row for ${ticket.cashierPrincipalId} at this branch`);
    return { missingCodes, refusals };
  }
  if (cashier.revoked) {
    missingCodes.push(`cashier ${ticket.cashierPrincipalId} is revoked`);
    return { missingCodes, refusals };
  }

  // 2. Authority, from app.principal_has and nothing else (§4.5.3). An authority gap is NEVER override-able.
  for (const code of requiredCodesFor(ticket)) {
    const [{ ok }] = await tx<{ ok: boolean }[]>`
      select app.principal_has(${ticket.cashierPrincipalId}, ${code}, ${ctx.companyId}, ${ctx.branchId}) as ok`;
    if (!ok) missingCodes.push(code);
  }
  if (missingCodes.length > 0) return { missingCodes, refusals };

  // 3. Limits, from the ROW. A branch with no row authorises nothing above zero — the migration's defaults
  //    and a missing row refuse identically, which is why `coalesce` is used and not a null-check branch.
  //    **Every fallback carries its column's own type.** An untyped `0` has scale 0, so `coalesce(x, 0)::text`
  //    returns "0" where a real row returns "0.0000" — the JS fallback below would then disagree with the SQL
  //    for exactly the branches that have no row, which are the ones this check most has to get right.
  const [policy] = await tx<{
    maxLineDiscountPct: string; maxTicketDiscountPct: string; maxVoidAmount: string;
    blindReturnAllowed: boolean; maxBlindReturnAmount: string;
  }[]>`
    select coalesce(max_line_discount_pct,   0::numeric(9,6))::text  as "maxLineDiscountPct",
           coalesce(max_ticket_discount_pct, 0::numeric(9,6))::text  as "maxTicketDiscountPct",
           coalesce(max_void_amount,         0::numeric(19,4))::text as "maxVoidAmount",
           coalesce(blind_return_allowed,    false)                  as "blindReturnAllowed",
           coalesce(max_blind_return_amount, 0::numeric(19,4))::text as "maxBlindReturnAmount"
      from app.branch_pos_policy where company_id = ${ctx.companyId} and branch_id = ${ctx.branchId}`;
  const limits = policy ?? {
    maxLineDiscountPct: "0.000000", maxTicketDiscountPct: "0.000000", maxVoidAmount: "0.0000",
    blindReturnAllowed: false, maxBlindReturnAmount: "0.0000",
  };

  let totalGross = Money.zero(cur);
  let totalDiscount = Money.zero(cur);
  for (const line of ticket.lines) {
    const g = gross(line, cur);
    const d = Money.of(line.discount.amount, cur).abs();
    totalGross = totalGross.add(g);
    totalDiscount = totalDiscount.add(d);
    // The allowance is computed rather than a ratio derived: `Money.multiply` takes the 6-dp fraction
    // directly, so no division, no float and no second rounding mode enter the comparison.
    const allowed = g.multiply(limits.maxLineDiscountPct, MODE);
    if (d.compare(allowed) > 0) {
      refusals.push({
        kind: "line_discount", lineNo: line.lineNo,
        detail: `line ${line.lineNo} discount ${d.toString()} exceeds ${limits.maxLineDiscountPct} of ${g.toString()} (${allowed.toString()})`,
      });
    }
  }

  const ticketAllowance = totalGross.multiply(limits.maxTicketDiscountPct, MODE);
  if (totalDiscount.compare(ticketAllowance) > 0) {
    refusals.push({
      kind: "ticket_discount", lineNo: null,
      detail: `ticket discount ${totalDiscount.toString()} exceeds ${limits.maxTicketDiscountPct} of ${totalGross.toString()} (${ticketAllowance.toString()})`,
    });
  }

  const absoluteTotal = ticket.lines.reduce((sum, l) => sum.add(Money.of(l.total.amount, cur).abs()), Money.zero(cur));

  if (hasVoidEvent(ticket)) {
    const cap = Money.of(limits.maxVoidAmount, cur);
    if (absoluteTotal.compare(cap) > 0) {
      refusals.push({ kind: "void", lineNo: null, detail: `void of ${absoluteTotal.toString()} exceeds max_void_amount ${cap.toString()}` });
    }
  }

  if (isBlindReturn(ticket)) {
    if (!limits.blindReturnAllowed) {
      refusals.push({ kind: "blind_return", lineNo: null, detail: `blind return: this branch does not allow one (blind_return_allowed = false)` });
    } else {
      const cap = Money.of(limits.maxBlindReturnAmount, cur);
      if (absoluteTotal.compare(cap) > 0) {
        refusals.push({ kind: "blind_return", lineNo: null, detail: `blind return of ${absoluteTotal.toString()} exceeds max_blind_return_amount ${cap.toString()}` });
      }
    }
  }

  // 4. Overrides lift refusals by SUBJECT — and that is ALL this function does with them. It records
  //    nothing: 6A's applyBatch step (h) already calls setEventContext + recordOverride for every
  //    TicketEvent, and 2B's `assert_override_authorised` trigger is the control (§4.5.3, spec:2173).
  //    An unrecognised subject lifts nothing (`?? []`), so a till cannot invent a word that widens a limit.
  const lifted = new Set<LimitKind>();
  for (const e of ticket.events) {
    if (e.kind !== "override") continue;
    for (const kind of OVERRIDE_LIFTS[e.subject] ?? []) lifted.add(kind);
  }
  return { missingCodes, refusals: refusals.filter((r) => !lifted.has(r.kind)) };
}
```

**Why the supervisor is not verified here, and why nothing is recorded here.** 2B's `assert_override_authorised` already resolves `coalesce(attributed_to, actor_id)`, calls `app.principal_has(p, override_code, company, branch)` and raises `ERR_OVERRIDE_NOT_AUTHORISED` when the principal is the document's creator or its `attributed_to` (§4.5.3). Re-implementing that check in `limits.ts` would give the system two answers to one question, and the trigger's is the one that binds. **And 6A's `applyBatch` step (h) is already the writer**: for each `TicketEvent` it runs `setEventContext(sp, { reason: \`${subject}: ${reason}\`, attributedTo: supervisorPrincipalId })` then `recordOverride(sp, ctx, { documentId, code: "pos.ticket.override", reason, attributedTo: supervisorPrincipalId })`, inside the same savepoint and before `postDocument` (6A:3674). The two tests that assert `ERR_OVERRIDE_NOT_AUTHORISED` are therefore testing **2B's trigger through 6A's call, on this slice's path**, which is exactly what test 37's "by a principal other than the cashier who holds the permission at that branch" clause means.

- [ ] **Step 5: Extend 6A's `applyBatch` — the seam, in the order that matters**

**Where exactly.** Inside 6A's per-ticket savepoint, **after step (f)** (the document and its lines exist, so `documentId` is real and `postDocument` has something to post) and **before step (h)** (6A's `recordOverride` loop). Two statements, and nothing else:

```ts
      // ── 6C: server-side re-verification (test 37; spec:1324, 2455) ────────────────────────────────
      // `terminal` is applyBatch's THIRD parameter (TerminalApplyContext: terminalId, companyId, branchId,
      // principalId). `ctx` is the TenantContext and carries NEITHER a company nor a branch — reading
      // `ctx.companyId` here is the mistake this comment exists to prevent.
      const verdict = await verifyTicket(
        sp, { companyId: terminal.companyId, branchId: terminal.branchId, currency: ticket.currency }, ticket);
      if (verdict.missingCodes.length > 0) {
        throw new SyncError("ERR_RBAC_FORBIDDEN",
          `cashier ${ticket.cashierPrincipalId} lacks ${verdict.missingCodes.join(", ")} at branch ${terminal.branchId}`);
      }
      if (verdict.refusals.length > 0) {
        throw new SyncError("ERR_POS_LIMIT_EXCEEDED", verdict.refusals.map((r) => r.detail).join("; "));
      }
```

**No `recordOverride` call is added here, and adding one is a defect.** 6A's step (h) already runs `setEventContext` + `recordOverride(sp, ctx, …)` for every `TicketEvent`, in this same savepoint and before `postDocument` (6A:3674), and 2B's `assert_override_authorised` is what authorises it. A second loop here would insert **two** `document_event` rows for one override; Step 3's fourth case asserts `toHaveLength(1)` precisely so that mistake cannot ship. The ordering still works out: a refusal no supervisor lifted throws *before* step (h) and the savepoint rolls back, so nothing is recorded for a ticket that never posts; a refusal a supervisor did lift passes through, and step (h) records the supervisor a moment later.

**`SyncError` is 6A's class and this task widens its code union by two — amendment A14.** 6A ships `export class SyncError extends Error { constructor(readonly code: SyncErrorCode, readonly detail: string) }` in `packages/db/src/sync/enrolment.ts` and states the reason no `AppError` is used: `dependency-rule` pins `packages/db` to `['@dulja/domain']` (6A:1772). `SyncErrorCode` is a **literal subset of `ErrCode`**, so adding `ERR_RBAC_FORBIDDEN` and `ERR_POS_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` — both rows of `ERRORS` — preserves that property exactly, and `applyBatch`'s existing classifier turns a `SyncError` into `TicketOutcome.error.code` with no change. **`PostingErrorCode` is not touched, and no third error type is created in `packages/db/src/sync/`.**

**The attendance path is not here.** It shipped in **Task 4**, with the migration whose existence turns 6A's `to_regclass` guard false (amendment A8). Adding it here as well would insert every clock-in twice.
