### Task 10: Test 36's branch-scoping clauses, the six regression appends, and this slice's pinned lists

**Test 36's second half is this slice's** (§6.1): *"a `branch_manager` at branch A cannot read or write documents, shifts, stock, attendance or tickets of branch B through any endpoint, filter or export; … 'who can reset a PIN at branch B' is one SQL query."* 0D owns the catalogue-integrity half and re-runs it; this task owns the scoping half, at the row level **and** through the API, because *"any endpoint, filter or export"* is a claim a row-level test alone does not make.

**Files:**
- Create: `tests/invariants/branch-scoping.test.ts` (the row-level half), **`apps/api/test/routes/branch-scoping.test.ts`** (the endpoint half — Step 2 says why it cannot live in `tests/`), `tests/invariants/phase-6c-pinned.test.ts`
- Modify: `tests/regression/legacy-025.test.ts`, `legacy-026.test.ts`, `legacy-028.test.ts`, `legacy-033.test.ts`, `legacy-044.test.ts`, `legacy-045.test.ts` (append one `describe("… — 6C", …)` block each; **never edit an earlier block** — §3.7.3)

**Interfaces**

- Consumes: `staffFixture`, `applyTicket`, `branchActor` (T9), `sharedDb`, `thinRegression`, `sharedApi`, `grantAdmin`, `fullRegression`, `problemOf`, `sourceFiles`, `REPO_ROOT` (0B, §4.17.1), `withTenant` (production, §4.2.2), `uuidv7`, `seedWarehouse` (3A), `seedTerminal` (6A — the branch-B `pos_terminal`, and it already inserts as `s.system`), `lintSchema`, `ERRORS`, `ROUTES`, `PROBES`, `PERMISSIONS`, `JOBS`, `startApi`.
- Produces: no exported symbol. `phase-6c-pinned.test.ts` is a file of equalities.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing branch-scoping test**

`tests/invariants/branch-scoping.test.ts`:

```ts
import { beforeAll, describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { withTenant, type Db } from "@dulja/db";               // the production 3-argument form
import { uuidv7, seedWarehouse, seedTerminal } from "@dulja/db/test";
import { createHash } from "node:crypto";
import { sharedApi } from "../support/api";                     // 0B's memoised StartedApi (§4.17.1)
import { staffFixture, branchActor, type StaffFixture } from "./support/staff";

let db: Db;
let f: StaffFixture;
let managerA: string;
let managerB: string;

/** Every read below runs as a named principal; this is the shape the production `withTenant` takes. */
const as = (actorId: string) => ({ tenantId: f.seed.tenant, actorId, requestId: crypto.randomUUID() });

/** Every table branch scoping must cover, and the id of a row this fixture put in BRANCH B. */
const TABLES: { table: string; via: string }[] = [
  { table: "document",           via: "branch_id (Form A)" },
  { table: "pos_terminal",       via: "branch_id (Form A)" },
  { table: "pos_cashier",        via: "branch_id (Form A)" },
  { table: "branch_pos_policy",  via: "branch_id (Form A)" },
  { table: "attendance_event",   via: "branch_id (Form A)" },
  { table: "terminal_enrolment", via: "branch_id (Form A, 6A's table)" },
  { table: "needs_attention",    via: "branch_id (Form A, 6A's table)" },
  { table: "employee",           via: "home_branch_id (Form A′)" },
  { table: "warehouse",          via: "branch_id (Form A″)" },
  { table: "inventory_movement", via: "warehouse_id → warehouse.branch_id (Form C)" },
  { table: "stock_on_hand",      via: "warehouse_id → warehouse.branch_id (Form C)" },
  { table: "cost_layer",         via: "warehouse_id → warehouse.branch_id (Form C)" },
  { table: "cost_consumption",   via: "movement_id → warehouse → branch (Form C)" },
  { table: "stock_valuation",    via: "warehouse_id → warehouse.branch_id (Form C)" },
];

beforeAll(async () => {
  const { api } = await sharedApi();    // memoised and shared; NOT stopped here (§4.17.1, 0B's file)
  db = api.db;
  f = await staffFixture(api, "scope36");
  managerA = await branchActor(db, f.seed, f.branchA, ["hr.employee.read", "inventory.stock.read", "accounting.ledger.read", "pos.cashier.manage_pin"], "mgr-a");
  managerB = await branchActor(db, f.seed, f.branchB, ["hr.employee.read", "inventory.stock.read", "accounting.ledger.read", "pos.cashier.manage_pin"], "mgr-b");
  await seedBranchBWorld();            // one row of every TABLES entry, all at branch B
}, 300_000);

/**
 * One row of every table in TABLES, all at branch B, written as the SYSTEM principal — which passes every
 * `branch_isolation` form (R8, spec:2179) and is therefore the only actor that can seed both branches in
 * one pass. `staffFixture` already created B's cashier, employee and policy row; this adds the seven that
 * need a warehouse, a document, a terminal or a 6A table.
 *
 * **`staffFixture` enrols exactly ONE terminal and it is at branch A** (`terminalA === pos.terminal`, and
 * `posFixture` enrols it at `s.hq`). Without the `seedTerminal` call below, `select count(*) from
 * app.pos_terminal` as branch B's manager is 0 and the `pos_terminal` case fails on its own
 * `expect(fromB).toBeGreaterThan(0)` guard — the guard that exists so the zero-rows assertion beside it
 * cannot pass vacuously. 6A's `seedTerminal` already inserts as `s.system` (6a:2611), so it needs no
 * wrapper here, and it creates the terminal's principal and R8 role assignment with the row.
 */
async function seedBranchBWorld(): Promise<void> {
  const s = f.seed;
  const whB = await seedWarehouse(db, s, { code: "WH-SCOPE-B", branchId: f.branchB });
  // A live pos_terminal at B. Its `code` is deliberately NOT the `terminal_code` the enrolment below uses:
  // `app.pos_terminal` and `app.terminal_enrolment` carry separate unique keys and one literal serving
  // both would read as if the enrolment had been redeemed into this row, which it has not.
  await seedTerminal(db, s, { code: "TB-SCOPE", branchId: f.branchB });
  await withTenant(db.rw, as(s.system), async (tx) => {
    // a document at B — its id is reused as the source document of the movement below, because
    // inventory_movement.document_id is NOT NULL with a composite FK to app.document (§4.7.3).
    const [doc] = await tx<{ id: string }[]>`
      insert into app.document (tenant_id, company_id, document_type_id, series, fiscal_period_id,
                                effective_date, currency, branch_id)
      values (${s.tenant}, ${s.company}, ${s.docType}, 'JV', ${s.period}, '2026-01-15', 'MYR', ${f.branchB})
      returning id`;
    await tx`insert into app.attendance_event (tenant_id, company_id, employee_id, branch_id, kind, occurred_at)
             select ${s.tenant}, ${s.company}, e.id, ${f.branchB}, 'clock_in', now()
               from app.employee e where e.home_branch_id = ${f.branchB} limit 1`;

    // 6A's two Form A tables. `terminal_enrolment` declares code_hash BYTEA NOT NULL UNIQUE, `name` NOT
    // NULL and `terminal_code` NOT NULL with `unique (company_id, terminal_code)` and no defaults
    // (6A's 0060) — a text literal bound to the bytea column, or either name omitted, throws in
    // `beforeAll` and takes the whole suite with it.
    await tx`insert into app.terminal_enrolment (tenant_id, company_id, branch_id, code_hash, name, terminal_code, expires_at)
             values (${s.tenant}, ${s.company}, ${f.branchB},
                     ${createHash("sha256").update("scope-b").digest()}, 'Scope Till B', 'TSB',
                     now() + interval '1 day')`;
    await tx`insert into app.needs_attention (tenant_id, company_id, branch_id, terminal_id, client_ref, payload, error_code, error_detail)
             values (${s.tenant}, ${s.company}, ${f.branchB}, ${f.terminalA.terminalId}, ${await uuidv7(db.admin)},
                     '{}'::jsonb, 'ERR_POS_LIMIT_EXCEEDED', 'seeded for the scoping test')`;

    // not `quantity_remaining` — is cost_layer's spelling, and inventory_movement carries document_id,
    // line_no, movement_no, unit_id, value, effective_date and posting_seq.
    const [lot] = await tx<{ id: string }[]>`
      select id from app.lot where item_id = ${f.itemId} and code = 'NONE' limit 1`;
    await tx`insert into app.stock_on_hand (tenant_id, company_id, item_id, warehouse_id, lot_id, quantity)
             values (${s.tenant}, ${s.company}, ${f.itemId}, ${whB}, ${lot!.id}, 3.000000)`;
    const [mv] = await tx<{ id: string }[]>`
      insert into app.inventory_movement (tenant_id, company_id, document_id, line_no, movement_no,
                                          item_id, warehouse_id, lot_id, unit_id, quantity, value,
                                          effective_date, posting_seq)
      values (${s.tenant}, ${s.company}, ${doc!.id}, 1, 1, ${f.itemId}, ${whB}, ${lot!.id}, ${f.unitId},
              3.000000, 3.0000, '2026-01-15', 1)
      returning id`;
    const [layer] = await tx<{ id: string }[]>`
      insert into app.cost_layer (tenant_id, company_id, item_id, warehouse_id, lot_id, movement_id,
                                  qty_in, unit_cost, qty_remaining, effective_at, sequence)
      values (${s.tenant}, ${s.company}, ${f.itemId}, ${whB}, ${lot!.id}, ${mv!.id},
              3.000000, 1.0000, 3.000000, '2026-01-15', 1)
      returning id`;
    await tx`insert into app.cost_consumption (tenant_id, company_id, movement_id, layer_id, quantity, unit_cost, value)
             values (${s.tenant}, ${s.company}, ${mv!.id}, ${layer!.id}, 1.000000, 1.0000, 1.0000)`;
    await tx`insert into app.stock_valuation (tenant_id, company_id, item_id, warehouse_id, as_of, quantity, value)
             values (${s.tenant}, ${s.company}, ${f.itemId}, ${whB}, '2026-01-31', 3.000000, 3.0000)`;
  });
}
```

⚠️ **`app.stock_valuation` is the one insert above whose column list no frozen document fixes.** §4.7.3 writes out `inventory_movement`, `stock_on_hand`, `cost_layer` and `cost_consumption` in full and those four are transcribed from it; `stock_valuation` appears in §4.5.6's Form C list and §4.6.1's register but has no DDL there, so **3B's merged migration is the authority and this one insert is the one to check first if `beforeAll` throws**. In every case the rule is the same: **correct the column list against the merged migration rather than dropping the table from `TABLES`** — a table quietly removed from that array is exactly the branch leak test 36 exists to find. `app.inventory_movement`, `app.stock_on_hand`, `app.cost_layer` and `app.cost_consumption` are on the `posting-only-tables` list, and this write is legal **only** because `tests/` is outside the lint runner's `ROOTS` (§3.7.5) — the same allowance `tests/support/ledger.ts` relies on. It is a scoping fixture and never a posting path. `app.inventory_movement` also carries 3A's `assert_stock_available` trigger: the quantity above is **positive** (a receipt), which passes it; a negative quantity here would need layers to consume and would be a posting path in disguise.

```ts

describe("Test 36 — branch scoping (spec:2454; LEGACY-042, 045)", () => {
  it.each(TABLES)("a branch_manager at A reads zero rows of B's $table ($via)", async ({ table }) => {
    const [{ n }] = await withTenant(db.rw, as(managerA), (tx) =>
      tx.unsafe<{ n: number }[]>(`select count(*)::int as n from app.${table}`));
    const [{ n: fromB }] = await withTenant(db.rw, as(managerB), (tx) =>
      tx.unsafe<{ n: number }[]>(`select count(*)::int as n from app.${table}`));
    expect(fromB).toBeGreaterThan(0);          // the row exists — otherwise the assertion above is vacuous
    expect(n).toBe(0);
  });

  it("the same fourteen tables are exactly the seventeen carrying branch_isolation, minus Form B's three", async () => {
    const rows = await db.admin<{ tablename: string }[]>`
      select tablename from pg_policies where schemaname = 'app' and policyname = 'branch_isolation' order by 1`;
    const covered = new Set(rows.map((r) => r.tablename));
    for (const t of TABLES) expect(covered.has(t.table)).toBe(true);
    // Form B's three are policy-carrying but deliberately NOT in TABLES: a NULL branch is visible to all.
    expect([...covered].filter((t) => !TABLES.some((x) => x.table === t)).sort())
      .toEqual(["catalogue_tombstone", "price_list", "promotion"]);
  });

  it("a branch_manager at A cannot WRITE into B, through the policy's WITH CHECK", async () => {
    await expect(withTenant(db.rw, as(managerA), (tx) => tx`
      insert into app.attendance_event (tenant_id, company_id, employee_id, branch_id, kind, occurred_at)
      select ${f.seed.tenant}, ${f.seed.company}, e.id, ${f.branchB}, 'clock_in', now()
        from app.employee e where e.home_branch_id = ${f.branchA} limit 1`)).rejects.toThrow(/row-level security|42501/);
  });

  it("a filter cannot widen it either — asking explicitly for B's branch returns nothing", async () => {
    const rows = await withTenant(db.rw, as(managerA), (tx) =>
      tx`select id from app.employee where home_branch_id = ${f.branchB}`);
    expect(rows).toHaveLength(0);
  });

  it("a request may NARROW scope below the assignment and never widen it (LEGACY-042, 045)", async () => {
    // manager A asking for A's own branch is fine; a company-wide principal asking for one branch gets one.
    const wide = await branchActor(db, f.seed, null, ["hr.employee.read"], "wide");    // null = no branch rows
    const all = await withTenant(db.rw, as(wide), (tx) => tx`select id from app.employee`);
    const narrowed = await withTenant(db.rw, as(wide), (tx) =>
      tx`select id from app.employee where home_branch_id = ${f.branchA}`);
    expect(all.length).toBeGreaterThan(narrowed.length);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the ENDPOINT half — and note where it has to live**

*"through any endpoint, filter or export"* (spec:2454) is not a claim a row-level test makes, so the API half is a real test — but it **cannot live in `tests/`**. `startApi()` is `apps/api/test/support/app.ts`, and §3.1's tier table lets the `tests` workspace import `@dulja/api` only through its two entry points (`.` and `./routes`), never a test-support file; the allowance §4.17.1 records runs the other way (`apps/api/test/**` may import `tests/invariants/support/**`). **So the endpoint half is `apps/api/test/routes/branch-scoping.test.ts`**, which imports `staffFixture` and `branchActor` through that existing allowance and needs no new one.

```ts
// apps/api/test/routes/branch-scoping.test.ts
import { afterAll, beforeAll, describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { startApi, type StartedApi } from "../support/app";
import { staffFixture, branchActor, type StaffFixture } from "../../../../tests/invariants/support/staff";
// `staffFixture` takes the StartedApi itself (it calls 6A's `posFixture`, which enrols a terminal through
// the real routes), so this file passes `api` and not `api.db`.

let api: StartedApi;
let f: StaffFixture;
let managerA: string;

const PATHS = ["employees", "cashiers", "attendance-events", "branch-pos-policies"] as const;

beforeAll(async () => {
  api = await startApi();
  f = await staffFixture(api, "scope-api");
  managerA = await branchActor(api.db, f.seed, f.branchA,
    ["hr.employee.read", "hr.attendance.record", "pos.cashier.manage_pin", "admin.company.manage"], "api-mgr-a");
  await api.loadPrincipals();
}, 300_000);
afterAll(async () => { await api.stop(); });

describe("Test 36 — no endpoint widens what branch_isolation allows (spec:2454)", () => {
  it.each(PATHS)("GET …/%s as a branch-A manager returns no branch-B row", async (path) => {
    const res = await api.request({ method: "GET", path: `/v1/companies/${f.seed.company}/${path}`, as: managerA });
    expect(res.status).toBe(200);
    const body = await res.json() as { items: { branchId?: string; homeBranchId?: string }[] };
    expect(body.items.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);                    // otherwise the assertion is vacuous
    for (const row of body.items) expect(row.branchId ?? row.homeBranchId).toBe(f.branchA);
  });

  it("an explicit filter for branch B returns an empty page rather than an error", async () => {
    const res = await api.request({
      method: "GET", as: managerA,
      path: `/v1/companies/${f.seed.company}/branch-pos-policies?filter[branchId][eq]=${f.branchB}`,
    });
    expect(res.status).toBe(200);
    expect((await res.json() as { items: unknown[] }).items).toEqual([]);
  });

  it("a by-id read of a branch-B cashier is 404 — never a 403 that confirms it exists", async () => {
    const res = await api.request({
      method: "GET", as: managerA, path: `/v1/companies/${f.seed.company}/cashiers/${f.cashierB}`,
    });
    expect(res.status).toBe(404);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the one-query clause**

Back in `tests/invariants/branch-scoping.test.ts`, and written out because *"is one SQL query"* is the assertion:

```ts
describe("Test 36 — 'who can reset a PIN at branch B' is ONE query (spec:2134, 2454)", () => {
  it("answers with branch B's manager and not branch A's", async () => {
    const rows = await withTenant(db.rw, as(f.seed.system), (tx) => tx<{ principal_id: string }[]>`
      select principal_id from app.who_can_reset_a_pin
       where company_id = ${f.seed.company} and (branch_id is null or branch_id = ${f.branchB})
       order by principal_id`);
    const ids = rows.map((r) => r.principal_id);
    expect(ids).toContain(managerB);
    expect(ids).not.toContain(managerA);
  });

  it("an expired assignment answers nothing — authority is not a row's existence (LEGACY-047)", async () => {
    await withTenant(db.rw, as(f.seed.system), (tx) =>
      tx`update app.role_assignment set expires_at = now() - interval '1 day' where principal_id = ${managerB}`);
    const rows = await withTenant(db.rw, as(f.seed.system), (tx) => tx<{ principal_id: string }[]>`
      select principal_id from app.who_can_reset_a_pin where company_id = ${f.seed.company} and branch_id = ${f.branchB}`);
    expect(rows.map((r) => r.principal_id)).not.toContain(managerB);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Append the six regression blocks**

Each is a `describe("LEGACY-NNN <Part I heading> — 6C", …)` appended to **0B's** file (contracts §2.1 lists all six under 0B's "Regression files created"), and **no earlier block is edited** (§3.7.3). If a file is missing, that is a reconciliation with 0B and a stop-and-raise — this slice creates none.

| File | The 6C block asserts |
|---|---|
| `legacy-025` — *`procQuickLogin` grants admin with no credentials* | There is **no** credential path that grants a session: the PIN endpoints accept a PIN only to **store** it, and a source scan of `apps/api/**` finds **zero** references to `verifyPin`. A cashier's principal is created only from an `AuthAdmin`-returned `sub`, never from a client-supplied subject. |
| `legacy-026` — *session identity is client-writable context* | `attendance_event.recorded_by` and `document_event.actor_id` are stamped: a synced ticket carrying an `actor_id` in its payload cannot move either. The block posts a ticket whose JSON carries a forged actor field and asserts the stored rows name the **terminal**. |
| `legacy-028` — *passwords stored and compared in cleartext* | The PIN is the only hashed credential and it is Argon2id: `select pin_hash from app.pos_cashier` matches `/^\$argon2id\$/` for every row; a source scan finds no `password` column anywhere in `packages/db/migrations/**`; and the API response of the create route does not contain the PIN. |
| `legacy-033` — *confidentiality is a display filter with a caller-supplied off switch* | Every `branch_isolation` policy is `RESTRICTIVE` (`pg_policies.permissive`), `ALLOWED_PERMISSIVE` does not contain `branch_isolation`, and no query parameter, header or body field in this slice's four route modules can disable one — asserted by scanning the four contracts modules for a name matching `/all_?branch|include_?all|no_?scope|unscoped/i` and finding none. |
| `legacy-044` — *session records are caller-controlled and attributable to the wrong user* | On an applied POS ticket: `document.created_by` and `posted_by` are the **terminal**, `document.attributed_to` is the **cashier**, `document_event.actor_id` on the override is the **terminal** and `attributed_to` is the **supervisor** — four assertions, one ticket, and the same shape hazard 19 states. |
| `legacy-045` — *department scoping is a report parameter* | Branch scope **narrows, never widens**: a branch-restricted principal asking for another branch's rows gets zero (not an error, not a widened set), and a company-wide principal narrowing to one branch gets fewer rows. The block re-runs Step 1's last two cases through the *production* `withTenant` so the claim holds on the path the application actually uses. |

```ts
// tests/regression/legacy-028.test.ts — appended
describe("LEGACY-028 Passwords are stored and compared in cleartext — 6C", () => {
  it("the PIN is the only hashed credential, and it is Argon2id (spec:2132)", async () => {
    const { db, alpha } = sharedDb();
    const rows = await withTenant(db.rw, alpha.tenant, alpha.system, (tx) =>
      tx<{ pin_hash: string }[]>`select pin_hash from app.pos_cashier`);
    expect(rows.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
    for (const r of rows) expect(r.pin_hash).toMatch(/^\$argon2id\$/);
  });

  it("no migration in the repository ever creates a password column", () => {
    const offenders = sourceFiles(["packages/db/migrations"])
      .filter((f) => /^\s*password\b|\bpassword\s+text/im.test(f.text))
      .map((f) => f.path);
    expect(offenders).toEqual([]);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Write `tests/invariants/phase-6c-pinned.test.ts` — the equalities**

```ts
describe("Phase 6C pinned lists", () => {
  it("app_rw's DELETE grants are unchanged — this slice extends nothing (§3.5.4)", async () => {
    const rows = await db.admin<{ table_name: string }[]>`
      select distinct table_name from information_schema.role_table_grants
       where grantee = 'app_rw' and table_schema = 'app' and privilege_type = 'DELETE' order by 1`;
    expect(rows.map((r) => r.table_name)).toEqual(
      ["document", "document_line", "idempotency_key", "role_assignment_branch", "role_permission"]);
  });

  it("the four new tables hold no DELETE grant and are never deleted from", () => {
    for (const t of ["employee", "pos_cashier", "branch_pos_policy", "attendance_event"]) {
      expect(sourceFiles(["packages/db/src", "apps/api/src"]).some((f) => new RegExp(`delete\\s+from\\s+app\\.${t}\\b`, "i").test(f.text))).toBe(false);
    }
  });

  it("this slice adds exactly one ERRORS row and four permission codes", () => {
    expect(Object.keys(ERRORS)).toContain("ERR_POS_LIMIT_EXCEEDED");
    expect(PERMISSIONS.filter((p) => p.phase === "6C").map((p) => p.code).sort()).toEqual(
      ["hr.attendance.record", "hr.employee.manage", "hr.employee.read", "pos.cashier.manage_pin"]);
  });

  it("four route modules, four PROBES keys, and a bijection (hazard 31)", () => {
    const mine = ROUTES.filter((r) => r.path.match(/(employees|cashiers|branch-pos-policies|attendance-events)$/));
    expect(mine).toHaveLength(4);
    for (const r of mine) expect(PROBES[r.path]).toBeDefined();
  });

  it("nothing outside posting/ and costing/ writes a protected table — this slice adds no allow-list entry", () => {
    const rule = readFileSync(join(REPO_ROOT, "scripts/lint/rules/posting-only-tables.ts"), "utf8");
    expect(rule).not.toContain("packages/db/src/sync");
    expect(rule).not.toContain("packages/db/src/staff");
  });

  it("no server code reads pos_cashier.snapshot to make a decision (ADR 0061)", () => {
    // The claim is about a READ OF THE COLUMN, not about the word. Three files this slice ships carry the
    // word legitimately and a bare /\bsnapshot\b/ would make this case red on the commit that adds it:
    //   - packages/db/src/sync/limits.ts — its own doc comment says "It never reads pos_cashier.snapshot";
    //   - apps/api/src/routes/pos/cashiers.ts — `snapshot: row.snapshot` in `toCashierDto`, because the
    //     projection TRAVELS to a `pos.cashier.manage_pin` holder who is entitled to see what the till
    //     will evaluate. Returning a row to its reader is not making a decision with it;
    //   - packages/db/src/staff/cashier.ts — the projection's own module, excluded by name below.
    // So the pattern matches a SELECT whose FROM is app.pos_cashier and which names the column, in either
    // clause order, which is the only shape that could make a server-side authority decision from it.
    const READS_THE_COLUMN =
      /from\s+app\.pos_cashier[\s\S]{0,300}?\bsnapshot\b|\bsnapshot\b[\s\S]{0,300}?from\s+app\.pos_cashier/i;
    const offenders = sourceFiles(["apps/api/src", "apps/worker/src", "packages/db/src/sync"])
      .filter((f) => READS_THE_COLUMN.test(f.text) && !/cashier-compare|staff\/cashier\.ts/.test(f.path))
      .map((f) => f.path);
    expect(offenders).toEqual([]);
  });

  it("limits.ts and the sync tier read branch_pos_policy and never the projection", () => {
    // The positive half of the same claim, so the narrowing above cannot hide a regression: the one file
    // that decides a limit must select from `app.branch_pos_policy`, and must not select the column.
    // `sourceFiles` takes ROOTS (directories) and returns every file under them, so the one file is
    // selected by path here rather than passed as a root.
    const limits = sourceFiles(["packages/db/src/sync"]).find((f) => f.path.endsWith("limits.ts"));
    expect(limits).toBeDefined();
    expect(limits!.text).toContain("from app.branch_pos_policy");
    expect(/select[^;]*\bsnapshot\b/i.test(limits!.text)).toBe(false);
  });

  it("`verifyPin` is referenced nowhere in apps/api — the server never accepts a PIN as a login (spec:1324)", () => {
    expect(sourceFiles(["apps/api/src"]).some((f) => f.text.includes("verifyPin"))).toBe(false);
  });

  it("the JOBS registry is unchanged and pos.cashier.compare has a registered handler", () => {
    expect(Object.keys(JOBS)).toHaveLength(16);
    expect(registeredJobNames()).toContain("pos.cashier.compare");
  });

  it("`sql.unsafe` appears in none of this slice's SOURCE tiers (§3.3, hazard 21)", () => {
    // Scoped to the source files this slice writes. The one `tx.unsafe` in the slice is in
    // `tests/invariants/branch-scoping.test.ts` — outside every lint root, over fourteen hard-coded
    // table literals — and Global Constraint 5 states it rather than pretending it is not there.
    const mine = sourceFiles(["packages/db/src/staff", "packages/db/src/sync/limits.ts", "apps/api/src/routes/hr", "apps/api/src/routes/pos"]);
    expect(mine.filter((f) => f.text.includes(".unsafe(")).map((f) => f.path)).toEqual([]);
  });
});
```

**Note on the branch-scoping test's own `tx.unsafe`.** Step 1's table-driven case interpolates a table name into a `select count(*)`, which is `tx.unsafe` in a **test** file. `tests/` is outside the lint runner's `ROOTS` (§3.7.5), so `no-sql-concat` does not see it, and the values interpolated are the fourteen literals of `TABLES` and nothing from any input. The alternative — fourteen hand-written cases — is fourteen chances to leave one out, which is the failure this test exists to prevent.

### Task 11: ⚠️ OD-11 — the alias e-mail domain for cashiers without a mailbox

**Isolated, and nothing else in this plan is gated on it** (hazard 6). §2.12: *"Alias e-mail domain for cashiers without a mailbox (changelog #21 — Confirm)"*, spec:39, 1324, 2406. §2.12's default is `cashier-<principal_id>@<placeholder>` **in tests only**, and that is exactly what Tasks 8 and 9 use.

**Files:**
- Create: `docs/decisions/od-11-6c-alias-domain.md`
- Modify (only when the answer lands): `apps/api/src/config.ts`, `packages/contracts/src/pos/cashier.ts`, `docs/runbooks/cashier-onboarding.md`

**Interfaces**

- Consumes: nothing at run time while the answer is outstanding — this task writes a record. **When the answer lands** it consumes `apps/api/src/config.ts`'s environment reader (0B) and `CreateCashierBody` (T8), and nothing else.
- Produces: `docs/decisions/od-11-6c-alias-domain.md`. **No exported symbol and no environment variable until the answer arrives** — `CASHIER_ALIAS_DOMAIN` is named in the record and added to §3.11 only then. Dropping this task leaves T8's route requiring a full `email` in the body, which is what every test in this slice already sends.

- [ ] **Step 1: The owner-account pattern (hazard 5)**

> **Owner provides:** the alias e-mail domain cashiers without a mailbox receive their Supabase address on — a domain the owner controls and can add an MX or catch-all record to, e.g. `staff.<the company's domain>`. **Verify:**
> ```bash
> dig +short MX staff.example.com
> ```
> prints at least one record, **and** the owner confirms in writing that addresses of the form `cashier-<uuid>@staff.example.com` are acceptable to their mail administrator. **Until provided:** the API requires the caller to supply a full `email`, tests use `cashier-<principal_id>@example.invalid` (RFC 6761's reserved TLD — it can never resolve and can never be mistaken for a real address), and **no domain literal appears anywhere in the repository**.

- [ ] **Step 2: Record the decision, answered or not**

`docs/decisions/od-11-6c-alias-domain.md` with the five headings every OD record in this project uses — **What was asked**, **What was assumed instead, and where the assumption lives**, **What changes when the answer arrives**, **What does NOT change, ever**, **Verify**.

- **What was assumed instead:** the create route takes `email` as a **required body field**; nothing derives an address. `apps/api` holds no domain, `packages/db` holds none, and the only address literal in the repository is `example.invalid` inside tests.
- **What changes when the answer arrives:** one environment variable, `CASHIER_ALIAS_DOMAIN`, joins §3.11 and `ApiConfig` (and `containerSecrets`, in the same PR — §3.11's closing sentence); `CreateCashierBody.email` becomes **optional**; when it is absent the handler composes `cashier-<principalId>@${config.cashierAliasDomain}` **after** the principal id is minted and **before** `authAdmin.createUser`; and the runbook's step 2 gains the sentence "leave the e-mail blank for staff without a mailbox".
- **What does NOT change, ever:** the address is never a login the cashier uses — it exists so `auth_subject` is a Supabase `sub` (spec:1324); the PIN stays the till credential; and a cashier *with* a mailbox always supplies their real address, so the alias path is the exception and not the rule.
- **Verify:** the `dig` command above, plus `bun run --cwd apps/api test -- test/routes/pos/cashiers.test.ts` passing a case with **no** `email` in the body.

**If the owner answers "no alias domain — every cashier has a mailbox"**, this task is deleted, `email` stays required, and nothing else in the slice moves.

### Task 12: ⚠️ OD-12 — the list of cashiers per branch

**Isolated, and nothing else in this plan is gated on it** (hazard 6). §2.12: *"List of cashiers per branch"* (spec:2406); the gate is *"6C → cashier seed task"*; the default is **none**.

**Files:**
- Create: `docs/decisions/od-12-6c-cashier-list.md`
- Modify (only when the answer lands): nothing under `packages/db/seed/` — see Step 2.

**Interfaces**

- Consumes: nothing at run time — this task writes a record. Even when the answer lands it consumes only T8's two routes (`POST …/employees`, `POST …/cashiers`) and the runbook, because Step 2's decision is that cashiers are created **through the API and never as pack data**.
- Produces: `docs/decisions/od-12-6c-cashier-list.md`. **No seed pack, no `packages/db/seed/` file and no fixture row** — a version-controlled PIN would be LEGACY-028 by another door. Dropping this task changes no code path.

- [ ] **Step 1: The owner-account pattern (hazard 5)**

> **Owner provides:** for each branch, the staff who operate a till — full name, employee number if one exists today, home branch, hire date, and whether the person has a work mailbox. **Verify:**
> ```bash
> bun run seed --tenant "$TENANT_ID" --only roles
> bun run --cwd apps/api dev &                # then, per person, the runbook's six steps
> curl -s "$BASE/v1/companies/$COMPANY/cashiers" -H "authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" | jq '.items | length'
> ```
> prints the number of people the owner listed for that branch. **Until provided:** no cashier is created anywhere outside a test, and `packages/db/seed/my/` gains **no** directory.

- [ ] **Step 2: Why this is a runbook and not a seed sub-pack**

**A cashier is not seed-pack data and cannot become one** — and this is the part a later reader is most likely to get wrong. §3.4.8 makes per-tenant *configuration* seed-pack data; a cashier is **a person with a Supabase auth user**, and creating one calls the Supabase admin API. A `PackLoader` runs inside `bun run seed`, whose only auth seam is 0C's `SeedAuthAdmin` port, and giving it a cashier loader would (a) put a second onboarding implementation beside Task 8's route (hazard 9), (b) mean a PIN in a version-controlled file, which is the exact defect LEGACY-028 names, and (c) widen `SubPack` for data that is never the same in two tenants.

**So OD-12's answer is executed through `docs/runbooks/cashier-onboarding.md`**, once per person, by an operator holding `admin.user.manage` and `admin.role.assign` — and the "seed task" the ledger names is this task: the record, the verification command and the runbook, not a loader. **This slice therefore adds no `SubPack` member, no seed directory and no loader**, and the §4.2.13 registry is untouched.

- [ ] **Step 3: Record the decision and commit**

`docs/decisions/od-12-6c-cashier-list.md`, same five headings, plus the paragraph above under **What does NOT change, ever** — a cashier never becomes seed-pack data, whatever the owner's list turns out to contain.

```bash
git add docs/decisions/od-12-6c-cashier-list.md
git commit -m "$(cat <<'EOF'
docs(decisions): record OD-12, the per-branch cashier list [6C T12]

Blocked on the owner. Executed through the onboarding runbook, never as seed-pack data: a cashier has
a Supabase auth user and a PIN, and a version-controlled PIN is LEGACY-028 by a different door. No SubPack
member, no seed directory, no loader.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>
EOF
)"
```

### Task 13: ⚠️ OD-24 — the staff directory, cashier and branch-limit screens

**Isolated, blocked, and it blocks nothing** (hazard 6, hazard 29). §2.12: *"Screenshots + operator description before any screen"* (spec:79, 108, 2408). **This task does not start until the owner's material is attached to it.**

**Files (all ⚠️, dropped with this task):**
- Create: `docs/owner/od-24/6c/` (the gate directory), `apps/backoffice/src/lib/staff-api.ts`, `apps/backoffice/src/routes/hr/employees.tsx`, `apps/backoffice/src/routes/pos/cashiers.tsx`
- Modify: `packages/i18n`'s `en` and `ar` catalogues

**Interfaces**

- Consumes: `packages/ui` and `packages/i18n` (0E), `bootLocalStack` and `grantAdminRole` (0E, §4.17.1 — the Playwright fixture test 23 re-runs through), and the four route modules this slice ships, by their wire contracts only: `EmployeeSchema` / `CreateEmployeeBody` (T8), `CashierSchema` / `CreateCashierBody` / `SetPinBody` (T8), `BranchPosPolicySchema` / `UpsertBranchPosPolicyBody` (T3) and `AttendanceEventSchema` (T4), all from `@dulja/contracts`. It calls no repository function and imports nothing from `@dulja/db`.
- Produces:
  ```ts
  // apps/backoffice/src/lib/staff-api.ts — ⚠️ dropped with this task
  export const staffApi;   // the typed fetch wrapper the three route components share; every response is
                           // parsed through the contracts schema above, so a wire drift is a test failure
                           // and never a runtime `undefined`
  ```
  plus the two route components `apps/backoffice/src/routes/hr/employees.tsx` and `apps/backoffice/src/routes/pos/cashiers.tsx`, and the `en` / `ar` catalogue keys they use. **Nothing outside `apps/backoffice` imports any of it**, which is what makes the whole task droppable without renumbering a neighbour's interface (hazard 6).

- [ ] **Step 1: The gate (hazard 5's pattern, applied to a screen)**

> **Owner provides:** screenshots of the three legacy screens these replace — the staff list, the cashier/PIN screen and wherever discount limits are set today — and a **two-minute operator description** of what a branch manager actually does at open of business. **Verify:**
> ```bash
> ls docs/owner/od-24/6c/
> ```
> lists at least one image and one `description.md`, and the description is linked from this task in the tracker. **Until provided:** this task does not start. **The other twelve tasks are complete without it** — the API, the policies, the job and both acceptance tests are green, and the screens are a client of an API that already works.

- [ ] **Step 2: What the screens are, once the gate opens**

Three screens, and the plan states their **keyboard** shape rather than their visual one, because keyboard parity is the requirement spec:2254 actually makes: a **staff directory** (list, filter by branch, open one; `/` focuses search, `Enter` opens, `Esc` closes); a **cashier panel** on an employee (create the till credential, reset the PIN, revoke — every action reachable without a mouse, and the PIN field never pre-filled, never copyable and cleared on blur); and a **branch limits** form (the six numbers of `branch_pos_policy`, each labelled with its unit — a per-cent field that stores a fraction is the one place a UI can silently multiply by a hundred, so the input shows `10 %` and the client sends `"0.100000"`, with a test asserting exactly that round-trip).

**No workflow is invented.** If the owner's description shows an operator doing something none of these three screens covers, the finding goes back to the owner as a question, not into a fourth screen (hazard 29).

- [ ] **Step 3: Both text directions, and the test-23 re-run**

Every string through i18next with an ICU message; `en` and `ar` complete before merge; the Playwright case added to `tests/e2e/` re-runs test 23 over the three screens in both directions using 0E's `bootLocalStack` and `grantAdminRole`. **A screen merged in one direction only is a merge-blocking finding** (spec:106's Always list).

- [ ] **Step 4: Commit**

```bash

        apps/backoffice/src/routes/pos/cashiers.tsx packages/i18n tests/e2e/
git commit -m "$(cat <<'EOF'
feat(backoffice): staff directory, cashier panel and branch-limit screens [6C T13]

Gated on OD-24: the owner's screenshots and operator description are attached in docs/owner/od-24/6c/.
Keyboard parity per spec:2254; the PIN field is never pre-filled, never copyable and clears on blur; the
per-cent inputs display 10 % and send "0.100000", asserted as a round-trip. Both text directions (test 23).

Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>
EOF
)"
```
