## Self-Review

**Against the contracts card (§2.7's 6C row).** Every deliverable the card lists has a task. The minimal `app.employee` with §4.6.2's DDL **verbatim**, `position` and `employment_type` deliberately absent (T1); `app.pos_cashier` keyed on `principal_id`, Argon2id with a per-cashier salt, `branch_id`, the `snapshot` **projection** regenerated by the catalogue-revision trigger on `role_assignment`/`role_assignment_branch`/`role_permission` change and on `branch_pos_policy` change, `revoked_at`, and the nightly `pos.cashier.compare` against `app.principal_has` (T2, T7); cashier onboarding through the Supabase admin API with a random undisclosed password and an alias address (T8, T11), so `auth_subject` is always a Supabase `sub`, and `pos.cashier.manage_pin` for `branch_manager` (T6, T8); `app.branch_pos_policy` under `admin.company.manage`, down-synced only to that branch's terminals, with sync apply re-verifying against **the row** (T3, T9); `app.attendance_event` from the till clock-in and from back-office corrections under `hr.attendance.record` (T4, T9); `app.actor_in_branch`, `app.actor_in_warehouse` and the five `branch_isolation` forms **in migration 0068 in one file**, with the `branch_isolation_policy` linter rule (T5); the four codes (T6). Migration range **0064–0069, all six used, six PRs**; ADR range **0060–0062**, all three written as proposals per spec:95. **Target size 12–14 tasks: 14** (T0 carries no application code — the shape 2C, 3C, 3D, 4B and 5A all used).

**One deviation from the card, deliberate, and stated where it bites.** The card gives 6C's migrations as six files including `0067_create_attendance_event.sql`. **The file ships as `0067_create_attendance_event_and_cashier_snapshot.sql`, under that name from its first commit**, because `app.refresh_pos_cashier`, its five triggers and `app.who_can_reset_a_pin` are DDL, 0064–0069 are all spoken for, and §3.4.2's round-2 rule — *a file whose name understates what it does is renamed, not left* — makes the longer name the correct one. **No rename ever happens**: Task 4 creates the file already so named and Task 7 appends to it; both Files blocks say so. 2A's `0018_…` and 2B's `0023_…` are the two recorded precedents; this is the third. Tasks 4 and 7 are two commits in one PR. **§2.7's card and §3.4.1's parenthetical are updated to the long name by Task 0 amendment A16**, because §3.4.2's round-2 rule says in its own precedent text that the card moves with the file (*"Both cards in §2.3 now state the long name"*) — a deviation the register does not carry is not a deviation, it is a divergence.

**One further deviation, forced by 6A.** The card's delivery bullet says `app.branch_pos_policy` is *"down-synced only to that branch's terminals"*, and 6A's merged `readCatalogue` implements that by calling **`app.pos_cashier_sync` and `app.branch_pos_policy_sync`**, two functions it names and does not ship. Those functions are therefore 6C's, and they land in **migrations 0065 and 0066 beside their own tables** rather than in a seventh file — the range is full, and a function that the moment its table exists is already being called cannot ship a migration later than the table.

**This plan is longer than the 2500–4000-line guidance — about 5,000 lines — and the reason is recorded rather than hidden.** Six of the fourteen tasks carry a complete migration or module in full (0064's frozen DDL, 0065 plus `pin.ts`, 0066 plus its repository and route, 0067's two halves, **0068's five policy forms and the linter rule**, and `limits.ts` plus the `applyBatch` seam), and hazard 35 requires the plan to repeat the code rather than gesture at it. The written peer plans in this folder run 5,400–8,400 lines for the same reason. **Nothing was padded, and the file contains no elision standing in for content**: every fixture body, every route handler, every Zod module and every test is written out. Where a `…` does appear it abbreviates a URL prefix inside a prose table (`GET …/employees`), quotes a sentence from the spec or the contracts document, stands for the tail of a SQL predicate quoted verbatim from §4.5.6, or marks the unchanged arguments of a `describe(…)` call whose real body is written out in the same task — never a body a reader has to invent.

**Nine things this review pass changed, every one of which would have failed on first run or first `typecheck`.** Five were blockers.

1. **The `branch_isolation_policy` rule would have failed the build the day it shipped, on a table nobody had thought about.** `app.role_assignment_branch` (plan0A:1348–1357) carries `branch_id uuid not null`, so §3.5.2's rule as written fires on it — and attaching the policy is far worse than the violation: `app.actor_in_branch` **selects from that table**, so a restrictive policy there calling it is self-referential and PostgreSQL aborts *every* read of it with "infinite recursion detected in policy", taking `app.actor_has` and therefore every authorisation decision in the system with it. Amendment **A2** declares `BRANCH_ISOLATION_EXEMPT` at exactly that one name, ADR 0062 records why, and T5's second test asserts both halves — no policy, **and** the predicate still answering for a branch-restricted principal.
2. **Three of 6A's tables carry a branch key and would have been left uncovered.** `terminal_enrolment` and `needs_attention` (`branch_id`, Form A) and `catalogue_tombstone` (nullable `branch_id`, **Form B**) are created by migrations 0059–0060, *before* 0068 — and §3.5.1 step 7 attaches a form only "if 6C's migration 0068 has landed", so 6A **cannot** attach one and 0068 must. Amendment **A1** adds them to the two existing lists (no sixth *form* is created). Getting `catalogue_tombstone` wrong in the other direction is not silent either: Form A on a nullable column hides every **company-wide** tombstone from every terminal, and a till stops learning that an item was deleted. T5 Step 1 makes the executor read the three declarations before writing the file.
3. **`AuthAdmin` had no method that could create a cashier.** §4.2.3's port is `inviteUser` only, and spec:1324 requires a user *created* at hire with a random undisclosed password and **no invitation** — a mailbox-less alias address cannot receive one. Amendment **A3** adds `createUser`, returning the existing `InvitedUser` shape rather than a second one. Without it Task 8 does not compile.
4. **`pos.attendance.clock` cannot be referenced by this slice, and the card's sentence implies it can.** The code is **6B's** (§3.6.2) and 6B executes *after* 6C: `PERMISSION["pos.attendance.clock"]` is a type error, and a `principal_has` check against a code no role holds refuses **every** clock event. Amendment **A10** records the resolution — the till evaluates it from the snapshot (where A5's derivation puts it the moment 6B seeds it), the server's gate at 6C is the cashier's live `pos_cashier` row at the terminal's branch, and **6B adds the one-line server check in the PR that seeds the code**. Naming the owner is what stops this becoming a gap nobody holds.
5. **A literal snapshot code list could not be written.** It would have to name `pos.shift.close_any` and `pos.report.read` — 6D's codes — which `PERMISSION[…]` cannot reference and which `permission-catalogue` would reject. Amendment **A5** makes the set a **derivation** in both spellings (`module = 'pos' and code not like 'pos.sync.%'`), and T7's first test asserts the SQL and the TypeScript agree. 6D's codes then join with no edit to this slice.
6. **`seedCashier` would have produced an empty snapshot that nothing noticed.** The helper is written in T2 and the function it calls is created in T7, so T2 Step 8 ships the line commented with an explicit `// TASK 7:` marker and **T7 Step 6 deletes the marker and re-runs the two earlier suites** — a fixture that silently changed shape under an already-green test is exactly what that step exists to catch.
7. **`revokeCashier` had to be idempotent or the tombstone trigger would fire twice.** `pos_cashier_tombstone`'s `when (old.revoked_at is null and new.revoked_at is not null)` is what makes one revoke one tombstone; a handler writing `revoked_at = now()` unconditionally would satisfy the `when` clause on every call. The repository writes `coalesce(revoked_at, now())`, T2 and T8 each assert the count is 1 after two calls, and the reason is on the line.
8. **The supervisor check was in two places and is now in one.** An early draft verified `app.principal_has(supervisor, 'pos.ticket.override', …)` inside `limits.ts` *and* let 2B's `assert_override_authorised` verify it again. Two answers to one question drift; the trigger's is the one that binds (spec:2173), so `verifyTicket` only *lifts* refusals by subject and records nothing at all. The two tests asserting `ERR_OVERRIDE_NOT_AUTHORISED` are therefore testing 2B's trigger **through 6A's own `recordOverride` call on this slice's path**, which is precisely test 37's second-principal clause.
9. **Percentage limits had no scale discipline and no CHECK.** A limit stored as `15` rather than `0.150000` authorises a fifteen-hundred-per-cent discount the first time somebody divides instead of multiplying — the arithmetic half of LEGACY-020 in a different module. 0066 now carries `branch_pos_policy_pct_range` (`between 0 and 1`), the contracts module's `Pct` regex refuses anything else at the wire, `limits.ts` **multiplies** an allowance instead of dividing to a ratio (no division, no float, one rounding mode), and T13's screen test asserts the `10 %` ↔ `"0.100000"` round-trip.

**Ten further things the 6A-reconciliation pass changed, once 6A's merged plan file existed.** Five of these were blockers too; all ten are named here because each replaces a guess this plan had recorded as an open question.

10. **`applyBatch` is four parameters, not three, and its terminal context carries the branch.** 6A freezes `applyBatch(tx, ctx: TenantContext, terminal: TerminalApplyContext, batch)` with `TerminalApplyContext { terminalId; companyId; branchId; principalId }`. `applyTicket` and the Task 9 seam both called it with three arguments and both read `ctx.companyId` / `ctx.branchId`, which `TenantContext` does not have. Every call site now passes both objects and the seam reads `terminal.*`. The returned per-ticket shape is 6A's db-local **`TicketOutcome`**, not `@dulja/sync`'s `TicketResult`.
11. **The seam recorded overrides 6A's step (h) already records.** 6A's algorithm runs `setEventContext` + `recordOverride(sp, ctx, …)` for every `TicketEvent` before `postDocument` (6A:3674), and contracts §4.3.1 fixes `recordOverride` at three parameters. This slice's loop was both a compile error (two arguments) and a duplicate write; it is deleted, `TicketVerification.overrides` with it, and Step 3's fourth case asserts `toHaveLength(1)` so the duplicate cannot come back.
12. **6A's catalogue down-sync calls two functions this slice never shipped.** `readCatalogue` probes `to_regclass('app.pos_cashier')` and then executes `app.pos_cashier_sync(...)`; 6A states the obligation twice and hands 6C the exact signature. Migrations 0065 and 0066 now ship `app.pos_cashier_sync` and `app.branch_pos_policy_sync` **in the same file as their tables** — without which every `GET /v1/sync/catalogue` fails the moment 0065 applies — and Task 3 Step 3 deletes 6A's `extra` block, folds both streams into the `union all` and extends `currentCatalogueRevision`.
13. **`staffFixture` was a second POS corpus and could not post a ticket.** §4.17.1 gives 6A's `posFixture` the company, items, **warehouse**, **stock**, tax pack, **`POS_TICKET` type** and terminal, and names 6C a consumer. The old fixture seeded none of those and every `accepted` assertion would have failed at the header insert. It now wraps `posFixture` and adds only the second branch, three cashiers and two policy rows; tickets are composed by 6A's `buildTicket`, which is also what negates a return's four fields — without which both blind-return cases died at 6A's shape validation with `ERR_VALIDATION_FAILED`.
14. **`refresh_pos_cashier` stored a revision one behind its own row.** Its UPDATE fires 0065's `catalogue_revision` BEFORE trigger, so the value it computed was always stale by one and `findSnapshotDrift` would have reported every cashier as drifted, permanently. The function no longer writes `revision` at all; a second BEFORE trigger, `snapshot_revision_stamp` — ordered after `catalogue_revision` by name — stamps the value actually stored, as **text**, which is also the one spelling `computeSnapshot` and the corrupted-fixture test now use.
15. **Untyped `coalesce` fallbacks returned `"0"` where the tests asserted `"0.0000"`.** PostgreSQL's numeric-to-text cast keeps the value's own scale and an untyped `0` has scale 0, so a branch with no policy row produced a limit set that matched neither the migration's defaults nor `limits.ts`'s TypeScript fallback. Every fallback in both spellings now carries its column's type: `0::numeric(9,6)`, `0::numeric(19,4)`, `0.05::numeric(19,4)`.
16. **The attendance seam was five tasks too late.** 6A refuses a clock-in behind `to_regclass('app.attendance_event') is null`, which goes false the instant migration 0067 applies — so between Task 4 and Task 9 a ticket carrying a clock-in would have been accepted with the HR event dropped, and 6A's own test would have been red the whole way. The insert and 6A's test rewrite moved into **Task 4**, beside the migration that causes them.
17. **`limits.ts` imported `@dulja/sync`.** 0B's `dependency-rule` pins `packages/db` to `['@dulja/domain']` — the same rule that made 6A ship `SyncError` instead of using `AppError`. The file now types against 6A's db-local `TicketInput` / `TicketLineInput`, and the two `as never` casts are gone: `SyncErrorCode` gains `ERR_RBAC_FORBIDDEN` and `ERR_POS_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` (amendment A14), both already `ErrCode` rows, so its "literal subset of `ErrCode`" property is preserved.
18. **Three test cases proved the wrong thing.** The blind-return case gave its cashier a role assignment and no `pos_cashier` row, so `verifyTicket`'s first check refused it as `ERR_RBAC_FORBIDDEN` before any limit was read; the revocation case revoked a **branch-B** cashier and then applied at branch A, where no row matched and the detail said "no pos_cashier row" rather than "revoked"; and `expect(countLedger()).toBe(ledgerBefore + 1)` assumed one `ledger_entry` row per ticket, when `app.ledger_entry` is one row per posting **line** with a single-sided CHECK (plan0A:1917–1921) and a balanced ticket writes at least two. All three now seed a real cashier at the branch they test, and every ledger assertion is an equality against a captured value or a per-document count.
19. **The `catalogue_tombstone` insert had no `on conflict`, the `terminal_enrolment` seed omitted two NOT NULL columns, and three Form C inserts named columns that do not exist.** The tombstone upserts on `(stream, row_id)` exactly as 6A's own trigger does, so reinstating a cashier and revoking again no longer aborts; `terminal_enrolment` gets `name`, `terminal_code` and a real `sha256` **bytea**; and `inventory_movement`, `cost_layer` and `cost_consumption` are transcribed from §4.7.3 in full, `qty_remaining` spelled the way that document spells it.

**Eleven things the independent-audit pass changed, after the 6A reconciliation.** Two were blockers, and every one of them was a line that would have failed at run time rather than a wording preference.

20. **BLOCKER — every 6C fixture wrote a branch-isolated table as `s.human`, and Task 5's own migration then refused it.** `seedEmployee`, `seedCashier` (both its `pos_cashier` insert and its `refresh_pos_cashier` call) and `seedBranchPosPolicy` inserted under `withTenant(db.rw, s.tenant, s.human, …)` — §4.17's default. From migration 0068 all three tables carry a RESTRICTIVE `branch_isolation` policy declared with `using` and no `with check`, which PostgreSQL applies **as** the `with check` on INSERT; `app.actor_in_branch` is true only for a `kind = 'system'` principal or one with a live `role_assignment`, and `seedTenant` grants `s.human` nothing (§4.1, pinned). Every fixture call from Task 5 onward — Task 5's own Form A/A′ cases, Task 7's snapshot suite, `staffFixture`, Task 10 — would have died with `new row violates row-level security policy`. All three now insert as `db.migrator`/`s.system`, recorded as amendment **A15**, which is the identical rule and wording 6D's A17 already states for `seedShift` and cites 6C as the precedent for. The **reads** are the other half and are fixed the other way: five suites gain one `grantCodes(db, s)` line in `beforeAll`, because their actor must stay human for what they assert to mean anything. **Task 5 Step 7 is new** and runs the whole repository suite after 0068 with the two-remedy rule written out, because 0A's `draftDocument` and 3A's `seedWarehouse` write branch-keyed tables as `s.human` too and neither is 6C's to change.
21. **BLOCKER — `pos.cashier.compare` inserted into `app.audit_log` using two columns that do not exist.** The handler wrote `before_image` / `after_image`; plan0A:2260–2274 declares `before jsonb` / `after jsonb`, and `app.audit_chain()` hashes them by those names. The first drift row — the only row the job ever writes — would have failed with `column "before_image" of relation "audit_log" does not exist`, so the job would have looked healthy for exactly as long as nothing was wrong. Renamed in the insert and in both prose descriptions of the test case.
22. **`getBranchPosPolicy` called a postgres.js query object as a template tag.** `const SELECT = (tx) => tx\`select …\`` returns a `PendingQuery`, not a function, so `` SELECT(tx)`where …` `` is a `TypeError` at the first call — and the `as unknown as BranchPosPolicyRow[]` cast is what would have kept `typecheck` quiet about it. It now interpolates the fragment the way its own sibling `listBranchPosPolicies` already did, with no cast.
23. **The pinned "no server code reads `pos_cashier.snapshot`" test was red on the commit that introduced it.** It scanned for the *word* and excluded two paths; three files this slice ships carry the word legitimately — `limits.ts`'s doc comment saying it never reads the column, and the cashier route's `toCashierDto`, where the projection travels to a `pos.cashier.manage_pin` holder. The pattern now matches a select **of the column from `app.pos_cashier`**, and a second case asserts the positive half so the narrowing cannot hide a regression.
24. **Test 36's `pos_terminal` case asserted a branch-B row nothing seeded.** `staffFixture` enrols one terminal and it is at branch A; `seedBranchBWorld` seeded a `terminal_enrolment` at B and no `pos_terminal`, so `expect(fromB).toBeGreaterThan(0)` — the guard that keeps the zero-rows assertion non-vacuous — failed on its own. It now calls 6A's `seedTerminal` at `f.branchB`, which already inserts as `s.system`.
25. **Task 5's Form C case inserted zero rows.** It selected its item and lot with `from app.item i join app.lot l … limit 1`, and `seedTenant` creates neither — `Seed` has no item member. The insert was a no-op, `expect(asB).toHaveLength(1)` failed and `expect(asA).toHaveLength(0)` passed **vacuously**, which is the branch leak the case exists to find. `beforeAll` now seeds an item through 3A's `seedItem`, reads its sentinel `NONE` lot, and the case inserts explicit ids and asserts the row's existence **first**.
26. **Task 0's gate could not detect whether amendment A1 had landed.** Its probe was the fixed string `terminal_enrolment`, which the contracts document already contains five times, so the line printed `ok` unconditionally. It now probes the sentence 6A's own Task 0 Step 4 appends to §4.5.6 — and A1 is restated as a **verification of 6A's amendment** rather than a second edit of the same three rows, since 6A merges first. Step 3's expected counts are corrected with it.
27. **`seedEmployee`'s default hire date contradicted the amendment that records it.** A12 says `'2026-01-01'`; the body wrote `'2026-01-05'`. One literal now, in both places.
28. **Three of Task 3's Produces declarations did not describe what Task 3 ships.** `BranchPosPolicySchema` was called fifteen members and has fourteen; `listBranchPosPolicies` was declared without the `branchId` its route passes on every call; `seedBranchPosPolicy`'s `limits` was declared `Partial<BranchPosPolicyInput>` and implemented as a hand-rolled union that dropped `maxCashInDrawer` and `requireCustomerOver` — so the two nullable columns could not be seeded at all. All three now match, and the fixture's insert carries the two columns it was missing.
29. **`seedCashier`'s amended body was described and never written.** A12 and Task 4 Step 6 both say the helper gains `principalId?` and skips the mint when it is supplied, but no step showed the guarded code and Task 2's body inserts the principal unconditionally. Hazard 35 asks for the code; Task 4 Step 6 now carries it in full. Task 1's and Task 4's Consumes lists gained the names their own steps use.
30. **`recordAttendance` returned `undefined` from a non-nullable signature**, so both callers' `if (!created)` guards read as dead code. It is `Promise<AttendanceEventRow | null>` now, and the sync seam refuses the ticket on a null rather than accepting it with the clock-in dropped. The job handler's unexplained `await import("@dulja/db")` became a static import in the same pass.

**Contract additions — every name invented because the registers lacked one.** All **sixteen** register amendments land in Task 0; nothing below is a literal in a registered namespace, because **this slice writes into none of the four literal registers**: no document-type code (§4.14), no account code (§4.15), no report code or `DatasetId` member (§4.16), and exactly one allocated `ERR_*` (§4.2.4's `ERR_POS_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, already 6C's row).

- `packages/db/src/staff/pin.ts` — **`hashPin`**, **`verifyPin`**, **`PinFormatError`**, **`PIN_MIN_LENGTH`**, **`PIN_MAX_LENGTH`** (ADR 0060). The one PIN function; 6B imports it in the Electron main process.
- `packages/db/src/staff/employee.ts` — **`EmployeeRow`**, **`CreateEmployeeInput`**, **`createEmployee`**, **`getEmployee`**, **`listEmployees`**, **`linkEmployeePrincipal`**.
- `packages/db/src/staff/cashier.ts` — **`CashierLimits`**, **`CashierSnapshot`**, **`SnapshotDrift`**, **`computeSnapshot`**, **`findSnapshotDrift`**, **`readSnapshot`** (T7) and **`CashierRow`**, **`createCashier`**, **`setCashierPin`**, **`revokeCashier`**, **`getCashier`**, **`listCashiers`** (T8).
- `packages/db/src/staff/branch-pos-policy.ts` — **`BranchPosPolicyRow`**, **`BranchPosPolicyInput`**, **`upsertBranchPosPolicy`**, **`getBranchPosPolicy`**, **`listBranchPosPolicies`**. `packages/db/src/staff/attendance.ts` — **`AttendanceKind`**, **`AttendanceEventRow`**, **`RecordAttendanceInput`**, **`recordAttendance`**, **`listAttendance`**. `packages/db/src/staff/index.ts` — the barrel `packages/db/src/index.ts` re-exports.
- `packages/db/src/sync/limits.ts` — **`LimitKind`**, **`LimitRefusal`**, **`TicketVerification`** (two members: `missingCodes`, `refusals` — no `overrides`, because 6A's step (h) is the only writer), **`TicketContext`**, **`verifyTicket`**, **`requiredCodesFor`**, **`OVERRIDE_LIFTS`** (the one subject → limit-kind mapping; 6D's till events read it for the same `void` subject). `packages/db/src/sync/enrolment.ts` gains **two members on 6A's `SyncErrorCode`** and no new type.
- SQL objects: **`app.refresh_pos_cashier`**, **`app.stamp_snapshot_revision`** (and its trigger `snapshot_revision_stamp`, whose name must keep sorting after `catalogue_revision`), **`app.refresh_pos_cashier_for_assignment`**, **`app.refresh_pos_cashier_for_role`**, **`app.refresh_pos_cashier_for_branch_policy`**, **`app.pos_cashier_tombstone`**, **`app.pos_cashier_sync`** and **`app.branch_pos_policy_sync`** (the two down-sync functions 6A's `readCatalogue` names and does not ship — their signature is 6A's, not this slice's), **`app.assert_attendance_correction`**, **`app.who_can_reset_a_pin`** (a **view**, so spec:2134's "one SQL query" is a relation an auditor can filter and join), and the named constraints **`branch_pos_policy_pct_range`**, **`branch_pos_policy_amount_non_negative`**, **`attendance_event_kind_check`**, **`attendance_event_correction_kind`** plus the raised token **`attendance_event_corrects_same_employee`**. `app.actor_in_branch` and `app.actor_in_warehouse` are **§4.5.6 verbatim** and are not inventions.
- `packages/contracts/src/pos/branch-pos-policy.ts` — `BranchPosPolicySchema`, `UpsertBranchPosPolicyBody`, `BranchPosPolicyQuery`, `BranchPosPolicyList`, `BranchPosPolicyDto` and the file-local `Pct` / `Amount` (§4.2.8's registered practice, 4B's precedent). `packages/contracts/src/hr/attendance.ts` — `AttendanceKindSchema`, `AttendanceEventSchema`, `RecordAttendanceBody`, `AttendanceQuery`, `AttendanceList`, `AttendanceEventDto`. `packages/contracts/src/hr/employee.ts` — `EmployeeSchema`, `CreateEmployeeBody`, `EmployeeQuery`, `EmployeeList`, `EmployeeDto`. `packages/contracts/src/pos/cashier.ts` — `CashierSchema`, `CreateCashierBody`, `SetPinBody`, `CashierQuery`, `CashierList`, `CashierDto`, `CashierSnapshotSchema`. `packages/contracts/permissions.ts` — four `PermissionDef` rows and **`CASHIER_SNAPSHOT_CODES`**. `packages/contracts/src/errors.ts` — **one** `ERRORS` row.
- `apps/api` — **`employeeRoutes`** / **`employeeRouteModule`**, **`createCashierRoutes`** / **`cashierRouteModule`** (a factory, like 0B's users and 2C's attachments), **`branchPosPolicyRoutes`** / **`branchPosPolicyRouteModule`**, **`attendanceEventRoutes`** / **`attendanceEventRouteModule`**, the private `toCashierDto` / `toEmployeeDto`; `ports.ts` gains **`AuthAdmin.createUser`** and `auth-admin.ts` gains **`SupabaseAuthAdmin`'s `createPath` option**.
- `apps/worker/src/jobs/cashier-compare.ts` — **`cashierCompare`**; `packages/db/src/jobs/payloads.ts` gains **one key**.
- `packages/db/src/lint-schema.ts` — **`BRANCH_ISOLATION_EXEMPT`**, the module-private **`BRANCH_ISOLATED_BY_WAREHOUSE`**, the **`branch_isolation_policy`** rule, and two set memberships (`pos_cashier` into `NO_ID_TABLES` and `AUDIT_EXEMPT`) — §3.5.3's exact state and nothing more.
- `packages/db/test/fixtures.ts` — **`seedEmployee`**, **`seedCashier`**, **`seedBranchPosPolicy`**: the three §4.17 rows this slice owns, and no fourth. Test tier: **`tests/invariants/support/staff.ts`** (§4.17.1, amendment A9) with **`StaffFixture`**, **`staffFixture`**, **`TicketDraft`**, **`applyTicket`**, **`branchActor`**; the file-local `seedBranchBWorld`, `snapshotOf` and `TABLES`.
- Drizzle mirrors **`employee`**, **`posCashier`**, **`branchPosPolicy`**, **`attendanceEvent`**. Documents: ADRs `0060-argon2id-in-webassembly`, `0061-cashier-snapshot-is-a-projection`, `0062-branch-isolation-five-forms`; `docs/decisions/od-11-6c-alias-domain.md`, `od-12-6c-cashier-list.md`; `docs/runbooks/cashier-onboarding.md`; `docs/owner/od-24/6c/`; `scripts/check/contracts-6c-amendments.sh`. ⚠️ Backoffice (dropped with T13): `staff-api.ts`, two route components.

**Against the never/always lists.** Four tables are created and every one carries `tenant_id`, `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY`, a policy named exactly `tenant_isolation`, `created_at`, composite `(tenant_id, x)` foreign keys to every tenant-scoped parent, `uuidv7` ids where §3.5.3 does not exempt them, snake_case singular names, a hand-written down file and a two-tenant test **in its own task**. `attach_audit` on three of them and `AUDIT_EXEMPT` + `NO_ID_TABLES` on `pos_cashier` — §3.5.3's exact state, edited once, in T2 only. Every FK to `app.principal` is the **single-column** inline form, because `principal` is global and carries no `unique (tenant_id, id)`. Every endpoint has a Zod schema, an OpenAPI entry regenerated **in the same commit**, `IdempotencyHeader` on every unsafe method and a probe on each of the four new modules. **This slice writes no ledger, movement, layer, projection or tax row at all**, adds no `posting-only-tables` allow-list entry, and T10 asserts that. No `number` holds money or a percentage; every limit is a 4- or 6-dp string and every comparison is `Money.compare` against a `Money.multiply` allowance. No `sql.unsafe` in any of this slice's **source** tiers (T10 Step 5 asserts exactly that scope); the one `tx.unsafe` is in a `tests/` file, outside every lint root, over fourteen hard-coded table names, and Global Constraint 5 says so rather than claiming there is none. No DDL from application code, no disabled invariant, no enum label added (one new vocabulary, `attendance_event.kind`, as `text` + a named CHECK). Every commit message carries the trailer.

**Against the hazards.** **1**: no statutory rate, threshold, ceiling or formula anywhere — this slice touches HR tables and seeds no HR data; every `branch_pos_policy` default is zero and every test figure is a round number chosen to make an arithmetic error visible. **2**: no LHDN or MyInvois detail. **4**: this slice ships **no report** and does not widen `ReportCodeSchema` — Phase 6's two minimal reports (`pos.z_report`, `pos.sales_by_cashier`) are 6D's rows in §4.16.1. **5**: T11, T12 and T13 are all the owner-account pattern with an exact verify command and a stated local equivalent. **6**: OD-11, OD-12 and OD-24 are one isolated task each, each droppable without renumbering a neighbour's interface. **8** and **9**: every 0A helper is cited by its exact name; `fixtures.ts` gains exactly the three §4.17 rows and §4.17.1 gains one registered file. **10**: no per-tenant row is written by a migration — the four permission rows are global, the role grants are `roles.ts`, and a cashier is created through a route because a version-controlled PIN would be LEGACY-028 by another door (T12 Step 2). **11**: no enum label. **13**: `lint-schema.ts` is edited in two tasks (T2's two set memberships, T5's rule) and to exactly §3.5.3's and §3.5.2's stated state. **14**: all six allocated migration numbers are used, none is borrowed, and no step asserts `max(version)`. **15**: root `tests/` and both apps use the production `withTenant`; only `packages/db/test` uses the 4-argument one. **16**: T1 carries **both** branches of the 0064 rule and the verification branch is a fingerprint equality, not a diff. **19**: the terminal acts and the cashier is attributed, asserted four ways in T10's legacy-044 block. **20**: `pos.cashier.compare` runs as the tenant's system principal through `systemContext`, from the worker and never from a request path. **21**: `sql.unsafe` appears in no source tier of this slice; one documented `tx.unsafe` over fourteen literals lives in the test tier (T10 Step 1) and Global Constraint 5 names it. **22**: nothing writes a protected table. **24**: no handler inserts a `document_event`, and this slice does not even call `recordOverride` — 6A's applyBatch step (h) is the one caller on the POS path. **25**: the DELETE list is unchanged and T10 asserts it as an equality. **26**: four codes, all from §3.6.2, none invented, and 6B's and 6D's are not seeded here. **29**: T13 begins with OD-24, states its keyboard shape rather than a visual one, and invents no workflow. **31**: four route modules, four `PROBES` keys, one `JOB_PROBES` key, asserted as a bijection in T10. **32**: this slice writes a literal into **none** of the five shared namespaces. **33**: it seeds no policy and therefore no account — T9 asserts that `POS_TICKET`'s accounts are 6A's and re-seeds nothing. **34**: no document-type seed file is touched. **35**: every task has exact paths, real code, a failing test first and a commit.

**Six things a reviewer should check that this plan cannot prove on its own.** The four that were 6A reconciliations — `applyBatch`'s parameters and its error class, `app.catalogue_revision()`'s `tg_argv[0]` argument, the branch-key nullability of 6A's three tables, and what `applyBatch` does with `ticket.attendance` — **are now answered from 6A's merged file** and are recorded in the "Ten further things" list above, not here. `recordOverride`'s spelling is answered by contracts §4.3.1 (three parameters, `tx, ctx, input`) and by the fact that 6A already calls it, so this slice never does. What remains genuinely open is:

1. **3A's and 3B's stock DDL.** §4.7.3 fixes `inventory_movement`, `stock_on_hand`, `cost_layer` and `cost_consumption` in full, and T10's `seedBranchBWorld` is transcribed from it — but **`stock_valuation` has no DDL in any frozen document**. If `beforeAll` throws there, correct the column list against 3B's merged migration and **never drop the table from `TABLES`**.
2. **3A's `seedWarehouse` option for a branch.** T5's Form A″ and Form C cases and T10's `seedBranchBWorld` all need warehouses at two branches. If the helper has no `branchId` option, **add it additively** (§4.17's closing rule) rather than inserting a warehouse by hand in a test.
3. **`grantCodes`' exact options.** Every test in this slice restricts a principal to a branch through `grantCodes(db, s, { codes, principalId, roleCode, branchIds })` (§4.17, 2B's row). If 2B spelled `branchIds` differently, correct **this** plan's calls; never hand-roll the three inserts (§4.1's pinned paragraph exists because slices kept doing that).
4. **`PosFixture`'s member names.** `staffFixture` reads `pos.s`, `pos.api`, `pos.terminal`, `pos.items["SKU-A"].itemId` / `.baseUnitId`, `pos.taxCodes` and `pos.types`; 6A's Task 10 fixes all of them. A rename in 6A is a one-line correction **in this plan's fixture**, never a second corpus.
5. **6A's `bad`-ticket and self-override cases in `apply.test.ts`.** Both now pass through `verifyTicket` before reaching the code they assert. The *codes* are unchanged; a `detail`-string assertion is not. T9 Step 7 says to change 6A's wording rather than to add a second refusal path that keeps the old string alive.
6. **`sharedDb()`'s and `sharedApi()`'s return shapes** — §4.18 round-1 open item 5, still open for `sharedDb`. T10's regression blocks destructure `{ db, alpha }` and T9/T10's invariant suites destructure `{ api }` from `sharedApi()` (0B's plan writes `{ api; a; b }`). If a use site disagrees with §4.17.1, that open item is what to resolve, and this slice follows whichever spelling the merged file carries. **Neither suite calls `stop()` on the shared instance.**

**Deliberately not in this slice.** No `app.shift`, no `app.till_event`, no Z-report and no till-cash lifecycle — 6D's, with its own migration 0070. **No termination trigger and no "cashiers are employees" deferrable constraint** — both are 8C's (migrations 0093 and 0094, §2.9's card); this slice ships the `revoked_at` column, the tombstone on its transition, and the test that proves revocation reaches a disconnected till, which is what 8C's trigger will then drive. No `employee_sensitive`, no `position`, no `employment_type`, no `archived_at` on `employee` — all 8A's, in migration 0080. No leave register (OD-13, 8D). No loyalty (OD-03, 6B). **No server-side `max_price_override_pct` check** — the till enforces it from its mirror and the server verifies the override's supervisor; a server-side check needs a catalogue price on the wire and that is a §4.10.1 amendment (T9's decision (c)). No POS report of any kind. No `apps/pos`, no SQLite mirror and no PIN *verification* on the server: `verifyPin` exists in `@dulja/db` for 6B to call at the till, and T10 asserts `apps/api` never references it. No second `AuthAdmin` implementation and no new environment variable — `CASHIER_ALIAS_DOMAIN` arrives only with OD-11, and `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` stays out of the container (0C's ADR 0011, not re-opened).

---

## Execution handoff

**Read before starting.** This plan's Assumptions block, all of §3 of the contracts document, **§4.5.6 in full** (this slice implements it), §4.5.3 (`app.principal_has` and the override guard — Task 9 depends on its every clause), §4.6.2, §4.6.3 and §4.6.4 in full, §4.10.1–§4.10.4 (6A's message and apply surface), and §3.5.1's step 7. Then **Task 0 in full**: it is a gate, not a formality, and **Task 1 does not start until `bash scripts/check/contracts-6c-amendments.sh` exits 0**. Its three informational blocks change what Tasks 5, 7 and 9 do — read them rather than skimming past them; the first prints the branch-key declarations that decide Form A versus Form B for three of 6A's tables, the second prints the trigger the snapshot stamp must sort after, and the third prints the seam Tasks 4 and 9 insert themselves into. The two probes that once asked what `applyBatch`'s parameters and per-ticket error class were are now **hard `need_repo` lines** — 6A's merged file answers both, so a guess is no longer admissible.

**Order and independence.** **T0 → T1 → T2 → T3 → T4** are sequential (each migration builds on the last: 0065's FK needs 0064's table, 0067's needs both). **T3 also removes 6A's `extra` scaffolding from `catalogue.ts`**, which is legal only once *both* 0065's and 0066's `*_sync` functions exist — so T2 before T3 is not merely convenient. **T4 additionally needs T2**, because its attendance seam resolves the clock-in's employee through `app.pos_cashier` and its `posFixture` line calls `seedCashier`. **T5 (0068) needs T4** and is the file every later assertion rests on. **T5's PR is wider than its own five files**: Step 7 runs the whole repository suite because 0068 changes what an ungranted `s.human` may read *and* write across seventeen tables, and whatever `grantCodes(db, s)` lines that run requires land in the same PR as the migration that requires them — a migration whose fallout is fixed in a later PR leaves `bun run test` red in between, against Global Constraint 17. Budget for it; it is not a formality. **T6 (0069) needs nothing but T0** and may run in parallel with T1–T5 by a second engineer — but **it must land in the same review batch as T3, T4 and T8**, because `permission-catalogue` fails while a seeded code has no handler reference and this slice seeds four codes across four modules (the F-21 rule; the mitigation is batching, never a suppression). **T7 needs T2, T3 and T6** (the snapshot reads `app.permission` and `branch_pos_policy`) and **edits T4's migration file**, so T4 and T7 land in one PR. **T8 needs T2, T6 and T7.** **T9 needs T5, T7 and T8** and is where test 37 becomes true. **T10 needs T9.** **T11 and T12 are records and block nothing**; **T13 is gated on OD-24 and blocks nothing.**

**One migration per PR (spec:2057).** Six migrations, six PRs: T1 (0064), T2 (0065), T3 (0066), **T4 + T7 (0067 — two commits, one PR, one migration)**, T5 (0068), T6 (0069). Nothing else in the plan adds DDL. Tasks 8–13 may be grouped by whatever review cadence the team runs, but a task is never split across PRs — its failing test and its implementation land together.

**Three ADRs are written as proposals and must be accepted before their implementation merges** (spec:95, all three touching security or the data model): **0060** (T2 — Argon2id in WebAssembly), **0061** (T7 — the snapshot as a projection with one writer), **0062** (T5 — five branch-isolation forms and the one exemption). A still-`proposed` ADR blocks its own task's merge, not the plan.

**Where to stop and raise rather than working around.**

- **A `MISSING (repo):` line from Task 0's gate.** That is a reconciliation with 0B, 2A, 2B, 3A, 3B or **6A**, and the owning plan is the authority for what it exports (§4.18). Correct *this* plan's import path or name; never add a second spelling.
- **`new row violates row-level security policy for table "<table>"` in a fixture or a `beforeAll`, from Task 5 onward.** A `branch_isolation` policy declared with `using` and no `with check` is applied **as** the `with check` on INSERT, and `seedTenant` grants `s.human` no `role_assignment` at all — so an ungranted human can no longer write a branch-keyed row. The fix is one of exactly two (Task 5 Step 7): a **6C-owned** `fixtures.ts` helper inserts as `db.migrator`/`s.system` (amendment A15), and **any other suite** calls `grantCodes(db, s)` with no codes and no `branchIds` in its `beforeAll`. **Never widen the policy, never drop a table from 0068, and never change another slice's helper signature** — a policy a test can talk its way past is LEGACY-045 shipped under a new name. The same cause with a quieter symptom is a read that suddenly returns zero rows and an assertion that expected some; look for it in any suite that was green before 0068.
- **`infinite recursion detected in policy for relation role_assignment_branch`.** Somebody attached `branch_isolation` to that table. Remove it and read ADR 0062: the exemption is the design, not an omission, and the fix is never to rewrite `actor_in_branch` to avoid the table it exists to read.
- **A `TRANSFER_RECEIPT` at branch B failing on every movement after 0068.** Form A″'s in-transit exception is missing or was moved into `app.actor_in_warehouse`, where it is dead code — the function's own `select` is subject to `warehouse`'s policy, so the exception must be **on the warehouse row**. This is 3D's DoD clause failing, and the fix is in 0068 section 3.
- **Every company-wide price list or promotion vanishing for a branch manager.** Form B was written as Form A. `app.actor_in_branch(company_id, null)` is false for every branch-restricted principal, so a NULL branch must be admitted explicitly.
- **`ERR_POS_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` on a ticket that is plainly within the limit.** Read the units first: a percentage is a **6-dp fraction** (`"0.100000"` is ten per cent) and `branch_pos_policy_pct_range` refuses anything above `1`. The second suspect is `Money.multiply`'s rounding mode — the allowance and the till must use the same one (`HALF_AWAY_FROM_ZERO`), and 6B's mirror computation must match `limits.ts` exactly.
- **`ERR_OVERRIDE_NOT_AUTHORISED` on an override you believe is valid.** 2B's `assert_override_authorised` resolves `coalesce(attributed_to, actor_id)` and refuses when that principal is the document's `created_by` **or** its `attributed_to` (§4.5.3). On a POS ticket `attributed_to` is the **cashier**, so a supervisor who is also the cashier is refused by design — that is test 37's second-principal clause, not a bug.
- **A proposal to have sync apply read `pos_cashier.snapshot` "because it is already there".** The snapshot is the till's copy and can lag or be tampered with; spec:1324 says the re-verification is against the **row**, and T9 Step 3's *"a limit is read from branch_pos_policy and NOT from the snapshot"* case widens only the snapshot and asserts the refusal still comes. Never add that read; T10 asserts no server file contains it.
- **A proposal to hash the PIN with a native module.** ADR 0060's decisive argument is Electron at the till (6B), where a native rebuild stands between a cashier and their login. If the argument is to be re-opened, it is re-opened in ADR 0060 and in 6B's ADR 0017 together, not in one file.
- **A proposal to seed cashiers as pack data.** T12 Step 2 is the answer: a cashier has a Supabase auth user and a PIN, and a version-controlled PIN is LEGACY-028 by another door.
- **A proposal to give `attendance_event` a `*_by` column for the employee, or to name `recorded_by` after the cashier.** §3.3's naming rule is mechanical: a `*_by` column is stamped with the *acting* principal, and on the sync path that is the terminal. Attribution is `employee_id`, and calling it anything ending `_by` either fails `stamp_actor` or silently replaces the person (LEGACY-044 in reverse).

**The two commands that must be green after every task.** `bun run lint && bun run lint:rules && bun run format:check && bun run typecheck` and `bun run test`, both at the repository root. `lint:rules` is the one that catches this slice's likeliest mistakes: a `db.` outside `withTenant`, a `number` typed against a name matching `/amount|rate/i`, a permission code with no handler reference (four of them, until T8 lands), and a schema whose new table has a branch key and no policy.

**What Phase 6B, 6D and Phase 8 inherit, so nobody has to re-derive it.**

- **`hashPin` / `verifyPin` are the one PIN function.** 6B imports them into the Electron main process behind the `contextBridge` (§4.10.6) and verifies against the down-synced `catalogue_cashier` mirror. A second implementation gives one PIN two answers, and the wrong one will be the one at the till.
- **`CASHIER_SNAPSHOT_CODES` grows by itself.** 6B's `pos.price.check`, `pos.shift.own` and `pos.attendance.clock` and 6D's `pos.shift.close_any` and `pos.report.read` enter the snapshot the moment their migration seeds them — no edit to 6C, and T7's equality test is what proves the two spellings stayed in step.
- **`verifyTicket` and `OVERRIDE_LIFTS` are 6D's too.** A `till_event` of kind `void` is checked against the **same** `max_void_amount` through the same exported function; a second implementation would let a void be refused at the ticket and admitted on the event stream.
- **`branch_isolation` is attached in a table's own migration from now on** (§3.5.1 step 7). 6D's 0070 attaches Form A to `shift` and `till_event`; 8D's 0096 attaches Form B to `public_holiday`. The `branch_isolation_policy` rule makes forgetting a build failure, which is the whole point of shipping the rule with the policies. **And its fixture half travels with it: a `fixtures.ts` helper whose table carries the policy inserts under `withTenant(db.migrator, s.tenant, s.system, …)`** — amendment A15's clause on §4.17's preamble, which 6D's `seedShift` (A17), 6D's `till_banking_link` work and 8D's `public_holiday` all inherit. A test suite whose actor must stay human calls `grantCodes(db, s)` in `beforeAll` instead; those are the only two shapes, and 6C's Task 5 Step 7 is the worked example.
- **`app.employee` is 8A's to extend, never to re-create.** 0080 adds `position`, `employment_type`, `statutory_profile_note` and `archived_at`; **0064 is byte-frozen** and 8A's own task is a fingerprint assertion. 8C's 0093 adds the termination trigger and 0094 the deferrable "cashiers are employees" constraint — both against the columns this slice shipped.
- **`app.who_can_reset_a_pin` is the pattern for the other one-query question.** Test 36's twin clause, *"who can approve payroll in company X"*, is the same shape over `hr.payroll.approve` with no branch, and 8B should write it as a view for the same reason: an auditor filters and joins a relation, and cannot do either with a function that answers one question.
